## 2026-05-06, REQ-081 Per-Tick Zone Growth (Slice 1 of 2: Unconditional)

- Branch: `feature/20260506-zone-growth`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: First sim-loop feature where the city visibly advances on its own. Without this, zones placed via REQ-080 sit at density 0 forever. New `GROWTH_INTERVAL_TICKS = 20` constant in `src/lib/sim/state.ts` (5 seconds of wall time per density step at the 4Hz default; a fresh zone reaches max density 3 in ~15 seconds; sim-speed multipliers compress wall-clock pace proportionally because every tick is processed identically). `src/lib/sim/events.ts` extended `applyTick` to compute the next tick first, then call new exported pure helper `maybeGrowZones(zones, nextTick)` which returns the bucket unchanged when the tick is not a growth tick OR every cell is already at max density, and a fresh bucket with every density-<3 cell advanced by 1 otherwise. The helper preserves identity-on-no-change so the engine's downstream consumers (the React hook, the snapshot writer) see no spurious state changes when nothing actually grew.
- Verification: `npm run type-check` green. `npm test` 1811/1811 pass (1802 prior + 9 new growth cases). `npm run build` green. `npm run check:dashes` clean. `git diff --check` clean.
- Assumptions: Slice 1 ships unconditional growth (every density-<3 cell advances every Nth tick) because the alternative (waiting for citizens / power / water / services demand modeling to land before any growth happens) would block the visible payoff for a long time. The dev's "find the fun" framing favors the loop being visibly alive sooner; demand-gated growth lands as slice 2 of REQ-081 once those layers exist. The 20-tick interval is a tuning constant; a faster interval would feel arcade-y, slower would feel SimCity 2000-grindy. The mod-tick-equals-zero growth check is the simplest deterministic trigger; future demand-gated growth will check supply per-cell, but the timing keeps the same shape (every Nth tick is the growth pass). Replay determinism is preserved because growth is a function of `(state, event)` only, not wall-clock; two clients replaying the same event log derive the same density progression.
- GDD coverage: REQ-081 added as a new row in `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` with status `partial` (unconditional growth lands; demand-gated growth still missing). `implementationRefs` populated with `src/lib/sim/state.ts` and `src/lib/sim/events.ts`; `testRefs` populated with `tests/lib/sim/events.test.ts`. `docs/gdd/15-zoning-and-business.md` gains a build log entry.
- Followups: none new. Slice 2 of REQ-081 (demand-gated growth) lands when REQ-075 citizens, REQ-085 power, REQ-090 water, REQ-100 services ship.

Origin cell highlight stays for visual orientation. Tool defaults to residential because that is the most-used SimCity zone and the player's first click most likely intends to seed housing. Sim view is paused in the e2e tests before zone clicks to remove tick-timing flake; zoning is independent of speed so the assertions stay deterministic. The palette buttons share the existing speed-button styling pattern so a future slice that adds power / water / service buttons can drop them in without rethinking the layout. ## 2026-05-06, REQ-080 Zoning Slice 1: Schema + placeZone/eraseZone Events + Reducer

- Branch: `feature/20260506-zoning-events`
- PR: #N (when known)

Zones live on `city.sim.zones.cells` per the Q-012 event-sourcing pivot; the legacy `city.buildings` array stays untouched for v1 placeholder buildings (small-house etc.) and a future migration slice can lift those into the zone system when the visual divergence becomes a felt gap. Tightened `src/lib/sim/state.ts` `ZonesBucketSchema` from passthrough to a strict `{ cells: Record<string, ZoneCell> }` shape; new `ZoneKindSchema` (residential / commercial / industrial), `ZoneDensitySchema` (0..3 union per density steps), `ZoneCellSchema` strict `{ kind, density }`, `EMPTY_ZONES_BUCKET = { cells: {} }` frozen at module load, `zoneCellKey(row, col)` returning `"row,col"` matching the existing `streetCellSet` / `buildingCellSet` convention so future zone-vs-piece collision checks compare keys directly. `src/lib/sim/events.ts` adds strict `PlaceZoneEventSchema` carrying `(kind, row, col)` payload and `EraseZoneEventSchema` carrying `(row, col)` payload; both move into the `SimEventSchema` discriminated union; the `PlaceholderLayerEventSchema` enum drops `placeZone` and `eraseZone` since the typed variants now route them. New `applyPlaceZone` and `applyEraseZone` reducer cases in `applySimEvent`. `applyPlaceZone` returns identity when the same kind is painted on an already-zoned cell (no-op on idempotent click), overwrites the kind on retype, and preserves the existing density on retype so the per-tick growth reducer (REQ-081, follow-on slice) does not get reset by an editing player. `applyEraseZone` returns identity when the targeted cell is not zoned. Tests: `tests/lib/sim/state.test.ts` adds 25 cases covering the new zoning schemas; `tests/lib/sim/events.test.ts` adds 14 cases covering placeZone (new cell, identity-on-same-kind, kind-overwrite, density-preserved, no-mutation, multi-cell, negative-coords) plus eraseZone (identity-on-fresh-slug, removes-targeted, only-targeted, identity-on-nonexistent) plus `reduceSimEvents` over the full zoning vocabulary covering replay determinism and two-builder-id convergence; renamed the layer-specific forward-compat block to drop the placeZone test case since it is now strict.

- Assumptions: Zones live on `city.sim.zones.cells` rather than the legacy `city.buildings` array per the Q-012 event-sourcing pivot. Density-preserve-on-retype is the default for slice 1 because it matches the dev's "paint over without resetting growth" intuition; if the playtest reveals players want a clean reset on retype, an explicit `eraseZone` -> `placeZone` two-event sequence covers that case without changing the slice 1 semantics. Identity-on-no-op (same kind on same cell) is the reducer's standard short-circuit shape; the engine still buffers the event so other clients receive it, but local state stays the same. Density 0 means "zoned but ungrown"; the per-tick growth reducer (REQ-081) lands as a follow-on slice and advances density toward 3 as demand allows. The cell-key convention (`"row,col"`) matches the existing helpers exactly so a future "is this cell already a street piece?" check can compare strings directly without recomposing keys.

## 2026-05-05, REQ-070..074 Substrate Slice 5: React Hook + Sim View Scaffold

- Branch: `feature/20260505-sim-engine-hook`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: Final slice of the substrate (REQ-070 flips `partial` to `done`). New `src/lib/sim/useSimEngine.ts` ships the React hook owning the EngineRuntime + setInterval tick loop + autonomous flush trigger + visibilitychange flush + server poll loop + cold-load on mount; thin DOM/effect glue layer over the pure helpers from slice 3, every callback uses runtimeRef so a stale closure does not leak old state into a fired interval. The autonomous flush trigger checks `shouldFlush` every `FLUSH_CHECK_INTERVAL_MS = max(FLUSH_IDLE_MS / 2, 1000)` so the idle threshold fires within at most one half-window after it elapses. Cold load reads snapshot + tail from index 0 then slices past the snapshot's covered range so the snapshot is the start state and only the tail-since-snapshot is folded. Every fetch failure is non-fatal: a transient KV outage does not break the local sim; the next poll or POST retries. New `src/app/[slug]/sim/page.tsx` is the server component reading the builder cookie via `cookies()`; on missing cookie returns `notFound()` rather than redirecting because the redirect-to-self pattern would loop until the middleware response is processed by the browser. New `src/app/[slug]/sim/SimViewClient.tsx` mounts the hook with minimal UI: speed buttons Pause / 1x / 2x / 4x with `aria-pressed` mirror, tick / sim time / pending events / server cursor readout, Drive CTA back to `/<slug>`; the top-down camera (REQ-111), toolbar tabs (REQ-112), demand bars (REQ-113), and per-layer placement tools land in their own slices on top of this scaffold. `src/middleware.ts` REQ-009 mint path now propagates the new cookie to the request via `req.cookies.set` AND sets it on the response via `res.cookies.set`; the request-side propagation is required because the builder cookie is httpOnly and the client cannot read it via document.cookie, so the server component is the only path to surface the id to the SimViewClient prop on first visit. The `NextResponse.next({ request: { headers: req.headers } })` form propagates the mutated request headers (including the new cookie) to the downstream handler.
- Verification: `npm run type-check` green. `npm test` 1767/1767 pass (no new unit cases; the hook is exercised by e2e). `npm run build` produces a green production bundle and the new `ƒ /[slug]/sim` route at 3.29 kB / 122 kB. `npm run check:dashes` clean. `git diff --check` clean. `npx playwright test e2e/sim.spec.ts --project=chromium` passes 3/3 locally (page mount + speed buttons + Drive CTA + readout block, Pause click stops tick advance, 1x speed advances tick counter ~6 times in 1500ms with forgiving lower bound for slow CI). Pre-existing `e2e/home.spec.ts:12` flake on local also fires on main and is unrelated to this slice (the local playwright run hits the real KV which has cities from prior sessions; CI passes because the CI playwright webServer runs without KV).
- Assumptions: The hook is not unit-tested because vitest is `node` env without React Testing Library; adding the dep would expand slice scope. The pure logic is fully tested in slice 3, and the hook is thin glue exercised by the e2e spec. The middleware change to propagate cookies on the request is safe because the request mutation only affects subsequent processing on the same request (the page handler) and does not alter the response shape; the response cookie is set identically to the prior behavior. The `notFound()` fallback in the sim page handler fires only when middleware fails to mint the cookie (should never happen in practice); a refresh after the response carries the cookie always succeeds. The visibilitychange flush uses `fetch` rather than `navigator.sendBeacon` for v1 simplicity; on a true tab close the in-flight fetch may not complete, but the periodic flush + autonomous trigger cover the long-tail case. SSE / WebSocket server-push is deferred per Q-012 dev override (poll-on-save is enough for v1).
- GDD coverage: REQ-070 flips `partial` to `done`. `implementationRefs` extended with `src/lib/sim/useSimEngine.ts`, `src/app/[slug]/sim/page.tsx`, `src/app/[slug]/sim/SimViewClient.tsx`, `src/middleware.ts`. `testRefs` extended with `tests/app/middleware.test.ts`, `e2e/sim.spec.ts`. `docs/gdd/13-sim-engine.md` Status flips to `done` and gains a final build log entry.
- Followups: none new. The substrate is complete. Next dot pickup: any of the layer slices (REQ-075 citizens, REQ-080 zoning, REQ-085 power) which can now plug into the substrate directly.

## 2026-05-05, REQ-070..074 Substrate Slice 4: Server-Side Snapshot Writer (Q-013)

- Branch: `feature/20260505-sim-snapshot-writer`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: New `src/lib/sim/snapshot.ts` ships the pure snapshot helpers. `SNAPSHOT_EVERY_N_EVENTS = 1000` matches Q-013 default A. `shouldSnapshot(currentLength, snapshotCursor)` returns true when the unsnapshotted-event count crosses the threshold; defensive against KV-returned NaN cursors and negative inputs. `buildSnapshot(previous, tailEvents)` folds the tail on top of the previous snapshot (or `EMPTY_SIM_STATE` when no prior snapshot) via the same `reduceSimEvents` pure reducer. `parseEventStrings(raws)` JSON-parses + schema-validates raw KV strings, dropping malformed entries silently per slice 2's read-path convention. The events route's POST handler at `src/app/api/city/[slug]/events/route.ts` gained an inline snapshot trigger that runs after a successful RPUSH: reads the current snapshot cursor (defensive `Number.parseInt`), calls `shouldSnapshot`, and on yes reads the unsnapshotted tail via `lrange(snapshotCursor, nextCursor-1)`, parses via `parseEventStrings`, parses the previous snapshot via `SimStateSchema.safeParse`, builds the new snapshot via `buildSnapshot`, writes both `citySnapshot(slug)` and `cityEventsSnapshotCursor(slug)`. The snapshot block is wrapped in try/catch so a snapshot-write failure does not fail the POST (the events are already persisted); the next POST will retry. Snapshot writes are idempotent per cursor (writing the same snapshot twice produces the same state). The 30-minute-sim-time half of Q-013's OR is deferred to a follow-on per the snapshot module comment: the time bound matters only in a "running-but-quiet" scenario which v1 rarely hits, and computing simTimeMs requires reducing the tail anyway, so the count threshold is the cheaper trigger. Event pruning is intentionally not done: cursor advancement on the client is index-based and pruning would invalidate live cursors; v1.1 can add archival to a separate key when storage pressure is observed.
- Verification: `npm run type-check` green. `npm test` 1767/1767 pass (1742 prior + 20 new snapshot helper cases + 5 new snapshot trigger cases in the events route test file). `npm run build` green. `npm run check:dashes` clean. `git diff --check` clean.
- Assumptions: Snapshot trigger runs inline in the POST handler rather than via a separate Vercel Function. The trigger fires once per POST that crosses the threshold, doing one extra `lrange` + `get` + 2 `set`s of overhead; this matches the FakeKv-driven test expectations and keeps the v1 implementation simple. Cron-triggered snapshotting is a v1.1 alternative if the inline overhead becomes a performance issue. The `parseEventStrings` helper drops bad entries silently rather than failing the snapshot write because a single bad event in the log would otherwise permanently block snapshotting, and replaying a bad event is what produced the divergent-state risk in the first place. The `SimStateSchema.safeParse` of the previous snapshot guards against a malformed prior snapshot poisoning the new one (e.g. if the schema tightens between snapshot writes); on parse failure, the snapshot rebuilds from EMPTY_SIM_STATE plus the full event log up to nextCursor, not just the tail. The snapshot does not record the `clientReceivedAt` of the last event it incorporates, only the cursor; clients can derive the event's timestamp from a follow-on GET if needed.
- GDD coverage: REQ-070 stays `partial` (slice 5 still outstanding). `implementationRefs` extended with `src/lib/sim/snapshot.ts`; `testRefs` extended with `tests/lib/sim/snapshot.test.ts`. `docs/gdd/13-sim-engine.md` gains a build log entry.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-05, REQ-070..074 Substrate Slice 3: Client-Side Engine Helpers (Pure)

- Branch: `feature/20260505-sim-engine-helpers`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: New `src/lib/sim/engine.ts` ships the pure runtime helpers that drive the client-side sim loop without touching the DOM or timers. Exports: `TICK_INTERVAL_MS_BASE = 250` (4Hz at 1x speed), `FLUSH_IDLE_MS = 5000` (matches dev's 2026-05-05 broadcast-rate answer), `FLUSH_BATCH_MAX = 256` (matches server route's `MAX_EVENTS_PER_BATCH` so a flush always fits in one POST), `EngineRuntime` type with `state` / `pendingEvents` / `serverCursor` / `lastEnqueueAtMs`, `EMPTY_ENGINE_RUNTIME` frozen at module load, `tickIntervalMs(speed)` returning 250 / 125 / 62.5 ms for speeds 1 / 2 / 4 and null when paused, `buildTickEvent(runtime, builderId, nowMs)` building a TickEvent matching the runtime's current speed (null when paused), `enqueueEvent(runtime, event, nowMs)` pure helper that advances local state via `applySimEvent` and appends to the pending buffer (still buffers paused-tick events so other clients receive them), `shouldFlush(runtime, nowMs)` returning true when the buffer is at the batch cap or the idle threshold has elapsed, `commitFlush(runtime, newServerCursor)` clearing the buffer and advancing the cursor with idempotent + cursor-only-advances guards, `foldServerEvents(runtime, serverEvents, newCursor)` applying server tail events to local state via the same pure reducer, `loadFromColdResponse(snapshot, tailEvents, cursor)` for the cold-load path. The DOM and timer wiring (setInterval, visibilitychange, fetch) lives in the React hook that lands in slice 5 (added a 5th slice to the substrate split because the React hook is its own concern); this slice ships only the pure logic so tests can exercise tick scheduling, event buffering, and flush-decision math without mocking timers or the DOM.
- Verification: `npm run type-check` green. `npm test` 1742/1742 pass (1696 prior + 46 new engine cases). `npm run build` green (no new bundle routes; pure module). `npm run check:dashes` clean. `git diff --check` clean.
- Assumptions: Splitting the substrate into 5 slices instead of 4 because the React hook is materially separate from the pure engine logic and bundling them would force the slice 3 PR to mock DOM events to test buffer behavior. The pure engine slice ships first; the React hook + DOM wiring slice ships after. The `enqueueEvent` helper buffers an event even when the local reducer returns identity (e.g. paused-tick) because other clients on the same slug may have a different speed and the event matters there; the buffer-IS-the-server-log discipline keeps every client's view of what was sent consistent. The `shouldFlush` helper does NOT trigger on the explicit save action (the React hook handles that branch directly by calling `commitFlush` after a successful POST); the helper covers the autonomous triggers (idle and batch-cap) only. The `commitFlush` cursor-only-advances guard rejects a server-returned cursor below the current cursor as a server-bug guard rather than rolling back state; in practice this never fires (the events route uses RPUSH and returns the new length, which only goes up). The `loadFromColdResponse` helper trusts the caller to validate the snapshot via `SimStateSchema`; the React hook in slice 5 will do that validation before calling the helper.
- GDD coverage: REQ-070 stays `partial` (slices 4 + 5 still outstanding). `implementationRefs` extended with `src/lib/sim/engine.ts`; `testRefs` extended with `tests/lib/sim/engine.test.ts`. `docs/gdd/13-sim-engine.md` gains a build log entry.
- Followups: a 5th implement: dot will be filed for the React hook + DOM wiring slice (slice 5 of 5 in the substrate split). Original REQ-070 dot stays open until the full substrate ships.

## 2026-05-05, REQ-070..074 Substrate Slice 2: KV Event Log Persistence + Events Route

- Branch: `feature/20260505-sim-events-route`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: Server-side persistence for the sim event log (Q-012 event sourcing). Extended `src/lib/kv.ts` with three new keys: `cityEvents(slug)` for the append-only event log stored as a Redis list (insertion order is canonical replay order), `citySnapshot(slug)` for the latest derived state snapshot (slice 4 writes; slice 2 reads), `cityEventsSnapshotCursor(slug)` for the integer cursor recording how many events the snapshot incorporates. New `src/app/api/city/[slug]/events/route.ts` ships POST + GET handlers (runtime nodejs). POST validates cookie shape via `isValidBuilderId` per REQ-009, validates body via `PostBodySchema` (z.array(SimEventSchema).min(1).max(256) per `MAX_EVENTS_PER_BATCH` to bound payload size), stamps `clientReceivedAt = Date.now()` server-side on every event so untrusted client clocks cannot bid for ordering primacy by being late or by spoofing past timestamps, RPUSHes JSON-encoded events to the list keyed by insertion order, returns `{ slug, appended, nextCursor }` where nextCursor = the new list length. GET takes optional `?cursor=N` (defaults to 0; rejects non-finite or negative), reads up to `MAX_EVENTS_PER_GET = 2048` events per call, returns `{ events, nextCursor, totalLen, snapshot, snapshotCursor }`. The GET path drops malformed events silently (`SimEventSchema.safeParse` failure or invalid JSON) so a bad write does not 500 the reader; a future debug route can surface them when needed. KV-unconfigured fallback returns empty payloads to keep the playwright e2e webServer running without KV (mirrors `loadCity`'s REQ-015 pattern). `tests/_fakeKv.ts` gained `rpush(key, ...values)` and `llen(key)` mirroring the Upstash Redis surface. The new `rpush` semantically differs from the existing `lpush` (prepend) because the events route needs append-order for canonical replay; both methods coexist for callers that want one or the other.
- Verification: `npm run type-check` green. `npm test` 1696/1696 pass (1669 prior + 27 new event-route cases). `npm run build` produces a green production bundle and the new `ƒ /api/city/[slug]/events` route at 128 B / 102 kB First Load JS. `npm run check:dashes` clean. `git diff --check` clean.
- Assumptions: Storage is a Redis list (RPUSH / LRANGE / LLEN) rather than a sorted set (ZADD / ZRANGEBYSCORE) because (a) insertion order is the canonical replay order anyway, (b) cursor-as-integer-index is simpler than cursor-as-millisecond-timestamp, and (c) the existing FakeKv supports list operations with a small additional rpush + llen surface, while sorted-set + zrangebyscore would have required a larger fake extension. The list approach preserves the property that two concurrent POSTs from different builders both append in the order Redis processed the RPUSH commands; under network race conditions the merge order is whatever Redis decided, which matches the Q-012 event-sourcing contract (server-side ordering is canonical, clients reconcile against it). The 256-event-per-batch cap is a defensive bound rather than a hard performance ceiling; a client batching more than 256 events between flushes (uncommon in practice, since the slice-3 scheduler flushes on idle 5s + visibilitychange + explicit save) makes two POSTs instead of one. The 2048-event-per-GET cap supports a cold-load tail read beyond the most recent snapshot without unbounded payload growth; a client behind by more than 2048 events makes another GET with the new cursor (also uncommon since slice 4 will snapshot every 1000 events). Snapshotting itself is deferred to slice 4; this slice's GET reads the snapshot when present (so cold load uses it from day one) and falls back to `null` + cursor 0 when absent. The `clientReceivedAt` server stamp is `Date.now()` (single timestamp per POST batch, not per-event), which is fine because the per-batch ordering is preserved in the list and the per-event ordering within a batch matches the order the client sent; multiple consecutive POSTs from the same client get monotonically advancing batch timestamps (Date.now() always advances).
- GDD coverage: REQ-070 stays `partial` (slices 3 + 4 still outstanding). `implementationRefs` extended with `src/lib/kv.ts` and `src/app/api/city/[slug]/events/route.ts`; `testRefs` extended with `tests/app/api.cityEvents.test.ts`. `docs/gdd/13-sim-engine.md` gains a build log entry.
- Followups: none new.

Ships the type vocabulary and pure reducer that every subsequent layer slice (REQ-075 citizens, REQ-080 zoning, REQ-085 power, etc.) will plug into. New `src/lib/sim/events.ts` exports `TickEventSchema` / `SetSpeedEventSchema` / `SetTaxRateEventSchema` plus a `PlaceholderLayerEventSchema` reserving the layer-specific event vocabulary for forward-compat (`placeZone`, `eraseZone`, `runPowerLine`, `eraseLine`, `placePowerPlant`, `placeWaterSource`, `placeServiceBuilding`, `spawnDisaster`), the `SimEvent` discriminated union, `applySimEvent(state, event)` pure reducer that dispatches by event type and falls through to identity for events whose layer slice has not landed, and `reduceSimEvents(events, initial)` convenience helper. The strict validation is available to consumers that import `SimStateSchema` from `src/lib/sim/state.ts`. The placeholder layer-event reducer fall-through is deterministic across clients since the dispatch is local code, not config; every client running the same build dispatches identically. EMPTY_SIM_STATE is frozen at module load via Object.freeze on the top-level object plus per-bucket Object.freeze on the empty objects to guard against accidental in-place layer writes. Slice scope intentionally excludes the persistence layer (slice 2: KV keys + /api/city/[slug]/events route), the client-side scheduler (slice 3: simEngine.ts with the 4Hz tick + flush trigger), and the snapshotting trigger (slice 4: server-side function on event-count crossing). ## 2026-05-05, Q-010 / Q-012 Resolved: Event-Sourced Sim with Concurrent Reconciliation

- Branch: `research/20260505-fun-factor-and-asset-bumps`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: Q-010 resolved toward A modified (client-side sim authority with server-side event log). Q-012 resolved toward D (event sourcing for concurrent slug editors). Two new open questions filed with Recommended defaults: Q-013 (snapshotting strategy: every 1000 events or 30 minutes of sim time) and Q-014 (sim ticks are themselves events for deterministic replay). Q-011 (sim speed maximum) filed with the Pause / 1x / 2x / 4x recommended default. The REQ-070 substrate dot was rewritten to absorb REQ-070 through REQ-074 into a single foundation slice (event log + reducer + scheduler + snapshot + reconcile) because shipping piecemeal would force every sim layer slice to redefine the substrate. The OOS multiplayer fence in `docs/gdd/99-out-of-scope.md` partially lifts: concurrent state edits via event reconciliation move IN; presence, cursors, avatars in drive mode, and voice / text chat stay OUT. The `docs/gdd/13-sim-engine.md` substrate spec was rewritten to describe REQ-072 (event log + command vocabulary), REQ-073 (persisted event log + snapshots), and REQ-074 (concurrent edit reconciliation) explicitly; the previous REQ-072 (SimState schema only) and REQ-073 (delta persistence) framings are replaced.

## 2026-05-05, E2E Default PUT Interceptor (Stop Test KV Pollution): F-010

- Branch: `feature/20260505-e2e-default-put-intercept`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed a real seam surfaced during the open-edit pivot investigation. The Playwright editor specs that goto `/<slug>/edit` and place pieces issue an autosave PUT (REQ-025); specs that did not register their own `page.route('**/api/city/**', ...)` were letting that PUT travel to whatever KV the playwright webServer pointed at, polluting the global `city:index` and the per-slug version history with synthetic test data. The most visible artifact: the slug `rejection-flash-spec` ended up in production's `city:index` from a live CI run and surfaced on the home page's recently-updated cards alongside real cities, which is the same chain that produced the "Save failed" banner that triggered the open-edit pivot (Q-008). New `test.beforeEach` at the top of `e2e/editor.spec.ts` registers a default `**/api/city/**` interceptor that fulfills any PUT with a synthetic 200 + zero-hash payload + `updatedAt: 0`; non-PUT methods (the GET read path and the `?v=<hash>` variant) continue through to the test webServer's empty-city soft-fallback so the read path stays exercised. Per playwright's "later handler matches first" contract, tests that already register their own `page.route` inside the test body still take precedence; the beforeEach is the catch-all for tests that did not. The synthetic versionHash uses 64 zeros so it parses through `parseCityVersionHash` cleanly if any client code reads the response payload.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npm run type-check` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1605/1605 pass (unchanged; this slice is e2e-only). `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/editor.spec.ts` passed 28/28 locally including the rejection-flash-spec (which previously had no PUT interceptor and now relies on the default).
- Assumptions: A `test.beforeEach` in the editor spec file (vs a project-level fixture in `playwright.config.ts`) keeps the change scoped to the file that needs it; the home / drive / smoke specs do not goto `/edit` so no autosave fires from those files. The synthetic 200 payload (vs a 503 mock) lets the editor's autosave status indicator settle into `saved` rather than `error` so a test that did not opt into either explicit branch sees the production-default success path. The zero-hash payload (vs a freshly-computed `hashCity(EMPTY_CITY)`) stays decoupled from any future schema or hash change because the test does not assert on the hash value, only on the editor's response to a successful PUT. Per slice discipline, no e2e test bodies were rewritten to remove their own `page.route` (those are explicit per-test contracts that exercise specific 200 / 503 / abort paths and stay in place); no playwright config changes; no source / runtime changes; no test data cleanup against the live KV (that is a destructive shared-store action under AGENTS.md Rule 6 and stays an explicit ask).
- GDD coverage: F-010 closes against this slice. No GDD section file gains a build log entry (this slice is test plumbing; REQ-025 still applies to the autosave PUT path, which is unchanged). REQ-014's behavior is unchanged.
- Followups: F-010 added and resolved in the same PR.

## 2026-05-05, Open-Edit Pivot (Drop Per-Slug Owner Gate): REQ-014, REQ-009

- Branch: `feature/20260505-open-edit-no-owner-gate`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: VibeCity is now open-edit. Any visitor with a valid `vibecity.builderId` cookie can save to any slug. Removed the per-slug owner gate from the PUT route at `src/app/api/city/[slug]/route.ts`: the route no longer reads `city:${slug}:owner`, no longer returns 403 on a cookie mismatch, and no longer writes the owner key on the first save. The route still validates the cookie shape (REQ-009) so a request without a valid builder id continues to return 401, but the cookie is now identity-only (used for activity attribution and future "cities I made" surfaces) rather than a write gate. Removed the `kvKeys.cityOwner` namespace entry from `src/lib/kv.ts`; the literal `city:${slug}:owner` key is no longer read or written by VibeCity, and the orphan keys produced by prior PUTs stay in KV unused (a future cleanup slice can ZSCAN them out if it becomes worth doing). Tests: `tests/app/cityRoute.test.ts` renames the "saves a valid city, claims ownership, and returns the canonical hash" case to "saves a valid city and returns the canonical hash" with the owner-key assertion dropped; the "rejects a write from a different builder once a slug is owned" case flips to "lets any builder overwrite an existing city (open-edit)" asserting builder B's PUT returns 200 and `city:${slug}:latest` advances to B's hash; the "lets the owner overwrite their own city" case is renamed to "lets the same builder overwrite their own city" with the owner-key assertion dropped; the `clearSlug` helper and the per-test `fake.del(..., kvKeys.cityOwner(slug))` calls drop the removed key. `tests/lib/kv.test.ts` removes the "cityOwner is per-slug and namespaced under city:" case and drops `cityOwner` from the "every key starts with the city: prefix" invariant. `tests/app/middleware.test.ts` updates its file header to describe the cookie as identity-only, not a per-cookie write gate. GDD: `docs/gdd/03-persistence.md` REQ-014 key-namespace table drops the `city:${slug}:owner` row, the write-path step list collapses the owner check and the first-claim write into a single "validate cookie shape, do not gate writes per cookie" step, and a new build log entry records the pivot. `docs/gdd/05-identity.md` REQ-009 status text rewrites "Identity is a per-browser anonymous id used to gate who can overwrite a city's `:latest` snapshot" to "Identity is a per-browser anonymous id used to attribute saves to a builder for activity tracking..."; the "What it does" / "What it does NOT do" sections drop the per-cookie write-gate language and clarify that losing the cookie costs activity history but not edit access; a build log entry is appended. `docs/gdd/99-out-of-scope.md` Auth and identity fence rewrites the "Slug ownership transfer" line to a "Slug ownership" line that names the open-edit tenet directly. `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` adds Q-008 documenting the pivot from owner-gated to open-edit with the "rejection-flash-spec" CI-leak failure mode as the trigger.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npm run type-check` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1605/1605 pass (1606 prior minus the one removed `cityOwner is per-slug` invariant case; the renamed cityRoute cases reuse the same test slots so the net delta is exactly minus one). `npm run build` produced a green production build with the API route bundle slightly smaller (one removed `kv.get` plus one removed conditional `kv.set`). E2E left unchanged: the `e2e/editor.spec.ts:1483` rejection-flash spec no longer needs a workaround because the open-edit pivot makes the test-leaked slug freely writable by any visitor.
- Assumptions: The cookie stays required (a request without a valid builder id still gets 401) rather than dropped entirely because the middleware mints the cookie on every first visit, so requiring it costs the user nothing and creates a small friction layer against unauthenticated bots. Q-008 captures the alternative ("drop the cookie requirement entirely") and the rationale for keeping the 401 floor. Orphan `city:${slug}:owner` keys are left in KV rather than swept because (a) KV reads of removed keys cost nothing in code paths that no longer reference them, and (b) a sweep is a destructive shared-store action that AGENTS.md Rule 6 requires user approval for; if the orphans become storage-budget pressure, a follow-up slice can ZSCAN them out under explicit dev approval. The pivot is one consistent rule across every slug (any visitor can edit) rather than a per-slug "lock" toggle because Rule 7 prefers the simplest consistent flow; if a moderation surface ever ships, it will be a separate slice opt-in rather than the default. Per slice discipline, no editor UI changes (the autosave path's "Save failed" banner already handles the residual error states like 503), no cleanup of orphan owner keys in production KV, no e2e test changes (rejection-flash-spec no longer needs intercept), and no rate-limiting layer all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-level removal of the per-cookie write gate.
- GDD coverage: REQ-014 stays `done` (the route still validates schema, cookie shape, persists the version graph, and bumps the index; the only behavior change is dropping the per-cookie owner gate, which was always a v1-internal policy choice rather than a separate requirement). Its `implementationRefs` already list `src/app/api/city/[slug]/route.ts` and `src/lib/kv.ts`; the `cityOwner` line in the kv.ts file is gone. Its `testRefs` already list `tests/app/cityRoute.test.ts` and `tests/lib/kv.test.ts`. REQ-009 stays `partial` (cookie minting and validation already shipped; the spec rewrite clarifies the cookie's role as identity-only without changing its implementation surface). `docs/gdd/03-persistence.md`, `docs/gdd/05-identity.md`, and `docs/gdd/99-out-of-scope.md` each gain a build log entry.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Drive HUD Compass Cardinal Heading: REQ-066

- Branch: `feature/20260504-233031-drive-hud-compass`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed an orientation cue gap on the drive surface by adding a compass cardinal heading readout to the drive HUD so a player driving across a long city can read at a glance which way the car is pointing without forcing them to count chase-camera rotations or read radian values. New pure helper exports in `src/app/[slug]/driveHud.ts`: `CompassDirection` union over the eight cardinals plus corners (`N | NE | E | SE | S | SW | W | NW`), `COMPASS_DIRECTIONS` ordered array starting at north and walking clockwise so the index into the array equals the bin index used by the resolver and matches the `Dir` taxonomy from `src/lib/connectors.ts` (0 = N, 1 = NE, ..., 7 = NW), pure `headingToCompass(heading)` that normalizes the integrator's heading radians into `[0, 2pi)`, shifts by `pi/8` so the `N` bin straddles zero, floors into eight 45deg bins, and indexes `COMPASS_DIRECTIONS`; non-finite headings collapse to `N` so a tuning bug cannot flip the HUD into an undefined state; `HUD_COMPASS_LABEL` map emits the canonical one- or two-character compass abbreviations so the cue stays compact and every direction gets a non-empty label because the compass is always informational rather than a conditional alert. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` imports `headingToCompass` and `HUD_COMPASS_LABEL` alongside the existing direction imports, adds `hudCompassRef` span ref, the existing `updateHud()` helper writes the compass label into the ref's textContent and `data-hud-compass` onto the scene root via imperative `setAttribute` so the React tree does not re-render every frame; the per-frame mirror runs from the same three call sites that already drive the speed readout (post-mount sync, respawn handler, per-frame integration tick); JSX root gains `data-hud-compass="N"` default so the empty grid still emits the contract attribute; visible `<span data-testid="drive-hud-compass">N</span>` mounts on the right side of the surface row sharing the same `minHeight: 14` baseline so the surface label and compass cue read as one row that flexes between the warm amber penalty signal on the left and the cool sage `#a3c8a3` cardinal heading on the right. Tests: `tests/app/driveHud.test.ts` adds 29 cases under three describe blocks: `COMPASS_DIRECTIONS (REQ-066)` (3: exactly eight directions, starts at N walks clockwise, every union member exactly once), `headingToCompass (REQ-066)` (21: N at 0, E at +pi/2, S at pi, W at -pi/2, NE / SE / SW / NW at the 45deg bin centers, slight inside +/- pi/8 boundary stays N, just past +pi/8 flips to NE, just below -pi/8 flips to NW, just under +2pi wraps back to N, +2pi exactly wraps to N, negative wrap at -2pi reads as N, +pi/2 + 2pi wrap invariant reads as E, -pi/2 - 2pi negative wrap reads as W, NaN / Infinity defensive fallback to N, full-sweep coverage produces every direction, deterministic on input, every finite heading lands in the union, clockwise walk through the eight bin centers matches `COMPASS_DIRECTIONS` order), `HUD_COMPASS_LABEL (REQ-066)` (5: non-empty trimmed for every direction, canonical compass abbreviations, eight pairwise distinct labels, covers every union member, distinct from the surface / speed-direction / city-validity vocabulary).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npm run type-check` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1606/1606 pass (1577 prior + 29 new compass cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 145 kB unchanged from the prior slice's rounding.
- Assumptions: The eight-bin compass (vs four cardinals only or 16 bins) matches the `Dir` taxonomy already used by the connector graph so a future compass-based piece-snap layer or pace-notes module can speak the same vocabulary without an extra resolver. The compass cue is always visible (vs only when the speed bar is non-zero or only when the player is on-street) because the cardinal heading is always informational; the player wants to know which way they are pointing on a long drive, so an empty default would defeat the purpose. The cool sage `#a3c8a3` color is distinct from the warm amber `#f5b94a` of the surface label, the cool blue `#9bb6e0` of the city-validity label, and the warm gold `#e0c878` of the direction (forward / reverse) label so the four HUD label channels read as visually independent. Non-finite heading collapsing to `N` (vs throwing or rendering a placeholder) is the safer default because a malformed heading is a tuning bug, not a user-facing state, so painting a stable `N` is better than leaking an undefined readout. Per slice discipline, a graphical compass rose with a needle, a heading-aware route-back-to-spawn cue, an integration with a future pace-notes module, and a "you are facing toward unmatched ports" warning all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-to-HUD cardinal heading cue.
- GDD coverage: REQ-066 stays `done`; its `implementationRefs` and `testRefs` already list the touched files. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` build log entry added recording the resolver, the label map, the data attributes, the visible span, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Home Page Total Cities Count Cue: REQ-011, REQ-050

- Branch: `feature/20260504-231812-home-total-count`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed a substrate-level activity-readout gap on the home page (Pillar 2: "Your city, your URL.") by surfacing a "N cities so far" header cue below the tagline so a visitor reads at a glance how active the global city index is, not just the newest twelve in the recently-updated list. Until this slice the home page (REQ-050) listed a window of the `city:index` sorted set (REQ-011) but discarded the total cardinality of the set; a visitor scanning the recently-updated list saw twelve cards and could not tell whether that was the entire global feed or the tip of a much larger iceberg. New pure helper `src/lib/cityCount.ts` exports `formatCityCount(count)` which collapses a non-negative integer into the user-facing label across a band table with proper singular / plural agreement: 0 reads as "0 cities so far" (English convention treats zero as plural), 1 reads as "1 city so far" (singular noun), 2+ reads as "N cities so far" (plural noun). `CITY_COUNT_SUFFIX = 'so far'`, `CITY_COUNT_NOUN_SINGULAR = 'city'`, and `CITY_COUNT_NOUN_PLURAL = 'cities'` land as exported constants so tests can pin the literal vocabulary without parsing the formatter output and a future copy update lands in one place. Defensive: a non-finite `count` (NaN, +Infinity, -Infinity) collapses to the empty string mirroring `formatRelativeTime`'s defensive contract; a negative count collapses to the empty string (a negative count is not a real state and silently dropping the cue is safer than painting `-3 cities so far`); a fractional count floors so a future caller that passes a non-integer cannot leak `1.5 cities so far`. The home page treats the empty string as "no readout" and renders no cue at all rather than a stray label. `src/lib/recentSlugs.ts` gains a sibling `cityIndexCount()` reader that mirrors the existing `recentSlugs` / `recentCities` defensive contract: KV-unconfigured fallback returns 0 (so the local-dev experience without env vars is "0 cities so far" rather than a thrown error and matches the empty-state of the recently-updated list), a non-finite or negative `ZCARD` return collapses to 0 with a warning so a tuning bug or an older Upstash client returning a string cannot leak `NaN cities so far` onto the page, and a fractional ZCARD return floors. The reader calls `kv.zcard(kvKeys.cityIndex())` exactly once per call so the home page pays a single round-trip for the total-count read. `src/app/page.tsx` reads the recently-updated list and the total count in parallel via `Promise.all([recentCities(), cityIndexCount()])` so both KV reads complete in one network round-trip rather than serializing on the slower of the two; the header gains a conditional `<p data-testid="home-total-count" data-total-count={totalCount}>{totalCountLabel}</p>` rendered below the existing tagline at `fontSize: 14, opacity: 0.6, letterSpacing: 0.3` so the visitor's eye reads the brand first (56 px), the tagline second (18 px), and the activity cue third (14 px). The `data-total-count={totalCount}` attribute exposes the raw count on the element so a future test can pin the boundary without parsing the visible cue. Tests `tests/lib/cityCount.test.ts` ship 28 cases under six describe blocks: `constants` (5 cases: suffix literal, singular noun literal, plural noun literal, non-empty trimmed across all three, singular and plural differ), `zero band` (3 cases: literal "0 cities so far", contains the suffix, contains the plural noun with the count + space + noun shape so the prefix-of-plural ambiguity is disambiguated), `singular band` (3 cases: literal "1 city so far", contains the suffix, uses the singular noun with the count + space + noun shape so it cannot accidentally match a "1 cities" output), `plural band` (4 cases: 2, 7, 247, 1_000_000 all use the plural noun), `fractional inputs floor` (3 cases: 1.9 floors to "1 city", 2.5 floors to "2 cities", 0.5 floors to "0 cities"), `defensive fallbacks` (5 cases: NaN, +Infinity, -Infinity, -1, -0.5 all collapse to the empty string), and `output shape contracts` (5 cases: every non-empty output ends with the suffix, every non-empty output starts with a digit, zero and singular outputs differ, singular and plural outputs differ, formatter is deterministic). `tests/lib/recentSlugs.test.ts` adds 10 new `cityIndexCount (REQ-011, REQ-050)` cases mirroring the existing `recentSlugs` / `recentCities` test surface: KV unconfigured returns 0, empty index returns 0, ZADD count of three reads as 3, single-entry index reads as 1, ZADD upsert semantics so two writes for the same slug read as 1, `zcard` is called exactly once against `kvKeys.cityIndex()`, NaN return collapses to 0 with a warn, negative return collapses to 0 with a warn, fractional return floors, namespace key invariant. `e2e/home.spec.ts` extends the existing empty-state spec (the one that runs with KV unconfigured on CI; the same spec is the documented pre-existing flake when run locally with `.env.local` materialized so the live store leaks into the test) with a four-line block asserting the total-count cue is visible, carries `data-total-count='0'`, and reads the literal "0 cities so far" so the empty-state branch of the substrate is locked down end-to-end.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1577/1577 pass (1539 prior + 28 new cityCount cases + 10 new cityIndexCount cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/` route-specific JS at 1.41 kB unchanged (the new helper imports and the conditional `<p>` render path do not push the bundle past the prior slice's 1.41 kB rounding). `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/home.spec.ts` passed 2/3 locally; the third spec reproduced the same pre-existing flake the prior progress log entry documented (the `.env.local` materialized live store populates `city:index` so the empty-state asserts `data-recent-count='0'` but reads the live count instead, and the new `data-total-count='0'` assertion shares the same fate). Verified the new assertion passes when KV is unconfigured (the CI scenario) by running `KV_REST_API_URL='' KV_REST_API_TOKEN='' npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/home.spec.ts -g 'renders the heading'` (1/1 pass); CI does not exhibit the flake because the workflow runs without `.env.local` and both `recentCities()` and `cityIndexCount()` fall back to the empty / zero state.
- Assumptions: The total-count cue uses the conversational "N cities so far" shape rather than a label-style "Total: N" because the visitor's eye reads the cue at a glance to understand how active the city builder is across all cities; "so far" reads as a forward-looking invitation rather than a static counter and matches the build / drive loop vocabulary the GDD names ("Build it. Drive it. Build more.") where "more" implies the count is still growing. The cue sits below the tagline (vs a corner badge or a sidebar counter) so the brand / tagline / activity cue stack reads as one header unit at the top of the page; a corner counter would compete with the recently-updated section heading and a sidebar counter would not work on a centered single-column layout. The font size 14 (vs 12 like the recently-updated cards or 18 like the tagline) sits between the two so the visitor's eye reads it as a tertiary cue without overpowering the tagline. Singular / plural agreement uses two nouns ("city" / "cities") rather than a parenthetical "(s)" suffix because the parenthetical is awkward in screen-reader output and breaks the natural English rhythm; English convention treats zero as plural ("0 cities", not "0 city") so the band table only splits at the singular boundary. Non-finite or negative count collapsing to the empty string (vs a placeholder like "0 cities so far" or "Total unavailable") lets the home page render no `<p>` element at all rather than a stray cue; this is the safer default because a malformed count is a tuning bug, not a user-facing state, so silently dropping the cue is better than painting a confusing label. `cityIndexCount` lives in `src/lib/recentSlugs.ts` rather than its own module because it shares the same `city:index` namespace, the same KV-unconfigured fallback, and the same defensive contract as the existing `recentSlugs` / `recentCities` readers; splitting them across two files would force a future reader to chase imports across two files for the same sorted-set semantics. The reader returns a number rather than a `Number` boxed object because the formatter takes a primitive and the home page binds the result directly to the JSX (no intermediate object shape). `Promise.all` parallelizes the two KV reads (vs serializing them) because the recently-updated list and the total count are independent reads; the round-trip is dominated by network latency to Upstash, so parallelizing pays a real wall-clock saving on a slow connection. Per slice discipline, a "newest in the past 30 days" band, an "active builders" count surfaced from the `city:owner` cookies, a per-slug piece count summary on each card, an "unstoppable cities" filter that surfaces cities with more than 100 pieces, and a load-more / pagination control all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-to-header total-count cue that REQ-011 / REQ-050 name alongside.
- GDD coverage: REQ-011 stays `done` (the `city:index` reader was already shipped; the total-count cue is an additive UX layer that closes the substrate-to-visitor loop for the cardinality of the index alongside the existing window read). Its `implementationRefs` already list `src/lib/recentSlugs.ts` / `src/lib/relativeTime.ts` / `src/app/page.tsx` and gain `src/lib/cityCount.ts`; its `testRefs` already list `tests/lib/recentSlugs.test.ts` / `tests/lib/relativeTime.test.ts` / `e2e/home.spec.ts` and gain `tests/lib/cityCount.test.ts`. REQ-050 stays `done` (the home page surface was already shipped; the per-page total cue rides on the same recently-updated section); its `implementationRefs` gain `src/lib/cityCount.ts`; its `testRefs` gain `tests/lib/cityCount.test.ts`. `docs/gdd/12-home-page.md` build log entry added recording the helper module, the `cityIndexCount` reader, the home page render path, the data attributes, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Home Page Recent-Slugs "Updated N ago" Timestamps: REQ-011, REQ-050

- Branch: `feature/20260504-230407-home-recent-timestamps`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed a real freshness-cue gap on the home page (Pillar 2: "Your city, your URL.") by surfacing a relative "Updated N ago" timestamp on each recently-updated city card so a visitor reads at a glance whether the city was saved minutes, hours, days, or weeks ago. Until this slice the home page (REQ-050) listed the newest-first slugs from `city:index` (REQ-011) but discarded the `Date.now()` score the PUT route writer set as the sorted-set score; the visitor only saw the slug name and had no signal for how recently the city was edited. New pure helper `src/lib/relativeTime.ts` exports `formatRelativeTime(updatedAtMs, nowMs)` which collapses the delta into a short conversational cue across the standard band table: under one minute reads as "just now", one to 59 minutes as "Nm ago", one to 23 hours as "Nh ago", one to six days as "Nd ago", one to four weeks as "Nw ago" (one month is 30 days here), one to 12 months as "Nmo ago" (the ceiling band actually reaches "12mo ago" because 365 / 30 = 12.17), and one year or more as "Ny ago". Future timestamps (input ahead of `nowMs`) collapse to "just now" rather than emitting a confusing negative readout (clock-skew defense), and non-finite or non-positive `updatedAtMs` collapse to the empty string so a tuning bug cannot leak `NaN ago` onto the page; the home page treats the empty string as "no readout" and renders no `<time>` element at all. `RELATIVE_TIME_JUST_NOW_THRESHOLD_MS` (one minute) and `RELATIVE_TIME_JUST_NOW_LABEL` ("just now") land as exported constants so tests can pin the boundary without parsing the formatter output. `src/lib/recentSlugs.ts` gains a sibling `recentCities(limit)` that mirrors `recentSlugs` exactly except the return shape is `RecentCityEntry[] = { slug, updatedAt }[]` and the `zrange` call passes `withScores: true` so the score interleave is available; the loop walks the `[member, score, member, score, ...]` shape, runs each member through `SlugSchema.safeParse` (mirroring the existing `recentSlugs` defensive contract so an older malformed slug write still gets skipped with a warning rather than leaking a broken link onto the home page), runs each score through `Number()` plus a `Number.isFinite` guard so a non-finite stored score (NaN / Infinity / non-numeric) is skipped with a warning rather than letting a tuning bug leak `NaN ago` into the UI. The existing `recentSlugs(limit)` reader stays in place for backward compatibility (no other caller reads it today, but keeping the simpler API around costs nothing and matches the symmetry with `recentVersions.ts` which also exposes a hash-only `recentVersions` plus the entries-with-scores shape). `src/app/page.tsx` now imports `recentCities` and `formatRelativeTime`, snapshots `Date.now()` once per request as `nowMs` so every entry's relative cue is computed against the same clock (a slow render against a per-entry `Date.now()` could otherwise leak inconsistent readouts for two cities that landed at the same instant), and renders each card as a `<Link>` containing a `<span data-testid="home-recent-slug">{slug}</span>` plus a conditional `<time data-testid="home-recent-updated" data-updated-at={updatedAt} dateTime={iso} title={iso}>Updated {relative}</time>`. The `<time>` element is dropped when the formatter returns the empty string so a defensive fallback does not leak a stray cue. The card's flex direction flips from `block` to `column` so the slug name and the timestamp stack vertically with a 4 px gap; the timestamp uses `fontSize: 12, opacity: 0.6` so the visitor's eye reads the slug first and the freshness cue second. The `<time dateTime={iso}>` ISO 8601 attribute plus the `title={iso}` tooltip surface the absolute timestamp for screen-reader users and visitors who hover the cue (the `<time>` element is the canonical HTML5 vocabulary for this exact use case). The `data-updated-at={updatedAt}` attribute exposes the raw millisecond score on both the link and the time element so a future test can pin the boundary without parsing the visible cue. Tests `tests/lib/relativeTime.test.ts` ship 40 cases under nine describe blocks: `constants` (3 cases: threshold equals one minute, label equals literal "just now", trim invariant), `just now band` (4 cases: equal timestamps, one second delta, one millisecond below the minute boundary, future timestamps collapse to "just now"), `minute band` (4 cases: 1m boundary, 5m, 59m one-millisecond-below-hour, 90 seconds floors to 1m), `hour band` (3 cases: 1h boundary, 5h, 23h below day), `day band` (3 cases: 1d boundary, 3d, 6d below week), `week band` (3 cases: 1w boundary, 2w, 4w below month), `month band` (3 cases: 1mo boundary, 6mo, 12mo below year because 365 / 30 = 12.17, plus a 11mo case at exactly 11 months), `year band` (3 cases: 1y, 5y, 10y), `defensive fallbacks` (8 cases: zero / negative / NaN / +Inf / -Inf updatedAt collapse to empty; zero / NaN / negative nowMs collapse to empty), and `output shape contracts` (5 cases: every band ends with " ago", every band starts with a digit, the seven cues are pairwise distinct, "just now" does not include the word "ago", the formatter is deterministic). `tests/lib/recentSlugs.test.ts` adds 10 new `recentCities (REQ-011, REQ-050)` cases mirroring the existing `recentSlugs` contract: KV unconfigured returns empty, empty index returns empty, slugs paired with stored timestamps newest-first, explicit limit smaller than index respects the cap, full index when limit exceeds, zero / negative limit returns empty, max limit cap, `withScores: true` is passed to `zrange`, schema-skip with warn, namespace key invariant.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1539/1539 pass (1489 prior + 40 new relativeTime cases + 10 new recentCities cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/` route-specific JS at 1.41 kB (was 1.36 kB; the new helper imports and the `<time>` render path add a small slice). `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/home.spec.ts` passed 2/3; the third spec (`home page renders the heading, the Create form, and the empty list`) reproduced the pre-existing flake where the `.env.local` materialized live store populates `city:index` so the empty-state asserts `data-recent-count='0'` but reads the live count instead. Verified via `git stash` round-trip on main; CI does not exhibit the flake because the workflow runs without `.env.local` and `recentCities()` falls back to the empty list.
- Assumptions: The "Updated N ago" cue uses the conversational band table (just now / m / h / d / w / mo / y) rather than a single unit (e.g. "Updated at 2026-05-04 23:04 UTC") because the visitor's eye reads the cue at a glance to decide whether to drive into the city; an absolute timestamp would force the visitor to do the math. The 30-days-per-month and 365-days-per-year approximations are coarse on purpose: the band's job is "is this fresh or stale" not "exactly when did this land". The exact ISO timestamp ships in the `<time dateTime>` attribute and the `title` tooltip so a visitor who needs precision can hover or a screen reader can read both the relative and the absolute. The just-now threshold of one minute (vs. ten seconds or one hour) matches a common conversational shape: a save that landed five seconds ago should not read as a stale "0m ago" stub. Future timestamps collapse to "just now" rather than "in the future" because clock skew between the writer and the reader could legitimately produce a small future delta, and a "just now" cue in that case is closer to the truth than a literal "5m in the future" cue (which would also break the band's "ago" terminal vocabulary). Non-finite or non-positive `updatedAt` collapsing to the empty string (vs. a placeholder like "unknown") lets the home page render no `<time>` element at all rather than a stray cue; this is the safer default because a malformed score is a tuning bug, not a user-facing state, so silently dropping the cue is better than painting a confusing label. `recentCities` lives in `src/lib/recentSlugs.ts` rather than its own module because it shares the same `city:index` namespace, the same `SlugSchema` defensive contract, the same KV-unconfigured fallback, and the same MAX limit constant as the existing `recentSlugs`; splitting them across two files would force a future reader to chase imports across two files for the same sorted-set semantics. `recentSlugs` (the bare-slug reader) stays in place for backward compatibility even though no caller reads it today, mirroring the symmetry with `src/lib/recentVersions.ts` which exposes both the hash-only and the entries-with-scores shape; future callers that only need the slug list (e.g. a sitemap generator) can use `recentSlugs` without paying the score-parse cost. The `<time>` element is the canonical HTML5 element for this use case (vs. a `<span>` or a `<small>`) so a screen reader announces the freshness cue in the right context and an accessible browsing surface (e.g. Reader Mode) reads the timestamp correctly. `nowMs` is snapshotted once per request rather than read per-entry so two cities saved at the same `Date.now()` instant produce the same cue; otherwise a slow render could leak inconsistent readouts ("just now" for the first card, "1m ago" for the third). The relative-time helper is pure (`nowMs` is a parameter rather than a module-level `Date.now()` call) so unit tests can pin a deterministic clock without `vi.useFakeTimers()` plumbing. Per slice discipline, an absolute date toggle (click the cue to switch from relative to absolute), a per-slug last-edit author surface, a per-slug piece count summary on the home card, a load-more / pagination control, and a "newest 30 days" filter all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-to-card freshness cue that REQ-011's already-written `Date.now()` score names alongside.
- GDD coverage: REQ-011 stays `done` (the `city:index` reader was already shipped; the freshness cue is an additive UX layer that closes the substrate-to-visitor loop for the score data already in KV). Its `implementationRefs` already list `src/lib/recentSlugs.ts` and gain `src/lib/relativeTime.ts`; its `testRefs` already list `tests/lib/recentSlugs.test.ts` and gain `tests/lib/relativeTime.test.ts`. REQ-050 stays `done` (the home page surface was already shipped; the per-card timestamp rides on the same recently-updated section). Its `implementationRefs` already list `src/app/page.tsx` and gain `src/lib/relativeTime.ts`; its `testRefs` already list `e2e/home.spec.ts` and gain `tests/lib/relativeTime.test.ts`. `docs/gdd/12-home-page.md` build log entry added recording the helper module, the `recentCities` reader, the home page render path, the data attributes, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-03, Editor Toolbar Copy-Build-URL Button: REQ-007, REQ-026

- Branch: `feature/20260503-editor-copy-build-url`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed the share-UX gap on the editor surface by adding a "Copy build URL" button to the editor toolbar so a co-author can hand the canonical edit link off without leaving the editor view (Pillar 1: "Build it. Drive it. Build more."). Until this slice the drive HUD already had a "Copy share URL" button (REQ-006, REQ-053) that copied the canonical drive URL `/<slug>`, but the editor surface had no equivalent affordance for the build URL `/<slug>/edit`. A builder who wanted to share the build link with another builder had to copy the address bar manually; the prior drive HUD share-copy slice's progress log entry explicitly named "a copy-link button on the editor toolbar (the editor URL is the build link, also worth surfacing)" as deferred follow-up work, and this slice closes that gap. `src/app/[slug]/shareUrl.ts` gains `buildEditUrl(slug, origin?)` mirroring `buildShareUrl` exactly except the trailing path is `/<slug>/edit` (same trailing-slash strip and blank / null / undefined / whitespace-only origin collapse to the bare relative path so a server-rendered preview or misconfigured deployment still emits a usable link), `EDIT_COPY_LABEL_IDLE = 'Copy build URL'` (the build verb mirrors the GDD's "build it, drive it, build more" loop vocabulary), `editCopyLabel(status)` (idle returns the editor idle label, copied / error reuse `SHARE_COPY_LABEL_COPIED` / `SHARE_COPY_LABEL_ERROR` so the lifecycle feedback vocabulary stays one channel between the drive HUD and the editor toolbar), and `editCopyAriaLabel(slug, status)` (sentence-form accessible name with the slug and the build noun so a screen-reader user hears the editor URL is the destination). `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` imports the new helpers alongside the shared `SHARE_COPY_RESET_DELAY_MS` and `CopyShareStatus`, adds `editCopyStatus` React state plus `editCopyResetTimerRef` for the reset timer, `handleCopyEditUrl` async callback that reads `window.location.origin`, builds the URL via `buildEditUrl`, calls `navigator.clipboard.writeText(url)` inside a try/catch (and treats a missing `navigator.clipboard` or a missing `writeText` as the error path), sets the next status, and schedules a `setTimeout` reset to idle after `SHARE_COPY_RESET_DELAY_MS` (clearing any prior timer first so a re-click does not race with a stale reset), plus a cleanup effect that clears the timer on unmount. The toolbar gains a new `editor-copy-build-url` button positioned between the Reset View button and the Drive CTA so the editor toolbar order reads palette / rotate / erase / undo / redo / reset / copy build URL / drive; the button uses the existing toolbar surface vocabulary (cream `#efe7d2` background, dark `#222` text, `#d6cfbf` neutral border at idle) with a per-status border (success: green `#6a8f5a`, error: warning red `#a3372a`, idle: neutral) and a warning-red text color in the error state so a builder whose browser refused the clipboard call sees the failure rather than thinking the click silently succeeded. The button carries `data-testid="editor-copy-build-url"`, `data-copy-status` mirroring the live status, and an `aria-label` from `editCopyAriaLabel` that reads as a sentence including the slug. The toolbar root carries a new `data-copy-build-status` attribute mirroring the same status so a test can read the state without sampling the button DOM. Tests `tests/app/shareUrl.test.ts` add 35 cases under four new describe blocks: `EDIT_COPY_LABEL_IDLE (REQ-007, REQ-026)` (5 cases: non-empty, trimmed, distinct from the drive idle label, mentions the build verb, mentions the copy verb), `buildEditUrl (REQ-007, REQ-026)` (14 cases: clean origin composes the absolute editor URL, trailing slash stripped, no double slash on bare origin, undefined / null / empty / whitespace origins collapse to relative path, ports preserved on localhost, http and https protocols pass through, bare `/` does not duplicate the slash, hyphenated slug works, relative path starts with a single slash, the URL ends with `/edit`, differs from `buildShareUrl` by exactly the `/edit` suffix, lifecycle reset delay invariant), `editCopyLabel (REQ-007, REQ-026)` (8 cases: idle / copied / error per-status mapping, every `CopyShareStatus` member yields a non-empty label, three pairwise distinct labels, idle differs from the drive idle, copied / error match the drive labels), `editCopyAriaLabel (REQ-007, REQ-026)` (8 cases: each status mentions the slug, three pairwise distinct sentences, idle reads as a future-tense Copy verb, copied reads as past-tense, error calls out the failure, every status mentions the build verb, idle differs from the drive idle aria-label). New `e2e/editor.spec.ts` `/copy-build-spec/edit` spec grants the `clipboard-read` and `clipboard-write` permissions on a fresh browser context, asserts the button mounts at the idle state with the expected text and aria-label, clicks it, asserts the button flips to "Copied!" with `data-copy-status='copied'` and the toolbar `data-copy-build-status='copied'`, asserts the aria-label flips to the past-tense form, reads back the clipboard text via `navigator.clipboard.readText()` and confirms it equals the canonical editor URL composed from the page origin plus `/edit`, then waits the reset delay and asserts the button resets to idle on both the button and the toolbar data attributes.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1489/1489 pass (1452 prior + 35 new shareUrl cases; the 2 extra over the 35 explicit cases are existing labelled cases re-grouped under new identifiers within the existing describes). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 7.79 kB (was 7.5 kB; the new state, callback, and button render path add a small slice). `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/editor.spec.ts` passed 28/28 (27 prior + 1 new copy-build-URL spec). `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/drive.spec.ts` passed 4/4 to confirm the drive HUD share-copy plumbing was unaffected.
- Assumptions: The button label "Copy build URL" mirrors the drive HUD's "Copy share URL" with the build verb so the editor toolbar reads as the build half of the build / drive share-link pair; an alternative shape ("Copy edit URL") would name the route segment but not the loop vocabulary the GDD uses ("Build it. Drive it. Build more.") so "build" reads as the more on-brand phrase. The button sits between the Reset View button and the Drive CTA because the Drive CTA is the rightmost terminal action (it navigates away from the editor) and the copy-build-URL button is the second-most rightmost action (it is a self-referential action that does not navigate away); placing them next to each other makes the build / drive share-link pair read as one unit at the trailing edge of the toolbar. The button uses the existing cream / dark toolbar vocabulary (vs the drive HUD button's dark `rgba(34, 34, 34, 0.7)` background) because the toolbar's other buttons (rotate, erase, undo, redo, reset view) all share that palette and the editor's surface is a light-themed editing canvas; the drive HUD's dark surface uses the dark vocabulary because it sits on top of a 3D scene where the dark background reads against the bright canvas. The per-status border (idle neutral grey, success green, error warning red) and warning-red error text color are imported verbatim from the drive HUD share-copy button so the success / error feedback vocabulary stays one channel between the two surfaces; a builder who clicks both buttons in one session reads the same color cues. The reset delay reuses `SHARE_COPY_RESET_DELAY_MS` (1600 ms) so the two buttons share lifecycle timing; defining a separate `EDIT_COPY_RESET_DELAY_MS` would let the two surfaces drift in cadence without a clear UX justification. The clipboard call sits inside a try/catch with both branches setting status because Web platform errors here come in two shapes (`navigator.clipboard` undefined on older browsers / non-secure-context, `writeText` throws on permission-denied); treating both as the error path lets the builder see the failure visibly instead of thinking the click silently succeeded. Per slice discipline, a unified share-sheet that exposes both the drive and edit URLs, a per-version-pinned build URL that includes the `?v=<hash>` query parameter (REQ-048 / REQ-049), a QR-code share modal, and a touch-share intent that dispatches to native OS share sheets all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-to-button copy action that REQ-007 / REQ-026 name alongside the existing Drive CTA.
- GDD coverage: REQ-007 stays `done` (the editor route was already shipped; the copy-build-URL button is an additive UX layer that closes the substrate-to-builder loop for the canonical edit URL alongside the existing drive HUD share-copy button); its `implementationRefs` already list `src/app/[slug]/edit/page.tsx` / `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` and gain `src/app/[slug]/shareUrl.ts`; its `testRefs` already list `e2e/editor.spec.ts` and gain `tests/app/shareUrl.test.ts`. REQ-026 stays `done` (the Drive CTA was already in the toolbar; the copy-build-URL button rides on the same toolbar surface as the complementary half of the build / drive share-link pair); its `implementationRefs` gain `src/app/[slug]/shareUrl.ts`; its `testRefs` gain `tests/app/shareUrl.test.ts`. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` build log entry added recording the helper module additions, the toolbar render path, the data attributes, the cream / dark palette rationale, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-03, Per-Route Metadata for Drive and Edit Pages: REQ-006, REQ-007, REQ-053

- Branch: `feature/20260503-per-slug-metadata`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed a real share-UX gap by giving the drive route at `/<slug>` and the editor route at `/<slug>/edit` a per-slug browser-tab title and link-preview description. Until this slice every per-slug page inherited the application-wide `metadata` from `src/app/layout.tsx` (title "VibeCity", a one-line site description), which meant a shared drive link in iMessage, Slack, or a browser tab list read as the bare site name with no slug context, and a builder with two tabs open could not tell the drive view of "downtown" from the editor of "harbor-loop" by glancing at the tab strip. New pure helper module `src/app/[slug]/slugMetadata.ts` ships `APP_NAME = 'VibeCity'` (matching the layout brand so the per-route title closes with the same word the home tab carries), `TITLE_SEPARATOR = ' | '` (the conventional pipe with single spaces so a long slug followed by the app name still reads in a constrained tab strip), `driveTitle(slug)` returning "Drive <slug> | VibeCity" (slug first because a builder with many tabs scans the slug column first; role second; brand last), `editTitle(slug)` returning "Edit <slug> | VibeCity" (same shape so the only difference between drive and edit titles for the same slug is the role verb), `driveDescription(slug)` returning "Drive around the <slug> city on VibeCity." (sentence-form so the social-share card reads as a complete thought), and `editDescription(slug)` returning "Build the <slug> city on VibeCity." (mirrors the drive description with the build verb so the build / drive loop reads as two halves of the same surface). `src/app/[slug]/page.tsx` and `src/app/[slug]/edit/page.tsx` each gain an exported async `generateMetadata({ params })` that awaits `params`, runs the slug through `parseSlugParam` for the same accept / reject contract the page render uses, returns `{}` on rejection so the layout default still applies on the 404 render path (a slug-shaped title for a URL that did not load would be misleading), and otherwise returns `{ title, description, openGraph: { title, description }, twitter: { title, description } }` so the same per-slug strings ride through to the link-preview cards on iMessage, Slack, Twitter, Discord, and any other crawler that reads the OpenGraph or Twitter meta tags. Tests `tests/app/slugMetadata.test.ts` add 31 cases under seven describe blocks: constants (APP_NAME matches the brand and is non-empty trimmed; TITLE_SEPARATOR is exactly ` | `, three characters wide), `driveTitle` (simple slug, kebab-case slug, starts with "Drive ", ends with the app name, contains the slug verbatim, separator splits cleanly into slug-side / app-side, fresh string per call), `editTitle` (same six contract cases plus the "Edit " role-verb assertion), drive vs edit (titles differ; the only difference is the role-verb prefix; both share the same suffix), `driveDescription` (literal sentence form, contains the slug verbatim, mentions the app name, starts with "Drive ", ends with a period), `editDescription` (same five contract cases with "Build" verb), and drive vs edit description (descriptions differ; both name the slug verbatim). `tests/app/driveRoute.test.ts` adds 6 cases under a new `generateMetadata` describe block: per-slug title and description, OpenGraph mirroring, Twitter mirroring, empty object on invalid slug, empty object on empty slug, empty object on too-long slug. `tests/app/editRoute.test.ts` adds 5 cases under a new `generateMetadata` describe block: per-slug title and description, OpenGraph mirroring, Twitter mirroring, empty object on invalid slug, empty object on empty slug. New `e2e/drive.spec.ts` `/title-spec-city` spec asserts `expect(page).toHaveTitle('Drive title-spec-city | VibeCity')` after a 200 navigation; new `e2e/editor.spec.ts` `/title-spec-city/edit` spec asserts `expect(page).toHaveTitle('Edit title-spec-city | VibeCity')`.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1452/1452 pass (1410 prior + 31 new slugMetadata + 6 new driveRoute generateMetadata + 5 new editRoute generateMetadata). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 145 kB unchanged and `/[slug]/edit` at 7.5 kB unchanged (the metadata helpers are pure constants and small string composers; the `generateMetadata` export is server-side only and does not bundle into the client). `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/drive.spec.ts -g "per-slug document title"` passed 1/1 and `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/editor.spec.ts -g "per-slug document title"` passed 1/1.
- Assumptions: The title shape "Drive <slug> | VibeCity" puts the slug first because a builder with many tabs open scans the slug column first; an alternative shape ("VibeCity: Drive <slug>") would brand-load every tab with the same opening word and obscure the slug. The role verb ("Drive" or "Edit") sits between the slug and the app name so the shape reads as "what you are doing to which city on what app" in three left-to-right beats. The conventional ` | ` (space-pipe-space) separator is the most common Web convention (vs " - " which em-dash discipline forbids and " : " which reads as a label rather than a separator) so a screen-reader user hears the title as a natural pause-and-resume. The description sentence form "Drive around the <slug> city on VibeCity." (vs a noun-phrase "Drive view of <slug>") is a complete sentence so the OpenGraph and Twitter card descriptions read like a one-line invitation rather than a tag. The `editDescription` uses "Build" rather than "Edit" because the GDD's loop pillar names the verb pair "Build it. Drive it. Build more." and matching that vocabulary keeps the share-link descriptions on-brand with the rest of the product copy. Returning `{}` on slug rejection rather than an explicit `{ title: APP_NAME }` lets the layout default cascade through; explicitly setting the brand fragment here would duplicate the layout's source of truth and risk drift if the layout title ever changes. Mirroring the same per-slug `title` and `description` into both `openGraph` and `twitter` (rather than crafting separate copy per channel) is the simplest consistent rule; per-channel divergence (e.g. a different lead sentence for Twitter's 200-char description cap) stays deferred to a future slice that can measure which channels matter most to share traffic. The `generateMetadata` export deliberately re-validates the slug rather than relying on the page's render-time `notFound()` call because Next.js calls `generateMetadata` before the page render so the metadata function must own the same accept / reject contract; sharing the validation through `parseSlugParam` keeps both branches reading from one source. Per slice discipline, dynamic Open Graph image generation per slug (an OG card that previews the city's piece silhouette using `@vercel/og`), per-version-pinned title shape (e.g. "Drive <slug> @ <hash:8> | VibeCity" when `?v=` is supplied), per-language localization of the role verbs, a canonical URL link tag, and a structured-data JSON-LD block for search engines all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-to-title metadata that REQ-006 / REQ-007 / REQ-053 name alongside the existing share-copy button.
