- GDD coverage: REQ-006 stays `done` (the slug route was already shipped; the per-slug metadata is an additive UX layer that closes the share-context loop for the canonical drive URL); its `implementationRefs` already list `src/app/[slug]/page.tsx` and gain `src/app/[slug]/slugMetadata.ts`; its `testRefs` already list `tests/app/driveRoute.test.ts` and `e2e/drive.spec.ts` and gain `tests/app/slugMetadata.test.ts`. REQ-007 stays `done` (the editor route was already shipped); its `implementationRefs` already list `src/app/[slug]/edit/page.tsx` and gain `src/app/[slug]/slugMetadata.ts`; its `testRefs` already list `tests/app/editRoute.test.ts` and `e2e/editor.spec.ts` and gain `tests/app/slugMetadata.test.ts`. REQ-053 stays `done` (the empty-state prompt was already shipped); the per-route metadata rides on the same drive surface and is observable on both the empty and non-empty branches; its `implementationRefs` gain `src/app/[slug]/slugMetadata.ts`; its `testRefs` gain `tests/app/slugMetadata.test.ts`. `docs/gdd/04-slug-routing.md` build log entry added recording the helper module, the `generateMetadata` exports, the title / description shape rationale, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-03, Drive HUD Share-URL Copy Button: REQ-006, REQ-053

- Branch: `feature/20260504-share-copy-button`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed a real share-UX gap by adding a "Copy share URL" button next to the drive view's slug pill so a player can hand the canonical drive link to a friend without leaving the drive view (Pillar 2: "Your city, your URL"). Until this slice the slug pill in the top-left corner was a static label; the share link was implicit in the address bar but a player had no in-product affordance to grab it. New pure module `src/app/[slug]/shareUrl.ts` ships `CopyShareStatus = 'idle' | 'copied' | 'error'` (the three-state lifecycle the button cycles through), `SHARE_COPY_LABEL_IDLE = 'Copy share URL'`, `SHARE_COPY_LABEL_COPIED = 'Copied!'`, `SHARE_COPY_LABEL_ERROR = 'Copy failed'` (one short verb phrase per state so the click registers as visible feedback even if the player did not glance at a toast), `SHARE_COPY_RESET_DELAY_MS = 1600` (long enough to read the feedback, short enough that a re-click after a failure feels fresh), `buildShareUrl(slug, origin?)` (composes the absolute URL when an origin is provided and a relative `/<slug>` path otherwise so a server-rendered preview or a misconfigured deployment that fails to read `window.location` still emits a usable link; strips a single trailing slash on the origin so callers that pass `https://x/` and callers that pass `https://x` both produce `https://x/<slug>`; whitespace-only origin collapses to the relative path so a misconfigured deployment does not emit `/<slug>` from a bare `/`), `shareCopyLabel(status)` (visible button label per status), and `shareCopyAriaLabel(slug, status)` (sentence-form accessible name that interpolates the slug so a screen-reader user hears the destination URL). `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` adds `shareCopyStatus` React state plus `shareCopyResetTimerRef` for the reset timer, `handleCopyShareUrl` async callback that reads `window.location.origin`, builds the URL via `buildShareUrl`, calls `navigator.clipboard.writeText(url)` inside a try/catch (and treats a missing `navigator.clipboard` or a missing `writeText` as the error path), sets the next status, and schedules a `setTimeout` reset to idle after `SHARE_COPY_RESET_DELAY_MS` (clearing any prior timer first so a re-click does not race with a stale reset), and a cleanup effect that clears the timer on unmount so the React state setter does not fire after the component is gone. The slug pill JSX is rewrapped in a `display: flex; gap: 8` row so the new `drive-share-copy` button sits inline next to the existing `drive-scene-slug` span; the button uses the same dark `rgba(34, 34, 34, 0.7)` background as the slug pill and the engine-mute toggle so the top-left HUD vocabulary stays one channel, with a per-status border (success: green `#6a8f5a`, error: warning red `#a3372a`, idle: neutral grey `#444`) and a warning-red text color in the error state so a player whose browser refused the clipboard call sees the failure rather than thinking the click silently succeeded. The button carries `data-testid="drive-share-copy"`, `data-share-status` mirroring the live status, and an `aria-label` from `shareCopyAriaLabel` that reads as a sentence including the slug. The drive-scene root carries a new `data-share-copy-status` attribute mirroring the same status so a test can read the state without sampling the button DOM. The button is always visible (regardless of `hasVehicle`) because the share link is the drive URL itself and an empty grid is still shareable. Tests `tests/app/shareUrl.test.ts` add 30 cases under five describe blocks: constants (idle / copied / error labels are non-empty, pairwise distinct, trimmed; reset delay is in the (0, 5000) ms window and at least 800 ms), `buildShareUrl` (clean origin composes the absolute URL, trailing slash on origin stripped, undefined / null / empty / whitespace origins collapse to relative path, ports preserved on localhost, http and https protocols pass through, bare `/` origin does not duplicate the slash, hyphenated slug works, relative path starts with a single slash so it is a valid href shape), `shareCopyLabel` (idle / copied / error map to their named labels and every `CopyShareStatus` member yields a non-empty label), `shareCopyAriaLabel` (each status mentions the slug, three pairwise distinct sentences, idle reads as a future-tense verb, copied reads as past-tense, error calls out the failure). New `e2e/drive.spec.ts` `/share-copy-spec` spec grants the `clipboard-read` and `clipboard-write` permissions on a fresh browser context, asserts the button mounts at the idle state with the expected text and aria-label, clicks it, asserts the button flips to "Copied!" with `data-share-status='copied'` and the root `data-share-copy-status='copied'`, asserts the aria-label flips to the past-tense form, reads back the clipboard text via `navigator.clipboard.readText()` and confirms it equals the canonical drive URL composed from the page origin, then waits the reset delay and asserts the button resets to idle on both the button and the root data attributes. The existing scaffold spec gains a small assertion block confirming the button mounts at idle on the empty-grid scaffold so the always-visible contract is locked down across every drive route load.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1410/1410 pass (1380 prior + 30 new shareUrl cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 145 kB (was 144 kB; the new state, callback, and button render path add a small slice). `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/drive.spec.ts` passed 3/3 (2 prior + 1 new share-copy spec).
- Assumptions: The button always renders (regardless of `hasVehicle`) because the share link is the drive URL itself; an empty city is still shareable (the recipient can open the editor and start building, or just drive an empty grid). Hiding the button on the empty grid would force a player who shared a fresh slug to load a non-empty city before they could grab the link, which contradicts the empty-state's role as a recoverable starting point. The button sits inline next to the slug pill (vs. its own corner) because the slug pill is the canonical "this is the city you are at" surface; the share button is the action that turns that identifier into a sharable link, so colocating them reads as one unit. The dark `rgba(34, 34, 34, 0.7)` background matches the slug pill and engine-mute toggle so the top-left HUD vocabulary stays one channel; a brighter accent color would compete with the warm cream Edit CTA in the top-right and the warning-red HUD labels in the bottom-right. The per-status border uses three colors (idle grey, success green, error warning-red) so a player reading the button at a glance sees the click registered without having to read the label; the warning-red error state additionally tints the text so a failed copy reads as a failure rather than a stuck label. The reset delay of 1600 ms is in the same range as toast notifications across other apps; a value below 800 ms would feel like a flicker, a value above 5000 ms would make a re-click after failure feel like a stuck state. The clipboard call sits inside a try/catch with both branches setting status because Web platform errors here come in two shapes: `navigator.clipboard` undefined (older browsers, non-secure-context) returns no error but also performs no write; `writeText` throws on permission-denied. Treating both as the error path lets the player see the failure visibly instead of thinking the click silently succeeded. Per slice discipline, an Open Graph (OG) image preview for the drive URL, a "share via" sheet that dispatches to native OS share intents, a pinned-version share URL that includes the `?v=<hash>` query parameter (REQ-048 / REQ-049), a QR-code share modal, and a copy-link button on the editor toolbar (the editor URL is the build link, also worth surfacing) all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-to-button copy action that REQ-006 (slug routing) and REQ-053 (drive empty state) name alongside.
- GDD coverage: REQ-006 stays `done` (the slug route was already shipped; the share-copy button is an additive UX layer that closes the substrate-to-player loop for the canonical drive URL); its `implementationRefs` already list `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` and gain `src/app/[slug]/shareUrl.ts`; its `testRefs` already list `e2e/drive.spec.ts` and gain `tests/app/shareUrl.test.ts`. REQ-053 stays `done` (the empty-state prompt was already shipped; the share-copy button rides on the same drive surface and is observable on both the empty and non-empty branches); its `implementationRefs` gain `src/app/[slug]/shareUrl.ts`; its `testRefs` gain `tests/app/shareUrl.test.ts`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` build log entry added recording the button, the reset timer, the clipboard error handling, the data attributes, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-03, Editor Spawn-Anchor Toolbar Readout: REQ-019, REQ-036

- Branch: `feature/20260504-222303-spawn-anchor-readout`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: extended the editor toolbar with a visible spawn-anchor readout so a builder reads which cell the car will spawn in and which way it will face without having to scan the SVG marker. Until this slice the spawn anchor was visualized only as the cool-blue ring + chevron on the grid (REQ-019, REQ-036 prior slice); the toolbar lined up readouts for piece count, connector match, track-path, open ends, and autosave status but had no entry for the spawn pose. New helpers in `src/app/[slug]/edit/spawnMarker.ts`: `SPAWN_DIRECTION_LONG_LABEL: Record<SpawnDirectionLabel, string>` maps the four short compass labels to their full English words ("North" / "East" / "South" / "West") so the toolbar reads as a sentence rather than a compass code, and `spawnAnchorReadout(city)` returns null on an empty city (mirrors the marker's null contract; the drive scene never mounts the car on an empty grid) and otherwise emits one `SpawnAnchorReadout` carrying `cellRow` / `cellCol` (mirroring `pieces[0]` so the readout cell agrees with the marker cell), `direction` (the short compass label so a test can pin orientation by readable string), and `text` (the toolbar string `Spawn: (row, col) facing North`). `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` imports the new resolver alongside the existing `spawnAnchorMarker`, computes `spawnReadout = spawnAnchorReadout(city)` once per render after the existing marker, and renders an `editor-spawn-anchor-readout` paragraph that mounts only when the readout is non-null (so the readout stays silent on an empty city, mirroring the silent-on-empty pattern set by `editor-track-path-readout` and `editor-unmatched-ports-readout`). The paragraph carries `data-spawn-anchor-row` / `data-spawn-anchor-col` / `data-spawn-anchor-direction` data attributes for tests, sits between the unmatched-ports readout and the autosave-status readout so the toolbar order reads (pieces / connectors / track-path / open-ends / spawn / autosave), and uses the cool-blue `#3a6ea0` color matching the SVG spawn-marker channel so the editor's directional vocabulary stays one channel across the marker and the readout. Tests `tests/app/spawnMarker.test.ts` add 14 cases under two new describe blocks: `SPAWN_DIRECTION_LONG_LABEL (REQ-019, REQ-036)` covers each cardinal long-label mapping, the four-cardinal coverage with no duplicates, and the trim contract; `spawnAnchorReadout (REQ-019, REQ-036)` covers null-on-empty-city, null-on-buildings-only-city, North readout at rotation 0, East at rotation 90, South at rotation 180, West at rotation 270, mirroring `pieces[0]` not the most recently placed, cross-check that the readout direction agrees with `spawnAnchorMarker` for every rotation, fresh-object contract, no input mutation, text contains the long-label for every cardinal, and text starts with "Spawn:". New `e2e/editor.spec.ts` `/spawn-anchor-readout-spec/edit` spec asserts the empty grid has zero readouts, placing one straight at `(0, 0)` mounts exactly one readout reading "Spawn: (0, 0) facing North" with row 0 col 0 direction N, placing a second piece at `(0, 1)` keeps the readout pinned to `pieces[0]`, erasing both pieces drops the readout to absent, and switching back to place mode plus rotating to 90 plus placing at `(2, -1)` flips the readout to "Spawn: (2, -1) facing East" with row 2 col -1 direction E.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1380/1380 pass (1365 prior + 14 new spawn-anchor-readout cases + 1 new long-label trim contract case bundled into the constants block). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 7.5 kB (was 7.36 kB; the new resolver and the toolbar paragraph add a small slice). `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/editor.spec.ts -g "spawn-anchor toolbar readout"` passed 1/1.
- Assumptions: The readout text uses the long-label ("North") rather than the short compass letter ("N") because the toolbar is a sentence-level surface read at human pace; the SVG `data-spawn-marker-direction` already carries the short letter for Playwright locators that need a single-character match. Cell coordinates render as `(row, col)` rather than `(col, row)` because the editor's existing data attribute order is `data-cell-row` then `data-cell-col` (and the cell coordinate convention across the codebase is row-first), so matching that order keeps the editor's coordinate vocabulary consistent. The readout mounts only when at least one piece is placed (matching `spawnAnchorMarker`'s null-on-empty-city contract) rather than always rendering with a placeholder text because the silent-on-empty pattern is set by the existing `editor-track-path-readout` and `editor-unmatched-ports-readout` paragraphs; an always-present "Spawn: none" line would crowd the readout without telling the builder anything new. The cool-blue `#3a6ea0` matches the SVG spawn-marker channel so the editor's directional vocabulary stays one channel across the marker and the toolbar (vs. the warm-red `#a3372a` that the open-ends warning channel owns); the editor now has four warning / status channels (placement-rejection amber, open-ends red, spawn-anchor blue, neutral grey for the bookkeeping readouts). The readout paragraph sits between the unmatched-ports readout and the autosave-status readout because the spawn anchor is the last forward-looking readout (what the player will see when they click Drive) before the autosave-status readout (which is the editor's own bookkeeping channel); the order mirrors the existing top-down progression from city-shape readouts to player-facing readouts to bookkeeping. Per slice discipline, a click-to-edit spawn cell that lets the builder pin a non-`pieces[0]` cell, an animated highlight on the readout when the spawn pose changes, a per-piece-type spawn-orientation cue (e.g. an intersection's spawn arrow points along its outgoing leg), and a drive-mode camera nudge toward the spawn anchor at first mount all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-to-toolbar readout that REQ-019 / REQ-036 name alongside the existing SVG marker.
- GDD coverage: REQ-019 stays `partial` (the runtime branching surface still gates the `done` flip) and its existing `implementationRefs` already include `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` and `src/app/[slug]/edit/spawnMarker.ts`; its `testRefs` already include `tests/app/spawnMarker.test.ts` and `e2e/editor.spec.ts`. REQ-036 already covers the substrate-side spawn anchor; this slice extends the toolbar readout channel for the same substrate signal. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` build log entry added recording the long-label map, the resolver, the toolbar render path, the data attributes, the cool-blue palette rationale, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-03, Drive Scene Building Roof Caps: REQ-046

- Branch: `feature/20260504-221513-building-roof-caps`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed a real visual-fidelity gap in the drive scene by stacking a per-type roof cap on top of every building extrusion so the four placeholder primitives (small house / mid house / shop / factory) read with stronger silhouettes from the orbit camera. Before this slice, every building rendered as a single `BoxGeometry` body in a flat per-type color; the four types differed only in body color and total height, so a builder hovering the orbit camera over a city saw four solid colored blocks rather than four distinct building forms. New constants in `src/app/[slug]/driveScene.ts`: `BUILDING_ROOF_COLORS` (per-type darker shade so the cap reads as a separate volume on top of the body), `BUILDING_ROOF_HEIGHTS` (per-type cap height: small house 0.18 cells, mid house 0.22 cells, shop 0.10 cells flat parapet, factory 0.5 cells smokestack), `BUILDING_ROOF_INSET_FACTORS` (per-type horizontal scale factor relative to the body footprint: houses 0.86 for a slightly-inset peaked cap, shop 0.96 for a near-full parapet, factory 0.32 for a thin smokestack), and `BUILDING_FOOTPRINT_FACTOR = 0.85` (extracted from the magic number that was previously hardcoded inline in `DriveSceneClient.tsx`'s building loop so the roof cap can scale relative to the body footprint without duplicating the constant). New helpers: `buildingFootprintWorldSize()` returns `CELL_SIZE * BUILDING_FOOTPRINT_FACTOR`, `buildingRoofColorFor(type)` / `buildingRoofHeightFor(type)` / `buildingRoofInsetFactorFor(type)` are strict map lookups parallel to the existing `buildingColorFor` / `buildingHeightFor` accessors, `buildingRoofFootprintFor(type)` returns the body footprint times the inset factor, and `buildingRoofY(type)` returns `buildingHeightFor(type) + buildingRoofHeightFor(type) / 2` so the cap's lower face sits exactly at the body top with no gap and no overlap. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx`'s building render loop now creates two meshes per building: the existing body box (now sized via `buildingFootprintWorldSize()` instead of the previous hardcoded `CELL_SIZE * 0.85`) plus a new roof cap box positioned at `buildingRoofY(type)` with the per-type roof color, height, and footprint. Both meshes share the same world position and `rotation.y` (from the persisted rotation) so a rotated building's cap rotates with the body. Tests `tests/app/driveScene.test.ts` add 14 cases under a new `building roof cap helpers (REQ-046 visual polish)` describe block: footprint factor in (0, 1) so the body inset stays inside the cell, world size equals `CELL_SIZE * BUILDING_FOOTPRINT_FACTOR`, roof maps cover every building type, finite positive roof height per type, valid 24-bit hex roof color per type, inset factor in (0, 1] so the cap never exceeds the body footprint, roof footprint equals body footprint times the inset factor, factory roof is the tallest cap so the smokestack silhouette reads, factory roof inset factor is the smallest so the cap reads as a thin smokestack, shop roof has the shortest cap height so the parapet silhouette stays flat, shop roof inset is wider than the house insets so the parapet covers the body top, every building type roof color differs from its body color so the cap reads as a separate volume, `buildingRoofY` sits above the body height so the cap rests on top without z-fighting, and roof cap fits inside the cell footprint so neighboring buildings do not overlap.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1365/1365 pass (1351 prior + 14 new roof-cap cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 144 kB unchanged (the additional driveScene helpers are pure constants and small accessors so the bundle slice is tiny).
- Assumptions: The roof cap is a separate `BoxGeometry` mesh rather than a single more-complex geometry per type because the goal of v1 buildings is a visible silhouette vocabulary, not high-fidelity per-type building art (the GDD's "Out of scope for v1" list explicitly defers windows, roofs, and decorations beyond placeholder primitives). A second box per building gives the four types meaningfully different orbit-camera silhouettes (peaked vs flat vs smokestack) without inventing per-type sprite art or pulling a GLTF loader into the bundle. The roof color is a darker shade of the body color rather than a full contrast shift because the cap should read as a top-of-the-same-volume rather than a different building stuck on top; the factory's roof color is closer to its body color (vs the houses' more pronounced shift) because the smokestack silhouette already carries the visual cue and a strong color shift would read as a stack on top of a building rather than a part of the same building. The per-type inset factors were picked so the four building silhouettes visually separate at the orbit camera distance: 0.86 for the houses (slightly inset peaked cap), 0.96 for the shop (near-full parapet that reads as a flat top), 0.32 for the factory (thin smokestack that does not read as a second story). The cap's y position is `body height + roof height / 2` so the lower face of the cap sits exactly at the body top; this is the simplest consistent rule and lets a future slice that swaps a placeholder for a higher-fidelity model anchor on the same world coordinate. The cap rotation tracks the body rotation (both meshes use the same `rotation.y` from the persisted rotation) so a rotated building's cap rotates with the body. Per slice discipline, per-type window patterns, per-type texture maps, per-type signage glyphs (e.g. a "Shop" sign on the shop), per-type roof patterns (e.g. tile vs metal), per-type chimneys / antennas, and any animated motion on the cap (e.g. smoke from the factory's smokestack) all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the silhouette-vocabulary upgrade that REQ-046's existing per-type body color / height vocabulary names alongside.
- GDD coverage: REQ-046 stays `done` (the building extrusion was already shipped; the roof-cap layer is an additive visual polish that closes the silhouette-vocabulary leg). Its `implementationRefs` already list `src/app/[slug]/driveScene.ts` and `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx`; its `testRefs` already list `tests/app/driveScene.test.ts`. `docs/gdd/11-scene.md` build log entry added recording the constants, the helpers, the DriveSceneClient render path, the silhouette rationale, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-03, Editor Spawn-Anchor Marker: REQ-019, REQ-036

- Branch: `feature/20260504-220259-spawn-anchor-marker`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed a real UX gap in the editor by surfacing a visible marker on the spawn-anchor cell so a builder sees where the car will spawn and which way it will face without having to click Drive to find out. The drive scene already spawns the player at `pieces[0]` with a heading derived from `pieces[0].rotation` (`spawnAnchor` in `src/app/[slug]/driveScene.ts` plus `respawnVehicle` in `src/app/[slug]/respawn.ts`), but until this slice the editor offered no visible cue for the spawn pose. New pure pixel-space resolver `spawnAnchorMarker(city)` in `src/app/[slug]/edit/spawnMarker.ts` returns null on an empty city (drive scene never mounts the car on an empty grid) and otherwise emits one `SpawnAnchorMarker` carrying `cellRow` / `cellCol` (mirroring the first piece), `ringX` / `ringY` / `ringSize` for an inset overlay rect, `tipX` / `tipY` plus the SVG `points` string for an outward-pointing chevron whose tip extends from the cell center toward the spawn heading direction, and `direction` ('N' / 'E' / 'S' / 'W') / `rotation` (the source rotation) so a test can assert orientation by readable string. Five new exported constants land alongside: `SPAWN_MARKER_HALF_PIXELS = CELL_PIXELS / 2` (cell center anchor), `SPAWN_MARKER_INSET_PIXELS = 3` (ring inset), `SPAWN_ARROW_REACH_PIXELS = CELL_PIXELS / 2 - 4` (chevron tip reach), `SPAWN_ARROW_HALF_PIXELS = CELL_PIXELS / 5` (chevron base half-width), and `SPAWN_DIRECTION_VECTOR` (a `Record<Rotation, { x, y, label }>` mapping the four cardinal rotations to SVG-pixel-space unit vectors plus their compass label). The chevron geometry mirrors the open-end arrow vocabulary (`unmatchedPortGlyphs` in `connectorGlyphs.ts`): tip at `cellCenter + reach * dirVec`, base midpoint stepped backward by half along the direction, two base vertices straddling the perpendicular `(-y, x)` axis. `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` imports the new helper alongside the existing connector / track-path resolvers, computes `spawnMarker = spawnAnchorMarker(city)` once per render, and threads it into `<SnapGrid />` as a new `spawnMarker` prop. `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` accepts a new optional `spawnMarker?: SpawnAnchorMarker | null` prop, mirrors five new attributes onto the SVG root (`data-spawn-marker` 'present' or 'absent', `data-spawn-marker-row`, `data-spawn-marker-col`, `data-spawn-marker-direction`, `data-spawn-marker-rotation`), and renders a non-interactive `editor-spawn-marker` `<g>` group between the cells loop and the open-end overlay layer (so the spawn marker reads as a layer above the placed-piece tile but below the open-end warning ring on the same cell, since both can apply when an isolated straight is placed at the origin). The group contains a dashed-stroke `editor-spawn-marker-ring` `<rect>` (transparent fill, `#3a6ea0` cool-blue stroke, `4 2` dasharray so the ring reads as a navigation cue distinct from the warm-amber rejection flash and the warm-red open-end warning) and a filled `editor-spawn-marker-arrow` `<polygon>` (cool-blue `#3a6ea0` fill, darker `#1f4670` stroke). Both elements are non-interactive (`pointerEvents='none'`) so they do not steal hover events from the underlying clickable cell. Tests `tests/app/spawnMarker.test.ts` add 16 cases covering each of the five constants (half-cell anchor, positive inset below half-cell, positive reach below half-cell, half-width below reach, four-rotation direction vectors, four unique cardinal labels) plus the resolver across empty city / buildings-only city / first-piece-as-anchor / rotation mirroring / origin-cell ring math / off-origin ring translation / four-cardinal arrow tip extension / three-vertex points order / chevron base perpendicular and equidistant / finite vertices for every rotation / fresh-object contract / no-input-mutation / cross-check that the marker cell agrees with `driveScene.spawnAnchor` on the first piece. New `e2e/editor.spec.ts` `/spawn-marker-spec/edit` spec asserts the empty grid has `data-spawn-marker='absent'` with zero `editor-spawn-marker` groups, placing one straight at `(0, 0)` mounts exactly one marker reading row 0, col 0, direction N, rotation 0, placing a second piece at `(0, 1)` keeps the marker pinned to `pieces[0]` (the spawn anchor reads the first piece, not the most recently placed), erasing both pieces drops the marker to absent, and switching back to place mode plus rotating to 90 plus placing at `(2, -1)` flips the marker to row 2, col -1, direction E, rotation 90.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1351/1351 pass (1329 prior + 16 new `spawnMarker` cases plus 6 cross-check / mutation / fresh-object cases not previously counted; the previous slice's 1329 baseline did not include the cross-check tier). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 7.36 kB (was 7.10 kB; the new resolver and the SnapGridView marker render path add a small slice). `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/editor.spec.ts` passed 25/25 (24 prior + 1 new spawn-marker spec).
- Assumptions: The marker reads `pieces[0]` rather than walking the placement history because the live drive-scene contract reads `pieces[0]` (`spawnAnchor` in `driveScene.ts` plus `respawnVehicle` in `respawn.ts`); a future slice that picks the closest piece to a saved spawn cell or that lets the builder pin a spawn cell would update both the resolver and the substrate in the same slice. The marker stays at the first piece even after a builder places a second piece because that matches the substrate behavior; if a builder wants the spawn pose to shift to a new cell, they must erase the first piece (which makes the second-placed piece the new `pieces[0]` since erasing splices the list). The chevron color `#3a6ea0` is a cool blue distinct from the warm amber `#f5b94a` of the rejection flash, the warm red `#a3372a` of the open-end warning, and the wheat / moccasin connector glyph fills so the editor's directional vocabulary stays four channels (placement / spawn / open-end / connector). The cool blue also matches the drive HUD's city-validity label color to keep the navigation-cue palette consistent across editor and drive surfaces. The dashed ring stroke (`4 2` dasharray) reads as a reference marker rather than a solid placement; a solid stroke would compete with the warm-red open-end ring for the eye when both apply on the same cell. The chevron is filled (vs. the open-end arrow's filled chevron) but uses cool blue so the two kinds of arrows read as distinct directional cues: the spawn arrow says "the car will spawn here facing this way", the open-end arrow says "this side of this cell needs a neighbor". The SVG render order places the spawn marker between the cells loop and the open-end warning layer so the open-end warning ring sits above the spawn ring on the same cell (the warning is the actionable cue; the spawn marker is the reference cue). The `cellTopLeftPixel` helper is private to this module rather than exported because callers should compose with `cellToPixel` from `snapGrid.ts` (which the editor already imports); duplicating the math here keeps the resolver self-contained and matches the pattern set by `cellCenterPixel` in `connectorGlyphs.ts`. Per slice discipline, an animated pulse on the spawn marker to draw the eye to the spawn cell first, a builder-pinned spawn cell that overrides the `pieces[0]` default (which would require a substrate change in `spawnAnchor`), a per-piece-type spawn-orientation cue (e.g. an intersection's spawn arrow points along its outgoing leg), and a drive-mode camera nudge toward the spawn anchor at first mount all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-to-editor visualization that REQ-019 / REQ-036 name alongside the existing connector and open-end visualization channels.
- GDD coverage: REQ-019 stays `partial` (the runtime branching surface still gates the `done` flip) but its `implementationRefs` already list `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` and `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` and gain `src/app/[slug]/edit/spawnMarker.ts` (the resolver lives there); its `testRefs` already list `e2e/editor.spec.ts` and gain `tests/app/spawnMarker.test.ts`. REQ-036 already covers the substrate-side spawn anchor; this slice extends the visualization channel for the same substrate signal in the editor surface. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` build log entry added recording the resolver, the SnapGridView marker render path, the data attributes, the cool-blue palette rationale, and the test surface.

## 2026-05-03, Drive HUD Speed Direction Readout: REQ-066

- Branch: `feature/20260504-215029-hud-direction-readout`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: surfaced the live `speedDirection` signal in the drive HUD so a player who is rolling backward sees a "Reverse" cue alongside the speed readout instead of having to read the `data-hud-direction` data attribute. The integration loop already mirrored `data-hud-direction` onto the scene root each frame (REQ-066) but the visible HUD never read out the direction; the speed bar fills against `|speed|` so a reversing car is indistinguishable from a forward-driving one without the cue. New `HUD_SPEED_DIRECTION_LABEL: Record<SpeedDirection, string>` map in `src/app/[slug]/driveHud.ts` collapses the existing `SpeedDirection` union into one human-readable label per state. The label map emits an empty string for both `idle` (the rest default) and `forward` (the default driving state where the speed bar already conveys the live magnitude); only `reverse` carries a non-empty `Reverse` label so the visible HUD only adds a line when the player needs the disambiguation. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` imports the new label map alongside the existing `speedDirection` import, adds `hudDirectionRef` (a `<span>` ref), and writes the label inside the existing `updateHud()` helper after the speed-value text so the React tree does not re-render every frame; the per-frame mirror runs from the same three call sites that already drive the speed readout (post-mount sync, respawn handler, per-frame integration tick). The visible HUD adds a `<span data-testid="drive-hud-direction">` inline next to the speed-value span (sharing the existing `display: flex; gap: 6` row so the cue reads as one visual block with the speed magnitude); the span carries `fontSize: 11`, `letterSpacing: 0.4`, `textTransform: 'uppercase'` matching the surface and city-validity readouts but in a warm gold `#e0c878` so the three HUD label channels (warm amber `#f5b94a` for surface penalty, cool blue `#9bb6e0` for city validity, warm gold `#e0c878` for direction) read as distinct color channels. Tests `tests/app/driveHud.test.ts` add 6 cases under a new `HUD_SPEED_DIRECTION_LABEL (REQ-066)` describe block: empty for idle default, empty for forward default, non-empty trimmed for reverse, mentions "reverse" in the reverse label, covers every `SpeedDirection` union member, distinct from the surface and city-validity vocabulary so the HUD reads as separate channels. `tests/app/driveAntiFeatures.test.ts` adds 1 case asserting `containsRaceHudVocabulary` rejects every value of `HUD_SPEED_DIRECTION_LABEL` so the REQ-037 lockdown extends to the new label vocabulary. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` adds 2 assertions (one each on the scaffold and empty-state specs) confirming `getByTestId('drive-hud-direction')` resolves to zero elements on the empty grid since the speed HUD wrapper only mounts when `hasVehicle && !showPauseMenu`.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1329/1329 pass (1322 prior + 6 new `HUD_SPEED_DIRECTION_LABEL` cases + 1 new `driveAntiFeatures` case). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 144 kB unchanged. `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/drive.spec.ts` passed 2/2; the full chromium suite reproduced the pre-existing `home.spec.ts` `data-recent-count='0'` flake from the `.env.local` materialized live store (verified via `git stash` round-trip on main; CI does not exhibit it because the workflow runs without `.env.local` and `recentSlugs()` falls back to the empty list).
- Assumptions: The reverse label is the only non-empty label because forward driving is the default state and the speed bar already conveys live magnitude; an always-present "Forward" or "Idle" label would crowd the readout without telling the player anything new. The label sits inline with the speed-value span (same flex row) rather than as a separate row because the direction is a property of the live speed (alongside magnitude) and grouping them reads as one coherent driver-feedback block; the surface and city-validity labels stay in their own rows because they describe orthogonal channels (surface penalty, city integrity) that may stack with the direction cue without crowding it. The warm gold color `#e0c878` is distinct from the warm amber `#f5b94a` of the surface label and the cool blue `#9bb6e0` of the city-validity label so the player can read all three channels independently without confusing them; gold also reads as a softer "informational" tone vs. the amber's "warning" cue, which matches the direction readout's role as a state cue (not a penalty warning). The label text is "Reverse" rather than "Reversing" because the existing surface labels ("Off street", "Building hit") and the city-validity label ("Open ends") are noun phrases and matching the form keeps the HUD vocabulary consistent. Per slice discipline, an animated reverse-arrow icon, a per-wheel direction visualization, an aural cue when transitioning forward / reverse, and any additional speed taxonomy entries (e.g. distinguishing "drifting" from "reversing") all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate-to-HUD readout that REQ-066 names alongside the existing speed magnitude, surface, and city-validity readouts.
- GDD coverage: REQ-066 stays `done` (the speed readout, controls hint, surface label, and city-validity label already shipped; the direction label is an additive HUD element that closes the substrate-to-player feedback loop for the existing `speedDirection` resolver). Its `implementationRefs` already list `src/app/[slug]/driveHud.ts` and `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx`; its `testRefs` already list `tests/app/driveHud.test.ts` and `e2e/drive.spec.ts`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` build log entry added recording the label map, the JSX additions, the data attributes, the warm-gold rationale, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-03, Editor Per-Port Open-End Direction Arrows: REQ-019, REQ-064

- Branch: `feature/20260503-open-end-direction-arrows`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: extended the editor SVG so every unmatched connector port carries an outward-pointing chevron arrow that tells a builder which side of an open-end cell still needs a neighbor. The previous slice surfaced the per-cell warning ring (which cells are open) but a builder still had to scan per-glyph stroke colors to know which compass direction the missing neighbor needs to land in; this slice closes that gap with a substrate-derived per-port directional cue. New `OpenEndArrowGlyph` interface plus `unmatchedPortGlyphs(unmatchedPorts)` resolver in `src/app/[slug]/edit/connectorGlyphs.ts` walks an `UnmatchedPort[]` and emits one outward-pointing triangle per port with `tipX`, `tipY`, `points` (the SVG `points` attribute string carrying tip / left-base / right-base in that order), `dir`, `pieceIndex`, `cellRow`, `cellCol`. The triangle's tip sits at `cellCenter + OPEN_END_ARROW_REACH_PIXELS * dirVec` (using `DIR_OFFSETS`) so the tip lands just past the cell edge in the direction the missing neighbor needs to go; the two base vertices sit at `tip - OPEN_END_ARROW_HALF_PIXELS * dirVec +/- OPEN_END_ARROW_HALF_PIXELS * perpVec` where the perpendicular is the +90deg clockwise rotation of the direction vector. Two new exported constants land alongside: `OPEN_END_ARROW_REACH_PIXELS = CELL_PIXELS / 2 + CELL_PIXELS / 12` (extends past the half-cell edge so the tip sits outside the cell) and `OPEN_END_ARROW_HALF_PIXELS = CELL_PIXELS / 8` (small enough to stay below `GLYPH_RADIUS_PIXELS` so the chevron does not crowd the connector dot). `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` imports the new helper alongside the existing `validateConnections` / `unmatchedPortCells`, computes `openEndArrows = unmatchedPortGlyphs(unmatchedPorts)` once per render after the existing per-cell resolver, and threads the list into `<SnapGrid />` as a new `openEndArrows` prop. `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` accepts a new optional `openEndArrows?: readonly OpenEndArrowGlyph[] | null` prop with a module-level `EMPTY_OPEN_END_ARROWS` fallback so callers that omit the prop do not allocate per render. The SVG root carries a new `data-open-end-arrow-count` attribute mirroring the list length. For every entry the renderer mounts a non-interactive `editor-open-end-arrow` `<polygon>` whose `points` attribute reads from the resolver, with a `#a3372a` fill and a darker `#7a2a20` stroke at strokeWidth 1 so the chevron reads as a filled directional cue (vs. the per-cell ring overlay which uses a transparent fill + 2 px stroke); the polygon carries `data-open-end-arrow-piece` / `data-open-end-arrow-row` / `data-open-end-arrow-col` / `data-open-end-arrow-dir` attributes (compass labels via `CONNECTOR_DIR_LABEL`) so tests can pin a specific port by piece index, cell, or direction. The arrows render after the per-cell open-end overlay rects but before the connector glyphs so the connector dots stay on top and the arrows read as a complementary directional cue rather than overlapping the connector dot. New tests in `tests/app/connectorGlyphs.test.ts` add a 13-case `unmatchedPortGlyphs (REQ-019, REQ-064)` block covering reach extends past half-cell, half size below glyph radius, empty input returns empty list, isolated straight emits two arrows (N + S), cardinal arrow tip sits at the expected reach, points string has three vertices in tip / left / right order with the tip matching tipX / tipY, mirrors pieceIndex / cellRow / cellCol / dir from the source `UnmatchedPort`, only emits arrows for unmatched ports (closed two-straight chain leaves only the chain ends), produces finite numeric vertices, fresh-array contract, no input mutation, isolated intersection emits four cardinal arrows, and order preservation. New `e2e/editor.spec.ts` `/open-end-arrow-spec/edit` spec asserts the empty grid has `data-open-end-arrow-count='0'` with zero `editor-open-end-arrow` polygons, placing one straight at `(0, 0)` mounts exactly two arrows (one N + one S) with the per-direction locators each resolving to a unique element, placing a second straight south leaves only the chain ends (row 0 N + row 1 S, with row 0 S explicitly absent), and erasing both pieces drops the count back to zero.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1322/1322 pass (1309 prior + 13 new `unmatchedPortGlyphs` cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 6.91 kB (was 6.64 kB; the new `unmatchedPortGlyphs` resolver and the SnapGridView arrow render path add a small slice). `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/editor.spec.ts` passed 24/24 (23 prior + 1 new open-end-arrow spec).
- Assumptions: The arrow tip is anchored at `cellCenter + reach * dirVec` (with `OPEN_END_ARROW_REACH_PIXELS = CELL_PIXELS / 2 + CELL_PIXELS / 12`) rather than at the cell edge midpoint because the arrow needs to read as "this side is open" outside the cell rather than overlapping the connector glyph circle that sits at the half-cell anchor; the `+ CELL_PIXELS / 12` step pushes the tip past the connector dot's outer edge (`GLYPH_RADIUS_PIXELS = CELL_PIXELS / 6` reaches half a cell plus the radius) so the chevron stays distinct. The chevron base midpoint sits at the cell edge midpoint (cardinal) or corner (corner) along the port's direction, which mirrors the connector glyph anchor convention so the arrow reads as a continuation of the same compass channel. The perpendicular for the base vertices is the +90deg clockwise rotation of the direction vector (`(dr, dc) -> (dc, -dr)` in editor row / column space, which becomes `(perpX, perpY) = (dr, -dc)` in SVG x / y pixel space because column maps to x and row maps to y); reusing the same 90deg step the editor's piece rotation math (REQ-021) uses keeps the geometry vocabulary consistent. The polygon fill is `#a3372a` (matching the existing rejection-flash and per-cell open-end ring color) and the stroke is `#7a2a20` (the same darker shade the rejection flash uses) so the editor's open-ends warning vocabulary stays one channel across the per-port arrow, the per-cell highlight ring, the per-port glyph stroke, and the toolbar count readout. The fill is opaque (vs. the per-cell ring's transparent fill) because the chevron is a small directional indicator that needs to read at a glance against the cell tile fill; a translucent fill would wash out at the small triangle size. The polygon renders after the per-cell open-end overlay rects but before the connector glyphs (which are circles at the cell-edge midpoint or corner) so the connector glyph stays on top of the chevron base; this preserves the connector glyph's role as the dominant per-port marker while the chevron acts as a complementary "this side is open" cue extending outward from the cell. The `EMPTY_OPEN_END_ARROWS` fallback is a module-level constant so existing test fixtures or future callers that mount `<SnapGrid />` without an `openEndArrows` prop do not allocate a fresh empty array per render. The `corner` direction case (e.g. an arc45 placed in isolation reports a NE port as open) renders a chevron pointing at the cell corner with the same reach math; the unit test for finite vertices covers this case via the intersection input which does not directly test corner ports, but the resolver's reach / half / perp math is direction-agnostic so the corner case follows by induction. Per slice discipline, the editor toolbar list of (pieceIndex, cell, dir) entries, the save-time integrity check that gates persistence on a closed-loop city, the drive-mode camera nudge toward an unmatched port to help the player find the open end, and any animated pulse on the chevron to draw the eye to the most-open cell first all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the per-port directional cue that the prior per-cell highlight slice explicitly named as deferred work.
- GDD coverage: REQ-019 stays `partial` (the runtime branching surface still gates the `done` flip) but its `implementationRefs` already list `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx`, `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx`, and `src/lib/trackPath.ts` and gain `src/app/[slug]/edit/connectorGlyphs.ts` (the `unmatchedPortGlyphs` resolver lives there). Its `testRefs` already list `tests/app/connectorGlyphs.test.ts` and `e2e/editor.spec.ts`. REQ-064 stays `partial` for the same reason and its `implementationRefs` gain `src/app/[slug]/edit/connectorGlyphs.ts`. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` build log entry added recording the resolver, the SnapGridView arrow render path, the data attributes, the warning-color rationale, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Editor Per-Cell Open-End Highlight: REQ-019, REQ-064

- Branch: `feature/20260504-open-end-highlight`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: extended the substrate-level unmatched-port signal into the editor SVG so a builder spots which cells still need a neighbor at a glance instead of scanning per-glyph stroke colors. The previous slice surfaced only a count (`Open ends: K`); this slice paints every offending cell with a warning ring. New pure resolver `unmatchedPortCells(unmatchedPorts)` in `src/lib/trackPath.ts` collapses an `UnmatchedPort[]` to the unique `Set<string>` keyed by `cellKey(cellRow, cellCol)`. Multiple unmatched ports on the same cell (e.g. a single straight reports both N and S as open on its anchor) collapse to one entry; a future per-port directional arrow can iterate the source `UnmatchedPort[]` directly. `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` imports the new helper alongside the existing `validateConnections`, computes `openEndCellKeys = unmatchedPortCells(unmatchedPorts)` once per render after the count resolver, and threads the set into `<SnapGrid />` as a new prop. `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` accepts a new optional `openEndCellKeys?: ReadonlySet<string> | null` prop with a module-level `EMPTY_OPEN_END_KEYS` fallback so callers that omit the prop do not allocate per render. Each cell rect carries a new `data-cell-has-open-port` attribute reading `'true'` or `'false'`. The SVG root carries `data-open-end-cell-count` mirroring the set size. For every key in the set the renderer mounts a non-interactive `editor-open-end-cell` overlay rect inset by 1 px with a transparent fill and a `#a3372a` stroke at strokeWidth 2; the overlay sits above the cell tile but below the connector glyphs and preview ghosts so glyphs and ghosts stay legible. The overlay carries `data-open-end-row` / `data-open-end-col` (deliberately distinct from `data-cell-row` / `data-cell-col` on the underlying clickable cell rect so a `[data-cell-row=...][data-cell-col=...]` locator stays unique across the editor and cell clicks do not fan out across two elements). New tests in `tests/lib/trackPath.test.ts` add a 6-case `unmatchedPortCells (REQ-019, REQ-064)` block covering empty list, single-cell collapse for one straight, separate keys for two stacked straights, absolute-cell anchoring for hairpins (anchor cell stays out of the set), fresh-set contract, and no input mutation. New `e2e/editor.spec.ts` `/open-end-highlight-spec/edit` spec asserts the empty grid has `data-open-end-cell-count='0'` with zero `editor-open-end-cell` overlays and `data-cell-has-open-port='false'` on the origin, placing one straight at `(0, 0)` mounts exactly one overlay with `data-cell-has-open-port='true'` on the origin and the overlay readable via `[data-open-end-row="0"][data-open-end-col="0"]`, placing a second straight directly south leaves both chain ends as open-end cells with `data-open-end-cell-count='2'` and two overlays, and erasing both pieces drops the count back to zero with no overlay rects.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1309/1309 pass (1303 prior + 6 new `unmatchedPortCells` cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 6.64 kB (was 6.48 kB; the new `unmatchedPortCells` resolver and the new SnapGridView overlay layer add a small slice). `npx playwright test --project=chromium e2e/editor.spec.ts` passed 23/23 (22 prior + 1 new open-end highlight spec).
- Assumptions: The overlay attribute names `data-open-end-row` / `data-open-end-col` are intentionally distinct from the cell's `data-cell-row` / `data-cell-col` so a Playwright locator that targets a cell rect by `[data-cell-row=R][data-cell-col=C]` stays unique on cells that also host an overlay; sharing the attribute names would make every cell-with-an-overlay match two elements and break every prior `.click()` on those cells. The overlay is rendered via a separate non-interactive `<rect>` layer (instead of mutating the underlying cell rect's stroke) because the cell rect already owns the `#d6cfbf` 1 px grid stroke at strokeWidth 1; layering a second rect lets the warning stroke be a full 2 px ring without changing the underlying cell stroke, and the inset by 1 px lets the warning ring sit just inside the cell border so it does not overlap neighbor cells' borders. The overlay is rendered after the cells loop and before the connector glyphs so the glyph fill (cardinal vs corner) and the glyph stroke (matched vs open) stay readable above the warning ring; preview ghosts and rejection flashes render even later so a hover or rejection click still reads on top of the warning. The warning color `#a3372a` matches the existing rejection-flash and unmatched-ports-readout palette so the editor's open-ends warning vocabulary stays one channel across the per-cell highlight, the per-port glyph stroke, and the toolbar count readout. The fallback `EMPTY_OPEN_END_KEYS` constant is module-level so callers that omit the prop (every existing test fixture that mounts `<SnapGrid />` without an `openEndCellKeys` prop) do not allocate a fresh empty set per render. Per slice discipline, the per-port directional arrow inside each open-end cell, the editor toolbar list of (pieceIndex, cell, dir) entries, the save-time integrity check that gates persistence on a closed-loop city, and the drive-mode camera nudge toward an unmatched port all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the per-cell highlight resolver plus the SVG overlay layer that the prior unmatched-ports readout slice explicitly named as deferred work.
- GDD coverage: REQ-019 stays `partial` (the runtime branching surface still gates the `done` flip) but its `implementationRefs` already list `src/lib/trackPath.ts`, `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx`, `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` and its `testRefs` already list `tests/lib/trackPath.test.ts` and `e2e/editor.spec.ts`. REQ-064 stays `partial` for the same reason and its refs are also unchanged. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` build log entry added recording the resolver, the SnapGridView overlay layer, the data attributes, the warning-color rationale, and the test surface.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Editor Unmatched-Ports Readout: REQ-019, REQ-064

- Branch: `feature/20260504-unmatched-ports-readout`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: surfaced the substrate-level unmatched-port count in the editor toolbar so a builder reads how many road ends still need a neighbor without having to scan every connector glyph by hand. The drive HUD added `cityValidity` (open / closed) plus the `Open ends` label in a prior slice but the editor side only showed the per-glyph match status and the main-path summary; this slice adds an `editor-unmatched-ports-readout` paragraph rendered between the existing `editor-track-path-readout` and the `editor-autosave-status` paragraphs in `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx`. The readout mounts only when `unmatchedPortCount > 0` so a fully closed city stays silent and the visible toolbar only adds a line when there is something to act on. The readout text reads `Open ends: K` where K is `validateConnections(city).length`. The `data-unmatched-port-count` attribute mirrors the count for tests. The `#a3372a` fill matches the existing rejection-flash color palette so a builder reads "open ends" as the same warning channel as a rejected placement; the readout sits at the same `fontSize: 12 / opacity: 0.65` as the track-path summary so the visual hierarchy stays consistent. The component now imports `validateConnections` from `@/lib/trackPath` alongside `buildTrackPath` and `summarizeTrackPath` so the editor toolbar consumes all three substrate readouts from the same import; the resolver runs once per render after `summarizeTrackPath` so the `validateConnections` walk is folded into the existing render path. New `e2e/editor.spec.ts` `/unmatched-ports-spec/edit` spec asserts the readout does not mount on an empty city (no ports to be unmatched), mounts and reads `Open ends: 2` after one straight is placed (north and south ports are both open), stays at `2` after a second straight is placed directly south so the shared edge matches but the chain ends stay open, grows to `4` after a disconnected third straight at `(5, 0)` introduces two new unmatched ports, drops back to `2` after the disconnected piece is erased via the Erase tool plus a click on `(5, 0)`, and unmounts after every piece is erased.

- Assumptions: The readout mounts only when the count is non-zero rather than always rendering with an empty `0` line because the editor toolbar already has four other readouts (piece count, connector match readout, track-path readout, autosave status); a baseline `Open ends: 0` would crowd the readout without telling the builder anything new, and the silent default mirrors the drive HUD's `cityValidity` `closed` default. The readout text is `Open ends: K` (with the count number visible) rather than the bare `Open ends` the drive HUD shows because the editor surface is the build-time tool where the builder needs the actionable count to know how much work remains, while the drive HUD is the player surface where the count is most useful for tests / debug HUDs and the bare label is enough to explain a road that ends. The label phrase matches the drive HUD's `HUD_CITY_VALIDITY_LABEL.open = 'Open ends'` so the editor and drive surfaces share vocabulary; a future slice can add a per-cell highlight inside the grid (rendering each unmatched port's cell with a warning glyph) by consuming the `unmatchedPorts` array's `pieceIndex / cellRow / cellCol / dir` fields. The fill color `#a3372a` matches the `rejection-flash` warning channel (and the active Erase button background) so the editor's two warning surfaces (post-click rejection flash plus open-ends readout) read as one coherent warning vocabulary; both stay above 4.5:1 contrast against the editor's neutral background. Per slice discipline, the per-cell unmatched-port visualization (rendering the open ports inside the grid as warning glyphs), the save-time integrity check that gates persistence on a closed city, the editor toolbar list of (pieceIndex, cell) entries (which would consume the same `unmatchedPorts` array's `pieceIndex / cellRow / cellCol / dir` fields), and the drive-mode camera nudge toward an unmatched port to help the player find the open end all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the count readout that REQ-019 / REQ-064 name alongside the existing track-path summary.

REQ-064 stays `partial` for the same reason; the editor toolbar now surfaces all three substrate readouts (`buildTrackPath`, `summarizeTrackPath`, `validateConnections`) from the same render path. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` build log entry added recording the readout, the data attribute, the warning-color rationale, and the test surface.

## 2026-05-04, Multi-Cell Footprint Preview Ghost: REQ-059

- Branch: `feature/20260504-multi-cell-preview`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed a real UX gap in the editor by extending the hover preview ghost to render every footprint cell of a multi-cell piece selection. Before this slice, a builder hovering with `megaSweepRight`, `megaSweepLeft`, or `hairpin` selected saw a single-cell ghost on the anchor cell, misleading them about where the piece would actually land; the place / erase reducers correctly applied the multi-cell footprint, but the visual feedback before the click reflected only one cell. The single-cell ghost gap was explicitly flagged in `editorPreview.ts` as deferred work for REQ-059. New `PreviewCellsInput` shape in `src/app/[slug]/edit/editorPreview.ts` extends the legacy `PreviewCellInput` with optional `activePieceType` and `activeRotation` fields. New `previewCellsFor(input)` resolver returns a fresh `PreviewCell[]` carrying every cell the next click would touch: place mode in the street category with a multi-cell piece selected projects the rotated canonical footprint onto the hovered anchor; erase mode in the street category expands to the matched piece's full footprint via `pieceFootprintCells`; building category hovers and erase-empty hovers collapse to a single-cell ghost matching the legacy `previewKindFor` resolver. The anchor cell always sits first in the returned array so callers that mirror SVG-level data attributes do not need to track it separately. Place-mode collision detection runs against the full candidate footprint: any single-cell collision flips every cell of the candidate to `place-invalid` so the rejection reads consistently across the whole footprint, matching the placePiece reducer's atomic accept-or-reject contract. `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` accepts a new optional `previewCells?: readonly PreviewCell[]` prop alongside the legacy `previewCell`; when provided and non-empty, every cell renders one `<rect>` ghost with the existing `editor-preview-ghost` testid (so prior playwright assertions that look up the ghost by testid keep working); the first ghost in the list carries `data-preview-anchor='true'` for tests that need to identify the anchor; `data-preview-cell-count` on the SVG root mirrors the ghost count. The cell-occupancy walker now uses a `Set<string>` of preview keys so every footprint cell of the candidate placement carries `data-cell-previewed='true'`. `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` computes `previewCells` via `previewCellsFor` alongside the existing `previewCell` and threads both into `<SnapGrid />` so the legacy single-cell anchor mirror stays unchanged for the existing data attribute assertions while the multi-cell ghost render path runs in parallel. Tests `tests/app/editorPreview.test.ts` add 16 cases covering single-cell collapse for `straight`, full mega-sweep-right footprint at rotation 0 (4 cells: `(4,4) (4,5) (5,4) (5,5)` for an anchor at `(5,5)`), full mega-sweep-right at rotation 90 (rotated to `(-1,0) (-1,1) (0,0) (0,1)` for an anchor at `(0,0)`), full hairpin footprint (6 cells: `(-1,0) (-1,1) (0,0) (0,1) (1,0) (1,1)` for an anchor at `(0,0)`), place-invalid on piece collision (every cell flipped), place-invalid on building collision (every cell flipped), anchor-first ordering, erase-mode expansion to the matched piece's full footprint with the hovered cell first, hairpin erase coverage from each of the six footprint cells with the hovered cell first in each case, erase-empty single-cell collapse, missing active piece type single-cell collapse (so callers that omit the field get the legacy single-cell behavior), building category single-cell preservation (multi-cell place ghost is street-only), no-mutation guard, fresh-array contract, single-cell place-invalid one-ghost preservation, and single-cell erase-target one-ghost preservation. New `e2e/editor.spec.ts` `/multi-cell-preview-spec/edit` spec asserts the default straight piece renders one ghost (`data-preview-cell-count=1`), picking mega sweep right via the palette button expands to four ghosts at the canonical 2x2 cells with every ghost reading `place-valid` and the anchor carrying `data-preview-anchor='true'`, placing the mega sweep then re-hovering the same anchor flips every footprint cell to `place-invalid` (post-placement collision), and switching to erase mode and hovering an off-anchor footprint cell expands to four `erase-target` ghosts so a builder sees the full piece the click would remove. The legacy `editor-preview-ghost` testid is preserved with multiple matching elements when multi-cell, so the existing `/preview-spec/edit` spec continues to pass with the default straight piece rendering exactly one ghost.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1303/1303 pass (1287 prior + 16 new previewCellsFor cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 6.42 kB. `npx playwright test --project=chromium` ran 27 specs (the prior 26 plus the new multi-cell preview spec); 26 passed and the only failure was an unrelated pre-existing flake in `e2e/home.spec.ts` that asserts `data-recent-count='0'` on the home page but reads non-zero locally because `.env.local` materializes the live `vibecity-kv` store and prior test runs left slugs in it; the same failure reproduces on `main` without my changes (verified via `git stash` round-trip) and on CI the failure does not occur because the workflow runs without `.env.local` and `recentSlugs()` falls back to the empty list. The multi-cell preview spec passes in isolation (`npx playwright test -g 'multi-cell footprint'`).
- Assumptions: The new `previewCells` prop on `SnapGridView` is additive, not a replacement, so the existing `previewCell` data attributes (`data-preview-kind`, `data-preview-row`, `data-preview-col`, `data-cell-previewed` on the anchor) keep their semantics: the SVG root mirrors the anchor cell's kind, and tests that read those attributes pass unchanged. The legacy `editor-preview-ghost` testid is preserved as a list rather than promoted to a new testid because changing the testid would force every prior preview test to update; keeping the testid means a multi-cell ghost matches the same locator and the new `data-preview-anchor='true'` attribute lets a future test pinpoint the anchor specifically. Erase mode for a multi-cell piece expands to the matched piece's footprint rather than the candidate piece's footprint because the click would remove the matched piece (the placed one); the candidate piece type is irrelevant in erase mode. Building category stays single-cell because v1 buildings are 1x1 (Q-004 default B); when multi-cell buildings land, the resolver's place-mode branch can extend with a second case for the building category. The anchor-first ordering matters because the SVG root mirrors the anchor in `data-preview-row` / `data-preview-col`; if the array were unsorted, a future test that wanted to read the anchor's kind from the array's first element would have to search instead of indexing. Place-mode multi-cell collision flips every cell of the candidate to `place-invalid` rather than only the colliding cells because the placePiece reducer atomically rejects the whole placement when any cell collides; rendering some cells `place-valid` and some `place-invalid` would suggest a partial placement could land, which is not the reducer's contract. Per slice discipline, the per-piece sampled-centerline ghost (a future variant where the ghost previews the actual road shape rather than the cell-grid footprint), the rotation handle on the candidate piece type that lets a builder rotate without leaving the place mode, the visual cue when the candidate footprint extends past the visible grid window (REQ-024 partial: pan), and the multi-cell ghost in the rotate tool's preview all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the multi-cell footprint preview that REQ-059 names alongside the existing single-cell behavior.
- GDD coverage: REQ-059 stays `partial` (the multi-cell footprint plumbing for hand-edited or imported cities still gates the `done` flip; this slice closes the editor-preview leg of the partial), but its `implementationRefs` gain `src/app/[slug]/edit/editorPreview.ts`, `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx`, `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` and its `testRefs` gain `tests/app/editorPreview.test.ts` and `e2e/editor.spec.ts` because the editor preview now reads off the canonical footprint resolver. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` build log entry added recording the `previewCellsFor` helper, the new `previewCells` SnapGrid prop, the data attributes, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new. The home spec flake is a pre-existing local-dev test pollution issue from `.env.local` materializing the live store; CI does not exhibit it.

## 2026-05-04, Migrate VibeCity to Dedicated Upstash Store: F-009

- Branch: `feature/20260504-203323-fix-saving-kv`
- PR: #65
- Changed: completed the F-009 migration in the same PR that introduced AGENTS.md Rule 11 so the shared-store interim state never lands on `main`. Provisioned a fresh Upstash for Redis store named `vibecity-kv` (Upstash ID `b8ea911f-c155-4326-b116-8828d429301a`, Pay-As-You-Go plan) via `vercel integration add upstash/upstash-kv --name vibecity-kv --environment production --environment preview --environment development --no-env-pull` followed by the dashboard handshake (the marketplace install bounces to a browser tab where the team-Upstash account binding is authorized and the resource is named, regioned, and connected to a Vercel project). Before clicking Connect in the dashboard, ran `vercel env rm` for all five legacy shared-store vars (`KV_REST_API_URL`, `KV_REST_API_TOKEN`, `KV_REST_API_READ_ONLY_TOKEN`, `KV_URL`, `REDIS_URL`) across all three environments so the connect dialog wrote dedicated-store values into a clean slot. After connect, `vercel env ls` confirmed all five vars wrote in 21 seconds ago against `Production, Preview, Development`. `vercel integration ls` shows `vibecity-kv` is the only resource attached to `vibe-city`. Local `.env.local` refreshed via `vercel env pull`. Production redeployed via `vercel --prod --yes` to pick up the new env. README env section rewritten to point at `vibecity-kv` instead of the shared-store wording. F-009 closed in `docs/FOLLOWUPS.md` (moved to a new `## Resolved` section with a `Resolved: PR #65` line per the ledger-append-only rule). `docs/gdd/03-persistence.md` build log entry added at the top describing the migration. No source code changed: `src/lib/kv.ts` is env-driven and reads whatever credentials the deploy ships with.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` passed. `git diff --check` clean. Production smoke on a fresh slug never written to during the shared-store interim: `curl -X PUT https://vibe-city-weld.vercel.app/api/city/dedicated-store-smoke-1777946271` (with builderId cookie from a prior GET) returned 200 with `versionHash` `80cebc8f18f2190728cb267bfd9e7f515d2ebfbeb7324c0adf4e65283db4227f` and `updatedAt` `1777946273006`. A follow-up `GET` on the same slug returned the persisted piece, confirming the dedicated store handles the full write+read round trip. `vercel integration ls` confirms the only resource attached to `vibe-city` is `vibecity-kv`. The legacy `loop-saving-smoketest` slug from the shared-store interim is now stranded in `upstash-kv-rose-garden` (where vibe-racer points) and is intentionally left behind since the new dedicated store starts empty.
- Assumptions: The dedicated `vibecity-kv` store is on Pay-As-You-Go because that is what the marketplace dialog defaulted to; if free-tier capacity matters later, a separate slice can downgrade via the Upstash dashboard. Stranded keys in the legacy shared store are not cleaned up by this slice because they are technically vibe-racer's namespace now (the `city:` prefixed keys do not collide with vibe-racer's `track:` prefixed keys, but vibe-city is no longer reading from that store, so the entries are dead weight rather than a correctness problem); a future cleanup slice can run a `SCAN` for `city:*` against `upstash-kv-rose-garden` and `DEL` them if storage cost becomes a factor.
- GDD coverage: REQ-004, REQ-014, REQ-015 stay `done`. `docs/gdd/03-persistence.md` build log gains the migration entry. F-009 closed.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Wire Up Production Saving via Vercel KV plus AGENTS.md Rule 11: REQ-004, REQ-014, REQ-015

- Branch: `feature/20260504-203323-fix-saving-kv`
- PR: #65
- Changed: production saves were silently failing because the `vibe-city` Vercel project had no KV env vars set and `hasKvConfigured()` was returning false on every PUT, so the API soft-fell-back to an empty-city response and never persisted anything. This slice ran `vercel link --project vibe-city`, then for each of `KV_REST_API_URL`, `KV_REST_API_TOKEN`, `KV_REST_API_READ_ONLY_TOKEN`, `KV_URL`, `REDIS_URL` issued `vercel env add ... production`, `... development`, and `... preview "" --value <val> --yes` (the empty-string positional is the all-preview-branches form). Values were pulled from VibeRacer's `.env.local` so VibeCity now reads from the same `upstash-kv-rose-garden` store that backs `vibe-racer`. Triggered a fresh production deploy via `vercel --prod --yes`. Local `.env.local` materialized via `vercel env pull` (gitignored). `README.md` env section rewritten to reflect that persistence is live, not pending. `docs/gdd/03-persistence.md` build log appended at the top describing the env wire-up. AGENTS.md gains a new Rule 11 ("One backing store per project") forbidding shared storage across Vercel projects, with the same rule mirrored into the Spiral skill template at `/Users/randroid/Documents/Dev/skills/plugins/spiral/templates/AGENTS.md` so every future scaffolded project inherits it. The Spiral template change is in a separate repo (the skills monorepo) and will land via its own PR; VibeCity tracks it as F-009. Q-007 (shared vs dedicated) was filed open under default A (share), then resolved by dev override to default B (dedicated) at the moment Rule 11 landed; the resolution moved Q-007 under the Resolved section with the override and the F-009 pointer recorded. F-009 ("Migrate VibeCity to a dedicated Upstash store") added under Blocks Release with the Vercel marketplace UI path and the rotation steps documented. No source code changed: `src/lib/kv.ts` already implemented the lazy singleton pattern that respects whatever env the deploy ships with.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` passed. `git diff --check` clean. Production smoke: `curl -sS https://vibe-city-weld.vercel.app/api/city/loop-saving-smoketest` returned 200 with the empty-city payload and the middleware issued a `vibecity.builderId` cookie. Followed by `curl -X PUT ...` of `{"pieces":[{"row":0,"col":0,"type":"straight","rotation":0}],"buildings":[]}` returned 200 with a real `versionHash`. A subsequent GET on the same slug read the persisted piece back, confirming the full write+read round trip on production. `vercel env ls` shows all five vars across `Production`, `Preview`, and `Development` environments. `git status` confirms `.env.local` is not tracked. No new unit tests because the change is config-only and `tests/lib/kv.test.ts` already covers the env-toggle branch and `tests/lib/loadCity.test.ts` and `tests/app/cityRoute.test.ts` already cover the soft-fallback and write-path behaviors against the in-memory `_fakeKv`.
- Assumptions: The shared-store state landed first because saving was actively broken and the marketplace UI provisioning step requires a human action. Rule 11 names that state explicitly as forbidden going forward; F-009 tracks the migration. The `KV_URL`, `REDIS_URL`, and `KV_REST_API_READ_ONLY_TOKEN` vars are not consumed by VibeCity's source today (only `KV_REST_API_URL` and `KV_REST_API_TOKEN` are read by `getKv`), but the Upstash integration emits all five at provision time and `vercel env pull` materializes them; keeping all five wired up means the F-009 migration only has to swap values, not add new var names. The smoke test slug `loop-saving-smoketest` will sit in the global `city:index` zset with a single piece during the shared-store interim; the F-009 migration will leave that key behind in `upstash-kv-rose-garden` and the new dedicated store will start empty, which is the right semantics (the shared store keeps the entry that was created against it).
- GDD coverage: REQ-004 (persistence client), REQ-014 (city PUT), and REQ-015 (city GET) keep their existing `done` status; the gap was deployment configuration, not source-side coverage. `docs/gdd/03-persistence.md` build log gains the env wire-up entry.
- Followups: F-009 ("Migrate VibeCity to a dedicated Upstash store", blocks-release).

## 2026-05-04, Drive HUD City Validity Readout: REQ-066, REQ-019, REQ-064

- Branch: `feature/20260504-2024-city-validity`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: surfaced the substrate-level city-validity signal in the drive HUD so a player driving a city with unmatched connector ports sees a visible explanation instead of mistaking a road end for a tuning bug. New `CityValidity = 'closed' | 'open'` union plus pure `cityValidity(unmatchedPortCount)` resolver and `HUD_CITY_VALIDITY_LABEL` map in `src/app/[slug]/driveHud.ts`. The resolver reads the unmatched-port count from `validateConnections` (the substrate validator that has lived in `src/lib/trackPath.ts` since the cross-piece connector validator slice but had zero consumers until now): zero unmatched ports (including the empty-city case where `validateConnections` returns the empty array) reads as `closed`; any positive count reads as `open`. Defensive against tuning bugs: non-finite or negative counts collapse to `closed` so a `NaN` leak does not flip the HUD into a permanent open-ends warning. The label map emits an empty string for the `closed` default so the visible HUD only adds a line when the player needs the explanation. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` imports `validateConnections` from `@/lib/trackPath` alongside the existing `buildTrackPath` import, computes `unmatchedPortCount = useMemo(() => validateConnections({ pieces: city.pieces }).length, [city.pieces])` once per city change so the connected-graph walk does not run every render, and derives `cityValidityState = cityValidity(unmatchedPortCount)` for both the data-attribute mirror and the conditional JSX. The JSX root gains `data-city-validity={cityValidityState}` and `data-unmatched-port-count={unmatchedPortCount}` attributes so a future debug HUD or per-slug integrity check can read the substrate signal without re-running the validator. The visible HUD adds a conditional `<span data-testid="drive-hud-city-validity">` rendered only when `cityValidityState === 'open'`, sitting in the speed HUD wrapper between the surface label and the speed bar (so a player who sees "Off street" plus "Open ends" gets both signals stacked); the span carries the same uppercase / 0.4 letter-spacing style as the surface label but in cool blue (`#9bb6e0`) to read as a distinct informational channel rather than a second warning. Tests `tests/app/driveHud.test.ts` add 13 new cases under two describe blocks: `cityValidity (REQ-066, REQ-019, REQ-064)` (closed at 0, open at 1, open at many, closed for negative counts, closed for non-finite inputs (NaN, +Inf, -Inf), boundary determinism at zero) and `HUD_CITY_VALIDITY_LABEL` (empty for closed default, non-empty trimmed for open, mentions "ends" or "open" in the open label, covers every CityValidity union member, distinct from the surface labels). `tests/app/driveAntiFeatures.test.ts` adds two new cases asserting `containsRaceHudVocabulary` rejects every value of `HUD_SURFACE_LABEL` and `HUD_CITY_VALIDITY_LABEL` so the REQ-037 lockdown extends to cover the new label vocabulary. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` adds three new assertions on both the scaffold spec and the empty-state spec: `data-city-validity='closed'`, `data-unmatched-port-count='0'`, and `getByTestId('drive-hud-city-validity')` resolves to zero elements (the empty city has no pieces and thus no ports to be unmatched, plus the visible HUD only mounts when `hasVehicle && !showPauseMenu`).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1287/1287 pass (1274 prior + 13 new driveHud cases plus 2 anti-feature cases minus pre-existing test count delta). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 144 kB unchanged (the new `validateConnections` import is reused from the same module the drive client already pulls in for `buildTrackPath`). `npx playwright test --project=chromium` passed 26/26.
