`rejectionFlashFromClick` (null for accepted clicks, returns a fresh flash carrying the click cell and the matching kind, erase-empty kind for an empty erase click, threads the prev id through `nextRejectionId` so back-to-back rejections produce distinct ids, preserves negative coords)). `e2e/editor.spec.ts` (new `/rejection-flash-spec/edit` spec that asserts the fresh page has `data-rejection-kind='none'` with zero `editor-rejection-flash` elements, places a straight at the origin and clicks the now-occupied cell to confirm the overlay mounts with `data-rejection-kind='place-occupied'` plus the click row / col, clicks the same cell again before the flash clears and confirms the rejection id changed (the keyed React node remounts so the SMIL animation restarts), toggles erase mode and clicks an empty cell to confirm the overlay updates to `data-rejection-kind='erase-empty'` on the new cell, then waits past `REJECTION_FLASH_DURATION_MS` and confirms the overlay unmounts with the SVG root's `data-rejection-kind` attribute dropping back to `'none'`).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1103/1103 pass (1075 prior + 28 new rejectionFlash cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 6.82 kB (was 6.38 kB; the new module, the wired-in EditorClient state and timeout management, the SnapGridView overlay branch, and the SMIL animate children add a small overhead). `npx playwright test --project=chromium` passed 24/24 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs + 18 editor specs including the new rejection-flash spec).
- Assumptions: The flash duration of 350 ms is short enough that a fast double-click is not held back by the prior animation but long enough that the eye registers the visual pulse against the underlying cell color; this matches a typical UI feedback animation cadence (Material Design ripple, Apple HIG standard transitions). The fill (`#a3372a`) reuses the place-invalid preview ghost color and the active Erase button background so the rejection signal reads as "the same color the editor uses for the no-go state"; the stroke (`#7a2a20`) is a darker shade so the overlay's outline is visible on top of an already-filled occupied cell. The `place-occupied` kind covers both pieces and buildings under either palette category because the place reducers enforce the same rule (a candidate placement that overlaps any existing piece or building is rejected); the test surface walks both layers across both categories so the helper does not regress if the reducers diverge in a future slice. The `erase-empty` kind covers the cross-category case where the active category does not match the cell's content (e.g. a click on a building cell while street category is active reads as erase-empty because the street erase reducer is a no-op there); this matches the editor's two-array model where the active category gates which array a click mutates. The flash uses SMIL `<animate>` children rather than CSS keyframes because SVG attribute animation works without a browser-specific @keyframes declaration and the animation runs inside the SVG namespace where the rest of the editor's overlay layers live. The id-based React key on the overlay forces a remount on every fresh rejection, which is the standard way to restart an SMIL animation; without the keyed remount the `<animate>` element would not replay on a back-to-back click on the same cell. The `setTimeout` clear is intentionally separate from the SMIL `fill="freeze"` setting because freeze keeps the overlay at zero opacity but does not unmount it; clearing the React state on the same timeout keeps the SVG free of dead overlay nodes that would interfere with future hover or click event routing in a distant future slice. The keyed React node uses the rejection `id` (a monotonic counter) rather than a row / col tuple so a click rejected at the same cell twice still remounts; using a row / col tuple as the key would make a same-cell rejection a no-op for React's reconciliation. The `nextRejectionId` wrap at `MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` is theoretical (a session would need ~9 quadrillion rejections to wrap) but defensive so the helper is total. The flash is not gated on a settings panel because it is the only post-click feedback for a no-op click; the existing preview ghost (REQ-024 partial) is the pre-click forecast. The rejection flash and the preview ghost are independent helpers because the preview ghost is per-cell and per-hover (no id needed; the React key is the row / col tuple) while the rejection flash needs an id for the React key. Per slice discipline, animations on the next-click placement (a satisfying pulse on a successful place), keyboard-only activation, and the rejection toast / sticky banner UI all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the brief in-grid visual flash that REQ-027 names.
- GDD coverage: REQ-027 flipped `partial` to `done`. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` build log entry added recording the rejectionFlash module, the SnapGridView overlay render, the EditorClient state and timeout management, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Editor Connector Match Status: REQ-019, REQ-063

- Branch: `feature/connector-match-status`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: extended the editor's connector glyph layer with cross-piece match classification so authors get visual link feedback when two adjacent pieces' ports face each other. New `ConnectorMatchStatus = 'matched' | 'open'` union ships on every glyph in `src/app/[slug]/edit/connectorGlyphs.ts`. New internal `cityPortIndex(pieces)` walks every placed piece's ports through `connectorPortsOf`, records the source piece index keyed by `cellKey:dir` (so a single cell carrying multiple ports stays distinct), and keeps the earlier-placed port on a key collision so the match is deterministic. `pieceConnectorGlyphs(piece, pieceIndex, cityPorts?)` now classifies each port: when `cityPorts` is omitted (the single-piece preview path) every glyph defaults to `open`; when provided, a port is `matched` iff the neighbor cell along the compass direction exposes a port in the opposite direction sourced from a different piece (the `neighborOwner !== pieceIndex` guard prevents an intersection from matching its own ports against itself). `cityConnectorGlyphs(pieces)` builds the city port index once and threads it through the per-piece walk so the classification is O(N) over the total port count instead of O(N^2) over all piece pairs. New `countMatchedGlyphs(glyphs)` summarizer returns the count of matched glyphs for toolbar readouts. `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` (each connector circle now carries a `data-connector-status` attribute reading `matched` or `open`; matched glyphs render with a sage-green stroke `#3f6b3f` at strokeWidth 2 while open glyphs keep the original dark-brown stroke `#5a4a2a` at strokeWidth 1; the SVG root exposes `data-connector-matched` mirroring the matched count). `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` (computes `connectorGlyphs` and `matchedConnectorCount` once per render via `cityConnectorGlyphs` + `countMatchedGlyphs`; surfaces the matched / total counter via a new `editor-connector-match-readout` paragraph that mounts only when the city has at least one connector glyph; the existing `editor-piece-count` paragraph gains `data-connector-count` and `data-connector-matched` attributes for tests; the new readout reads `Connectors matched: M of N`). `tests/app/connectorGlyphs.test.ts` (13 new cases: open default for a single-piece city; two stacked straights both report matched on the shared edge; the open ends of a two-straight stack stay open; a single straight against an empty cell reports both ports open; two adjacent straights at right angles do not match because the cardinal-vs-cardinal opposite check fails; an intersection links to a straight on each cardinal arm with the other three intersection arms staying open; a port does not match itself when the source piece has multiple ports on the same cell; two diagonals laid corner-to-corner report matched on the shared corner; rotation propagates through to the match check (two horizontal-rotated straights link E-to-W); plus three `countMatchedGlyphs` cases including an intersection wired to four straights with matched count 8). `e2e/editor.spec.ts` (new `/connector-match-spec/edit` spec asserting the empty city has `data-connector-matched='0'`, placing one straight reports zero matched with both glyphs at `data-connector-status='open'`, placing a second straight directly south flips the shared edge to `matched` while the outer ends stay `open`, the toolbar readout `editor-connector-match-readout` reads `Connectors matched: 2 of 4`, and erasing the second straight drops the matched count back to zero).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1075/1075 pass (1062 prior + 13 new connector-match cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 6.38 kB (was 6.13 kB; the new match logic, the city port index builder, the conditional toolbar readout, and the per-glyph status attribute add a small overhead). `npx playwright test --project=chromium` passed 23/23 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs + 17 editor specs including the new connector-match spec).
- Assumptions: The match logic is purely geometric: it checks whether two ports face each other across the shared cell edge / corner, with no piece-type compatibility check beyond the cardinal-vs-cardinal / corner-vs-corner classification that `portsFaceEachOther` already enforces (the per-piece-type port table in `BASE_CONNECTOR_DIRS` already encodes the legal port directions per type, so a cardinal port always faces a cardinal direction and the neighbor's matching port automatically lives on the cardinal-adjacent cell). The classification ignores piece-type compatibility (e.g. a straight's S port and an intersection's N port are matched even though the runtime path geometry might require the intersection's branch logic); this matches VibeRacer's connector-validation pattern (a port pair is geometrically valid when the directions face each other, and the runtime path module decides what the route through the junction looks like) and lets the editor visualize the same link an upstream segment-based path (REQ-064) would build. The matched glyph stroke color (`#3f6b3f`) is the same olive-green family as the building palette fill (`#6b7d4a`) so the matched marker reads as "this is good" without competing with the brown piece fill or the wheat / moccasin glyph fills; the heavier stroke width (2 vs 1) ensures matched glyphs read clearly even at MIN_ZOOM 0.5. The toolbar readout sits in its own paragraph with its own testid (`editor-connector-match-readout`) rather than appending to the existing `editor-piece-count` text so the prior playwright assertions (e.g. `toHaveText('Pieces placed: 1')`) continue to pass without rewrites; the new paragraph mounts only when at least one connector glyph exists so the empty-city editor surface stays unchanged. The cross-piece match classification is computed at every render rather than memoized because the editor renders at human pace (one click at a time) and the city's piece count is bounded by `MAX_PIECES_PER_CITY = 256`; a future render-perf slice can memoize via `useMemo` on the city reference if a benchmark identifies a cost. The single-piece port-against-itself short-circuit (`neighborOwner !== pieceIndex`) handles the case where an intersection's four ports all live on the same cell: the N port walks one cell north and looks for a S port at the neighbor; without a different piece exposing that S port, the intersection's own E / S / W ports on the source cell do not satisfy the match because they live on the source cell, not the neighbor cell. The two-piece-on-same-port-key collision branch (the city port index keeps the earlier-placed port on a key collision) is theoretical for v1 because the place reducer already prevents footprint overlap (REQ-027), but the lookup is still defensive so a hand-edited city or a future drag-import flow stays well-defined. The matched / open status does not gate placement, autosave, drive-mode rendering, or the runtime path module; the editor just paints the indicator so the author can predict link behavior before the runtime port (REQ-064 segment-based path, REQ-065 multi-locator wheel contact) lands. Per slice discipline, the runtime path branching (intersection / hairpin / arc45 / diagonal driving), the cross-piece compatibility check (a future "this port type can link to that port type" rule), the auto-rotate-to-match assist, and the unmatched-port warning HUD all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the geometric match classification and the visual / textual indicator that REQ-019 and REQ-063 name.
- GDD coverage: REQ-019 stays `partial` (runtime branching via REQ-064 / REQ-065 still pending) but its editor surface gains the cross-piece match classification per the original "predict whether two adjacent pieces will link" goal of REQ-019. REQ-063 stays `done`; the runtime consumer count grew from one (the visual glyph layer) to two (the visual glyph layer plus the cross-piece match classifier built on top of `connectorPortsOf` and `opposite`). `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` Piece palette section text updated to describe the match status, and a build log entry was added recording the connectorGlyphs module additions, the SnapGridView render, the EditorClient toolbar readout, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Editor Connector Glyphs: REQ-019, REQ-063

- Branch: `feature/connector-glyphs`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: gave each placed piece in the editor SVG a small visual connector marker so authors can read piece orientation and predict whether two adjacent pieces will link before placing the second piece. New pure module `src/app/[slug]/edit/connectorGlyphs.ts` ships `CELL_HALF_PIXELS = CELL_PIXELS / 2` (cardinal connectors anchor at the cell-edge midpoint), `GLYPH_RADIUS_PIXELS = CELL_PIXELS / 6` (a third of a cell so the cell glyph stays dominant while the connector marker still reads clearly at the default zoom), the `ConnectorGlyphKind` union (`'cardinal' | 'corner'`), the `ConnectorGlyph` shape (x / y in SVG-pixel space, dir as `Dir` 0..7, kind, pieceIndex, cellRow / cellCol so the renderer can attach a stable React key and tests can assert on absolute placements), `pieceConnectorGlyphs(piece, pieceIndex)` (pulls every port through `connectorPortsOf`, anchors at `(piece.row + port.dr, piece.col + port.dc)` cell center plus a half-cell step along `DIR_OFFSETS[port.dir]`, classifies kind via `isCardinal`), `cityConnectorGlyphs(pieces)` (walks pieces in placement order; returns a fresh array on every call so callers cannot mutate cached state), and `CONNECTOR_DIR_LABEL` (per-Dir compass label N / NE / E / SE / S / SW / W / NW). `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` calls `cityConnectorGlyphs(city.pieces)` once per render, exposes the count via a new `data-connector-count` attribute on the SVG root for tests, and renders one `<circle>` per glyph carrying `data-testid='editor-connector-glyph'`, `data-connector-piece` (the source piece index), `data-connector-dir` (compass label), `data-connector-kind` (cardinal / corner), `data-connector-row` / `data-connector-col` (footprint cell), `cx` / `cy` set from the glyph position, `r` set to `GLYPH_RADIUS_PIXELS`; cardinal connectors get a wheat fill (`#f5deb3`) and corner connectors get a moccasin fill (`#ffe4b5`) with a dark stroke (`#5a4a2a`) at strokeWidth 1; every glyph carries `pointerEvents='none'` so it does not steal hover / click events from the underlying cell. `tests/app/connectorGlyphs.test.ts` ships 23 cases covering the constants invariants (CELL_HALF_PIXELS equals `CELL_PIXELS / 2`, GLYPH_RADIUS_PIXELS positive and below half a cell, CONNECTOR_DIR_LABEL has eight unique compass labels), the single-cell straight glyph (south and north positions, cardinal kind, rotation propagates through to the dirs, source piece index, absolute cell coordinates, fresh-array contract), the intersection 4-way (four cardinal glyphs covering N / E / S / W, rotation invariance), the corner-connector pieces (arc45 mixed cardinal-and-corner pair, diagonal pair of corner glyphs at SW / NE), the multi-cell mega sweep (canonical anchor cell, cardinal kind), `cityConnectorGlyphs` (whole-city walk preserves placement order across multiple pieces, empty-city empty-array, fresh-array contract, per-piece glyph order matches `connectorPortsOf` so the south port lands before the north port for a straight at rotation 0), and the glyph position invariants (cardinal glyph y-coordinate sits exactly on a cell-edge multiple of CELL_PIXELS, corner glyph x and y sit exactly on cell corners, finite numeric x and y across a mixed-piece city), plus the ConnectorGlyph type shape lockdown. `e2e/editor.spec.ts` adds a new `/connector-spec/edit` spec that asserts an empty city has `data-connector-count='0'` and zero `editor-connector-glyph` elements, places a straight piece at the origin and confirms the count flips to 2 with one S glyph and one N glyph on piece index 0, switches to the intersection palette and places at `(0, 2)` to confirm the count flips to 6 with N / E / S / W glyphs on piece index 1, switches to the diagonal palette and places at `(2, 0)` to confirm the count flips to 8 with two corner glyphs (SW / NE) on piece index 2, then toggles erase mode and erases the straight piece to confirm the count drops to 6 (4 intersection + 2 diagonal glyphs remain).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1062/1062 pass (1039 prior + 23 new connectorGlyphs cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 6.13 kB (was 5.56 kB; the new module, the imported helpers, and the per-piece glyph render add a small overhead absorbed by the SVG layer). `npx playwright test --project=chromium` passed 22/22 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs + 16 editor specs including the new connector-glyph spec).
- Assumptions: The glyph radius of `CELL_PIXELS / 6` (about 5.3 px at the default zoom) is conservative: the cell glyph (the brown fill) stays the dominant visual element while the connector circle still reads clearly at zoom 1 and remains visible at the MIN_ZOOM 0.5 viewport. Cardinal connectors anchor at the cell-edge midpoint (a half-cell step along the compass direction) and corner connectors anchor at the cell corner; the helper uses the same `DIR_OFFSETS` table as the runtime so a future port-based validator (when REQ-064 lands) reads the same coordinate space. The wheat (`#f5deb3`) / moccasin (`#ffe4b5`) palette inherits from a warm beige family that contrasts against the brown piece fill (`#7d6b4a`) without competing with the building olive (`#6b7d4a`); the dark stroke (`#5a4a2a`) keeps the glyph legible against the cream grid background. The glyphs render as `<circle>` rather than `<polygon>` (a triangle pointing outward) because the position alone communicates direction and a circle reads cleanly at every zoom level; a future shape upgrade (e.g. a chevron or arrow) is deferred to its own slice if playtest indicates the direction-from-position convention is hard to read. The glyphs are computed at every render rather than memoized because the editor renders at human pace (one click at a time) and the city's piece count is bounded by `MAX_PIECES_PER_CITY = 256`; a future render-perf slice can memoize via `useMemo` on the city reference if a benchmark identifies a cost. The glyph React key uses the piece index plus the glyph index inside the array (`connector-{pieceIndex}-{glyphIndex}`) so adjacent pieces with the same dir do not collide on the key; a place / erase that shifts indices would re-key but the cost is negligible for v1's piece counts. The new `data-connector-count` attribute on the SVG root mirrors the total glyph count so the e2e test can assert against a single attribute rather than counting `data-testid` elements; this matches the existing `data-occupied-count` and `data-building-count` mirrors. The glyph rendering is unconditionally enabled (no settings panel, no toggle) because the slice goal is a visible direction-indicator across the editor surface; if a future playtest shows the markers cluttering the view at high piece densities, a follow-up slice can add a "Show connectors" toggle to the toolbar. Per slice discipline, the cross-piece validator (the "this piece's east port faces that piece's west port" check across two adjacent placed pieces), the connector mismatch warning (a HUD or toolbar count of unmatched ports), and the segment-based path / multi-locator wheel contact ports all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the visual glyph layer that REQ-019 / REQ-063 name.
- GDD coverage: REQ-019 stays `partial` (runtime branching via REQ-064 / REQ-065 / cross-piece validator still pending) but gains the connectorGlyphs module and SnapGridView render in `implementationRefs`, the connectorGlyphs unit test in `testRefs`, and the new e2e spec. REQ-063 stays `done` and gains its first runtime consumer in `implementationRefs` and `testRefs`. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` Piece palette section now mentions the connector glyph rendering and a build log entry was added; `docs/gdd/06-city-schema.md` build log notes that REQ-063 substrate gained its first runtime consumer.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Editor Pan / Zoom Viewport: REQ-024

- Branch: `feature/req-024-pan-zoom`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-024 by porting the SVG viewBox manipulation pattern to the editor snap-grid so authors can wheel-zoom around the cursor and middle-button or alt-left drag to pan, with a Reset View button on the toolbar that restores the default unzoomed state. New pure module `src/app/[slug]/edit/gridViewport.ts` ships `Viewport` (`{ panX, panY, zoom }` in grid-pixel units), `DEFAULT_VIEWPORT` (zero pan, zoom 1 so the unzoomed editor renders unchanged), `MIN_ZOOM = 0.5`, `MAX_ZOOM = 3`, `DEFAULT_ZOOM = 1`, `ZOOM_WHEEL_PIXELS = 100` (one wheel notch maps to a ~1.0 exponential zoom factor), `PAN_MARGIN_CELLS = 1` (the visible viewBox is clamped so it always overlaps the underlying grid by at least one cell width so a builder cannot pan completely off-screen), `clampViewport(input)` (zoom first then pan, NaN collapses to MIN_ZOOM, positive Infinity to MAX_ZOOM), `panViewport(viewport, deltaX, deltaY)` (clamped delta-pan), `zoomViewport(viewport, targetZoom, focusX, focusY)` (zoom-toward-focus that solves for the pan that keeps the focus pixel stationary; non-finite focus collapses to viewBox center), `wheelZoomViewport(viewport, wheelDeltaY, focusX, focusY)` (exponential curve so a notch zooms by the same proportional amount near MIN_ZOOM as near MAX_ZOOM; negative deltaY zooms in matching the OS image-editor convention), `viewportToViewBox(viewport)` and `viewportToViewBoxString(viewport)` (render the viewport as the SVG `viewBox` tuple / string), `screenToGridPixel(viewport, sx, sy, w, h)` (maps a screen-space offset relative to the SVG bounding rect to the underlying grid-pixel coordinate space so a wheel can anchor zoom on the cursor), `dragDeltaToPan(viewport, dx, dy, w, h)` (converts a screen-pixel drag delta to a clamped pan delta with the sign flipped so dragging the grid right shifts the viewBox left, the natural drag-the-paper convention; `-0` collapses to `0`), and `isDefaultViewport(viewport)` (true when pan and zoom are at the defaults; the Reset View button reads it for its disabled flag). `src/app/[slug]/edit/SnapGridView.tsx` accepts a new optional `viewport` prop (defaulting to `DEFAULT_VIEWPORT` so unsubscribed callers render exactly as before), passes the live viewport through `viewportToViewBoxString` into the SVG `viewBox` attribute, exposes `data-viewport-pan-x` / `data-viewport-pan-y` / `data-viewport-zoom` / `data-viewport-default` attributes for tests, accepts `onSurfaceWheel` and `onSurfacePointerDown` callbacks (with React's WheelEvent / PointerEvent types), and adds `touchAction: 'none'` to the SVG style so a pen-drag on a touch device does not also scroll the page. `src/app/[slug]/edit/EditorClient.tsx` adds a `viewport` React state seeded from `DEFAULT_VIEWPORT`, a `panDragRef` (the pointerId, last clientX / clientY, and SVG bounding rect width / height; lives in a ref so the window-level pointermove / pointerup listeners do not pay the React re-render tax on every move event), `handleSurfaceWheel` (resolves the cursor offset via `getBoundingClientRect`, maps it to a grid-pixel focus via `screenToGridPixel`, and feeds the result through `wheelZoomViewport`), `handleSurfacePointerDown` (pan gesture is middle-button or alt + left-button so the plain left-button stays reserved for the place / erase cell-click contract; seeds `panDragRef` and calls `event.preventDefault()` plus `event.stopPropagation()` so the pan does not also fire a cell-click), `handleResetViewport` (restores `DEFAULT_VIEWPORT`), and a window-level `useEffect` that wires `pointermove` / `pointerup` / `pointercancel` listeners that read each move's `clientX` / `clientY`, convert the screen-pixel delta into a grid-pixel pan delta via `dragDeltaToPan`, and feed it through `panViewport`. The toolbar gains a `Reset View` button between Redo and the Drive CTA with `data-testid="editor-reset-viewport"`, `data-viewport-default` / `data-viewport-zoom` attributes, `aria-label`, `disabled` plus `not-allowed` cursor and 0.5 opacity when the viewport is at the default; clicking it restores the default viewport. `tests/app/gridViewport.test.ts` ships 39 unit cases covering the constants invariants (default zoom 1, MIN_ZOOM below 1 and MAX_ZOOM above 1, ZOOM_WHEEL_PIXELS positive finite, PAN_MARGIN_CELLS at least 1), `clampViewport` (default round-trip, zoom clamp below MIN and above MAX, NaN to MIN and Infinity to MAX, panX clamp at the maximum and minimum, clamp range widens at higher zoom, fresh-object contract), `panViewport` (delta application, clamp, zero delta, zoom preservation), `zoomViewport` (no-op identity short-circuit, target-zoom clamp, focus-pixel-stationary invariant, non-finite-focus center fallback, post-zoom pan clamp), `wheelZoomViewport` (positive deltaY zooms out, negative zooms in, zero deltaY identity, MAX / MIN convergence under many notches), `viewportToViewBox` (default produces full-grid viewBox, zoom 2 halves dimensions, pan flows through to x / y), `viewportToViewBoxString` (space-joined four-tuple), `screenToGridPixel` (1:1 default mapping, zoom 2 halves the offset, pan offset propagates), `dragDeltaToPan` (drag right shifts pan left, zoom 2 halves the pan delta, zero drag), and `isDefaultViewport` (default true, pan-different false, zoom-different false, zoom-in then zoom-out cycle returns to default). `e2e/editor.spec.ts` ships a new `/viewport-spec/edit` spec that asserts the default viewport attributes (`data-viewport-zoom='1'`, `data-viewport-pan-x='0'`, `data-viewport-pan-y='0'`, `data-viewport-default='true'`), the Reset View button is visible and disabled at the default, dispatches a wheel-up over the grid center to zoom in (the zoom attribute changes off `1`, `data-viewport-default` flips to `'false'`, the Reset button enables), then clicks the Reset View button and confirms every viewport attribute restores to the default and the button disables again.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1039/1039 pass (1000 prior + 39 new gridViewport cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 5.56 kB (was 4.66 kB; the new gridViewport module, the wired-in pointer / wheel handlers, and the Reset View button add a small overhead). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 21/21 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs + 15 editor specs including the new pan / zoom spec).
- Assumptions: The pan gesture is gated to middle-button or alt + left-button so the plain left-button click stays unambiguously a place / erase action; this matches every desktop image-editor convention (Photoshop, Figma) so muscle memory transfers. The wheel handler does not call `preventDefault()` because the React synthetic wheel event is passive in modern browsers; on the editor page the SVG is the only large block so a typical viewport does not produce a scrollbar and a scroll-while-zooming overlap is unlikely. If a future playtest reveals page scroll competing with wheel zoom, a follow-up slice can attach a native non-passive listener via a ref. The pan clamp uses `PAN_MARGIN_CELLS = 1` so the viewBox always overlaps the grid by at least one cell width; without the clamp a fast drag could leave the screen completely empty and a builder would lose the home anchor. The clamp range widens at higher zoom because the viewBox is smaller and there is more grid to walk through; the math is `pan in [margin - viewBoxSize, GRID_PIXEL_SIZE - margin]`. The zoom uses an exponential curve via `Math.exp(-deltaY / ZOOM_WHEEL_PIXELS)` so the perceived speed is proportional, not additive; one notch from zoom 1 to 1.1 feels the same as a notch from 2 to 2.2. `MIN_ZOOM = 0.5` and `MAX_ZOOM = 3` are conservative: zoom 0.5 keeps cells at 16 px (still legible) and zoom 3 brings cells to 96 px (large enough for fine corner / connector hand placement). The zoom-toward-cursor solves for the pan that keeps the focus pixel stationary so the cursor lands on the same logical cell after a zoom; mismatched zoom-and-pan would make the editor feel disorienting on every wheel event. The `panDragRef` lives in a ref (not state) so a 60-Hz move stream does not push 60 React re-renders per second; the ref reads the last clientX / clientY directly and the viewport setState batches the visible update. The window-level pointermove / pointerup listeners are global because a fast pan drag often exits the SVG bounds, and a SVG-local listener would lose the up event there. The Reset View button is disabled when `isDefaultViewport(viewport)` returns true so the button has a clear off / on state; this mirrors the Undo / Redo toolbar buttons' disabled-when-empty convention. The default viewport state `(0, 0, 1)` exactly reproduces the pre-pan-zoom rendering so existing e2e specs that assert on grid cell positions continue to pass without changes. The `touchAction: 'none'` on the SVG style is set unconditionally; on a touch device a single-finger drag will not pan the page but will not pan the editor either (single-finger taps still place pieces because the cell click handler fires on `click`, not on a multi-touch gesture). Multi-touch pinch-zoom is deferred to a future slice; v1 ships keyboard / mouse only. The viewport is intentionally not persisted to localStorage in this slice; persistence is a separate UX decision (some authors expect a fresh page to open at a known anchor, others expect to resume where they left off). If playtest signals indicate persistence is desired, a follow-up slice can add it under the existing controls envelope. Per slice discipline, the multi-touch pinch-zoom, the persisted viewport, the keyboard pan / zoom shortcuts, and the visible zoom-percent readout all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the wheel-zoom and middle-button / alt-left drag-pan that REQ-024 names.
- GDD coverage: REQ-024 flipped `partial` to `done`. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` build log entry added recording the gridViewport module, the SnapGridView viewport prop, the EditorClient state and handlers, the Reset View button, the slider bounds and step rationale, the unit and e2e test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Drive Mode Touch Input: REQ-035

- Branch: `feature/req-035-touch-input`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-035 by porting VibeRacer's float-where-you-tap virtual joystick model and wiring it into the drive scene as a sibling input source to the keyboard handler. New pure module `src/app/[slug]/touchInput.ts` ships `JOYSTICK_RADIUS = 64` and `JOYSTICK_DEADZONE = 0.25` constants matching VibeRacer values so a cross-project player lands on the same touch feel; `JoystickState` and `JoystickVector` types; `createJoystick()` builds an inactive joystick; `beginJoystick(js, pointerId, x, y)` anchors the origin and current position; `moveJoystick(js, x, y)` updates the current position with an inactive-guard so a stale move event is ignored; `endJoystick(js)` clears the active flag and pointer id; `readJoystick(js)` returns a unit-magnitude vector clamped to `JOYSTICK_RADIUS` along the angle of travel (y is positive when the thumb moves down to match CSS pixel space); `joysticksToInput(steer, throttle, mode)` maps a pair of joystick states to a `DriveInput` snapshot under the dual-stick / single-stick contract with a per-axis deadzone applied; `mergeDriveInputs(a, b)` per-action OR helper so the integration loop can combine keyboard and touch inputs each frame without mutating either; `joystickForTouch({ mode, clientX, viewportWidth, steerActive, throttleActive })` resolves which joystick a fresh touch should anchor under the active mode (single-stick: every touch anchors steer, second touch is rejected; dual-stick: left half anchors steer, right half anchors throttle, exact center is right half, second touch on same half rejected, opposite half allowed regardless of same-half active state). `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` adds a `touchModeRef` synced with the `touchMode` React state via `useEffect` so the pointer event listeners and the integration loop read the live mode without a stale closure capture, two `JoystickState` refs (steer / throttle) seeded by `createJoystick()`, two React state mirrors `steerStickActive` / `throttleStickActive` so the visible joystick rings re-render on activate / release, and four window pointer event listeners (`pointerdown` / `pointermove` / `pointerup` / `pointercancel`) installed inside the car-mounted branch with `pointerType === 'touch'` filtering plus an `isInteractiveTarget` guard that skips taps on `button` / `input` / `textarea` / `select` / `a` so a tap on the Resume button inside the pause menu does not also start a steering joystick. The `pointerdown` handler routes the touch via `joystickForTouch`, calls `beginJoystick` on the chosen ref, fires `ensureEngineAudio()` so the first touch unlocks the engine audio rig (REQ-068) for a touch-only player, calls `event.preventDefault()` to block the browser's scroll / pinch-zoom while steering, and bumps the matching React state mirror; `pointermove` updates the current position via `moveJoystick` for the matching pointer id; `pointerup` and `pointercancel` call `endJoystick` and clear the matching React state mirror. The integration loop's `inputFromPressedKeys` call now feeds both the keyboard input and the touch input from `joysticksToInput(steerStickRef.current, throttleStickRef.current, touchModeRef.current)` through `mergeDriveInputs` so a player can hold W and tap touch at the same time and the integrator just sees throttle on. The cleanup branch removes all four pointer listeners and calls `endJoystick` on both joystick refs (captured at effect entry to satisfy the react-hooks/exhaustive-deps lint warning) so a tab switch mid-touch does not leave the throttle stuck on. New `data-touch-steer-active` and `data-touch-throttle-active` attributes ride on the scene root so a test can assert the runtime wiring without inspecting the SVG layer. New inline `TouchJoystickRing` helper component (defined in the same file because it is private to the drive scene) renders a fixed-position circle at each active joystick's origin with a smaller filled dot at the thumb position (clamped to `JOYSTICK_RADIUS` along the angle of travel). The steer ring renders only when `hasVehicle && !showPauseMenu && steerStickActive` and the throttle ring renders only when `hasVehicle && !showPauseMenu && throttleStickActive && touchMode === 'dual-stick'` so the single-stick mode never renders a phantom throttle ring; both rings carry `pointerEvents: 'none'` so they never steal the next pointer event from the underlying canvas. `tests/app/touchInput.test.ts` ships 43 unit cases covering the constants invariants (positive integer radius, fractional deadzone below 1, VibeRacer-matching values), `createJoystick` (fresh inactive shape, no aliasing across instances), `beginJoystick` (anchor origin and current position, overwrite on a second begin), `moveJoystick` (active update, inactive-guard ignores stale events), `endJoystick` (clears active and pointer id, preserves last position, idempotent), `readJoystick` (zero on inactive, zero at anchor, fractional inside radius, magnitude-1 clamp beyond radius, clamp along the angle of travel, y positive on downward thumb), `joysticksToInput` dual-stick (zero on inactive, left-thumb steers left, right-thumb steers right, throttle stick up is throttle, down is brake, deadzone collapse to no action, exact-deadzone-boundary still no action via strict inequality, combined steer + throttle, ignores throttle stick vertical when steer-only is active), `joysticksToInput` single-stick (zero on inactive, horizontal axis steers + vertical axis throttles / brakes from the same stick, down-and-right combo, ignores throttle stick entirely), `mergeDriveInputs` (empty + empty, OR-per-action, no mutation of either input, fresh-object on every call), and `joystickForTouch` (single-stick first touch anchors steer, second touch rejected, throttle-active irrelevant; dual-stick left-half steers, right-half throttles, exact center is right-half throttle, second-touch on same half rejected, opposite-half allowed regardless of same-half state). `e2e/drive.spec.ts` adds REQ-035 assertions on both the scaffold and empty-state specs: `data-touch-steer-active='false'` / `data-touch-throttle-active='false'` ride on the scene root and the `drive-touch-steer-ring` / `drive-touch-throttle-ring` testids resolve to zero elements (the playwright webServer runs without KV so the city is empty and the listener gate prevents a fresh tap from starting a joystick without a car mounted).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 1000/1000 pass (957 prior + 43 new touchInput cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 144 kB (was 142 kB; the new module, the inline ring component, and the wired-in pointer event listeners add a small overhead). `npx playwright test --project=chromium` passed 20/20 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new REQ-035 assertions + 14 editor specs).
- Assumptions: The touch runtime ships with both modes wired (dual-stick default per REQ-042, single-stick when the picker is flipped) so the persisted touch mode (REQ-042) drives a real runtime instead of remaining a UI-only setting. The pointer event listeners attach to `window` (rather than the canvas) so a touch that started outside the canvas (e.g. partially over the slug label) still routes through `joystickForTouch`; the existing keyboard listeners use the same window-attached pattern so a future hit-test surface on the canvas can branch independently. The `isInteractiveTarget` guard uses `target.closest('button, input, textarea, select, a')` to skip taps on every interactive surface that already lives on the drive scene (Edit CTA, Sound on / off button, Resume / Edit buttons inside the pause menu, the future settings panel form controls); a touch on a non-interactive surface (the canvas, the empty-state prompt, the HUD overlays) anchors a joystick. `event.preventDefault()` is called only on `pointerdown` because that is when the browser commits a scroll / pinch-zoom; subsequent `pointermove` events are not cancelled because the joystick math already handles the move shape and a cancelled move on a non-interactive surface would interfere with future interactions outside the joystick rings. The `pointerType === 'touch'` filter skips mouse and pen events because the v1 contract is touch-only; mouse-driving the joystick on a desktop is left to a future slice if the playtest gate (`docs/PLAYTEST.md` "keyboard / gamepad / touch parity") flags it as a P0 gap. The `mergeDriveInputs` per-action OR contract matches VibeRacer's "touch and keyboard share a single source of truth" model so a player who holds W on the keyboard and taps the throttle joystick simultaneously sees the integrator treat throttle as on (rather than the touch input overriding the keyboard or vice versa). The visible joystick rings render imperatively via a small private React component because the rings are pure presentational state (the joystick ref carries the live data, the React state mirror just gates the render); a future visible-thumb-tracking optimization (rAF-driven imperative DOM updates instead of React re-renders) is deferred until the playtest gate flags it. The throttle ring is hidden in `single-stick` mode so the player never sees a phantom throttle ring (the steer stick handles both axes in that mode). The `data-touch-*` attribute mirrors expose the live joystick activity as boolean strings via React's auto-stringify so the e2e suite can read the runtime without touching the SVG; the test asserts the literal string `'false'` because the listener gate prevents a fresh tap from starting a joystick without a car mounted. The first touch is also the user gesture that unlocks the engine audio rig (REQ-068) so a touch-only player still gets engine sound; the gesture is fired inside the `pointerdown` handler after `beginJoystick` so a touch that starts on an interactive surface (which short-circuits before `beginJoystick`) does not also unlock the audio rig (the keyboard handler already has its own first-gesture trigger). The cleanup branch captures the joystick refs at effect entry into local `capturedSteerStick` / `capturedThrottleStick` so the cleanup branch reads the same instance the listeners wrote to; this satisfies the react-hooks/exhaustive-deps lint warning that a ref value might change between effect-entry and cleanup-entry, even though the ref values are pure data objects (not DOM nodes) and cannot change identity in this codebase. The integration loop's `joysticksToInput` call reads `touchModeRef.current` rather than capturing the mode in the effect closure so a mode change committed in the pause menu (REQ-042 picker) takes effect immediately on resume without re-attaching the integration effect; this matches the established `pauseStateRef` / `cameraTuningRef` / `keyBindingsRef` pattern. Per slice discipline, the visible-thumb-tracking imperative update, mouse / pen support, gamepad parity (`docs/PLAYTEST.md` accessibility row), and per-axis touch sensitivity tuning all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the float-where-you-tap dual / single stick runtime that REQ-035 names.
- GDD coverage: REQ-035 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Input section expanded to cover the touch runtime alongside the keyboard handler; build log entry added.
- Followups: none new. F-008 (seed a non-empty city in playwright so visible-movement assertions land) stays open and now has the touch input runtime ready to verify end-to-end alongside the chase camera, the car movement, the building / off-street penalties, the HUD readout, the engine audio mute, and the minimap car marker once the seed mechanism lands.

## 2026-05-04, Keyboard Rebinding Settings Pane: REQ-041

- Branch: `feature/req-041-keyboard-rebinding-pane`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-041 by wiring a keyboard rebinding panel into the pause menu (REQ-039) below the existing camera tuning and touch settings panes so a player can rebind any drive action to a different key and have the choice persist across visits via the substrate that landed in REQ-043. New pure module `src/app/[slug]/keyboardSettings.ts` ships `DRIVE_ACTION_OPTIONS` (per-action `{ action, label, description }` records ordered throttle / brake / steerLeft / steerRight so go-then-turn reads naturally), `clampKeyBindings(input)` (runs `unknown` through `KeyBindingsSchema.safeParse`, falls back to `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS` on a malformed payload, returns a fresh object), `bindingsByAction(bindings)` (groups a binding map into per-action sorted code lists so two equivalent maps render in the same order), `REBINDABLE_KEY_CODES` (every alpha letter except M and R, every digit-via-Digit-prefix is intentionally excluded since the panel ships only letter / arrow / Space rebinds in v1, all four arrow codes, Space) and `RESERVED_KEY_CODES` (`Escape` / `KeyM` / `KeyR`), `isRebindableKeyCode(code)` (rejects reserved first then checks the allowlist), `keyCodeDisplayLabel(code)` (renders `KeyW` as `W`, `Digit1` as `1`, `ArrowUp` / `ArrowDown` / `ArrowLeft` / `ArrowRight` as their compass labels, `Space` verbatim, falls back to the raw code for unknown shapes), `setBinding(bindings, code, action)` (atomic replace; refuses non-rebindable codes and unknown actions; never mutates input), `clearBinding(bindings, code)` (atomic remove; never mutates input; no-op for absent codes), and `keyBindingSignature(bindings)` (deterministic `code:action,...` string sorted by code so two equivalent maps produce the same signature). New `'use client'` component `src/app/[slug]/KeyboardSettingsPanel.tsx` renders a controlled-component panel: the parent owns the live `KeyBindings` state and the panel reports each change via `onChange`; the panel renders one row per `DRIVE_ACTION_OPTIONS` entry showing the bound codes (translated via `keyCodeDisplayLabel`) plus three buttons (Rebind / Cancel while capturing, Clear). The Rebind capture attaches a window keydown listener while capturing; on the next bindable key it commits via `setBinding`, on Escape it cancels, on a non-rebindable code it stays in capture mode silently. Each row exposes testids `drive-keyboard-settings-row-<action>`, `-label`, `-codes`, `-description`, `-rebind`, `-cancel`, `-clear` so a future e2e can drive the rebind without inspecting button internals. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` adds a `keyBindings` React state seeded from `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS`, a `keyBindingsRef` synced via a separate effect so the integration loop reads the live value each frame, hydration on the existing first-mount effect that already loads the camera tuning and touch mode (the persisted `keyBindings` is fed through `clampKeyBindings`), `handleKeyBindingsChange` (clamps the patch via `clampKeyBindings`, updates state, calls `saveControls({ keyBindings })`), and `handleKeyBindingsReset` (mirrors the change handler but seeds with `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS`). The integration loop's `inputFromPressedKeys(pressedKeys)` call now passes the live ref `keyBindingsRef.current` so a rebind takes effect on the next frame; `isBoundKey(code)` reads the live ref so a rebind to a non-default key (e.g. `KeyJ`) starts capturing keydowns immediately. The pause menu JSX renders `<KeyboardSettingsPanel />` immediately after `<TouchSettingsPanel />` so the three panels stack inside the dialog with consistent visual chrome (same panel padding, font, color palette, Reset button affordance). `data-key-bindings` rides on the scene root with the `keyBindingSignature` output so a test can read the live mapping without inspecting React state. The reserved key codes (Escape / KeyM / KeyR) cannot be bound via the capture so the pause toggle (REQ-039), engine mute toggle (REQ-068), and respawn key (REQ-067) cannot collide with a player rebind. `tests/app/keyboardSettings.test.ts` ships 45 cases covering the action options invariants (one option per `DriveActionSchema` enum value, no extras, every option has a non-empty label and description, unique values, throttle / brake render before steerLeft / steerRight, race-term vocabulary lockdown so REQ-037 does not regress), `clampKeyBindings` (non-object collapse, unknown-action collapse, valid pass-through, defaults pass-through, fresh object, empty map valid, idempotent), `bindingsByAction` (default grouping, alphabetical sort, empty map, fresh object), `REBINDABLE_KEY_CODES` (contains every default-binding code so reset-then-rebind is a closed cycle, every reserved code rejected by `isRebindableKeyCode`, unique entries), `isRebindableKeyCode` (WASD accepted, arrows accepted, Esc / M / R rejected, unknown codes rejected, empty string rejected), `keyCodeDisplayLabel` (Key prefix stripped, Digit prefix stripped, arrow compass labels, Space verbatim, fallback for unknown shapes, empty input), `setBinding` (fresh add for unbound code, replace existing, no mutation of input, no-op fresh map for non-rebindable codes, no-op fresh map for unknown actions, preserves other bindings, schema-valid output), `clearBinding` (removes binding, no mutation, no-op for absent code, preserves others), and `keyBindingSignature` (sorted comma-separated, order-independent, empty map empty string, default-bindings literal match). `e2e/drive.spec.ts` adds REQ-041 assertions on both the scaffold and empty-state specs: `data-key-bindings='ArrowDown:brake,ArrowLeft:steerLeft,ArrowRight:steerRight,ArrowUp:throttle,KeyA:steerLeft,KeyD:steerRight,KeyS:brake,KeyW:throttle'` rides on the scene root and the `drive-keyboard-settings` testid resolves to zero elements (the panel only mounts inside the pause menu which itself only opens with a car mounted).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 957/957 pass (912 prior + 45 new keyboardSettings cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 142 kB (was 141 kB; the new module, the panel component, and the wired-in bindings state add a small overhead). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 20/20 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new REQ-041 assertions + 14 editor specs).
- Assumptions: The capture step uses a window-level keydown listener attached only while a row is in capture mode (rather than a focused button's onKeyDown) so a player can press the rebind button and immediately tap any key without first clicking back into the panel for focus; the pause menu already gates the integration loop's keyboard listeners so a capture press does not double-fire steering or throttle. Escape cancels the capture (rather than committing as a binding) because Escape is reserved for the pause toggle (REQ-039) and using it for cancel keeps muscle memory consistent (the existing pause behavior says "Escape backs out of the menu" and capture cancel says "Escape backs out of capture"). The reserved-key list (Escape / KeyM / KeyR) is the union of every other drive surface's reserved code; a reserved code that lands while capturing is silently ignored and the panel stays in capture mode rather than showing an error or committing the press, because a noisy "that key is reserved" hint would clutter the panel and the player can read the existing pause / mute / respawn affordances elsewhere on the surface. The Clear button per row removes every binding for an action (rather than per-code) because the v1 panel does not expose a per-code remove control; a future slice that adds an inline remove icon per code can shrink the Clear scope. Multiple codes can map to a single action (e.g. KeyW + ArrowUp both throttle); a single code maps to exactly one action and rebinding atomically replaces the prior mapping (not adds a second), matching VibeRacer's contract. The `data-key-bindings` mirror uses the deterministic `keyBindingSignature` output so a test can match the literal default string without inspecting `JSON.stringify` output (which is not order-stable across engines). The integration loop reads the bindings via `keyBindingsRef.current` (a ref) rather than capturing them in the effect's closure because the bindings can change at runtime when the player commits a rebind in the pause menu; capturing in closure would require re-running the integration effect (and tearing down / rebuilding the renderer) every time the bindings change, which would interrupt the live audio rig and reset the camera framing. The `isBoundKey` check on keydown / keyup also reads the live ref so a rebind to a non-default code (e.g. `KeyJ`) becomes a captured keydown immediately on the next press; a leftover entry in `pressedKeys` for a now-unbound code is silently ignored by the next frame's `inputFromPressedKeys` so a held W that gets unbound mid-press does not stick on. The persistence layer's `KeyBindings` shape allows an empty map (all actions unbound) so a player who clears every action on every row sees an unusable but well-defined state; the panel renders an explicit "Unbound" hint per row so the player can tell the action is unbound without inspecting devtools. Per slice discipline, the runtime touch input handler (REQ-035) and the segment-based path (REQ-064) stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the keyboard rebinding surface.
- GDD coverage: REQ-041 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/10-settings.md` Status flipped from `partial` to `done` because REQ-041 was the last open settings requirement (REQ-040 / REQ-042 / REQ-043 were already done); a build log entry was added recording the keyboardSettings module, the panel component, the DriveSceneClient wiring, the test surface, and the file list.

## 2026-05-04, 8-direction Connector Substrate: REQ-063

- Branch: `feature/req-063-connectors`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-063 by porting the 8-direction connector substrate from VibeRacer Phase 0d so REQ-064 (segment-based path) and REQ-065 (multi-locator wheel contact) can layer on top without re-deriving the primitives. New pure module `src/lib/connectors.ts` ships the `Dir` type (`0..7` clockwise from north), the `DIR_N` / `DIR_NE` / `DIR_E` / `DIR_SE` / `DIR_S` / `DIR_SW` / `DIR_W` / `DIR_NW` named constants, the `DIR_OFFSETS` table mapping each direction to a `(dr, dc)` cell-step, `opposite(d) = (d + 4) % 8`, `isCardinal(d)` / `isCorner(d)` classifiers (cardinals are even, corners are odd), the `ConnectorPort` interface carrying a footprint-cell offset and a compass direction, `connectorPortsOf(piece)` resolving the rotated ports for every piece type in the v1 taxonomy (the cardinal-only block `straight` / `left90` / `right90` / `scurve` / `scurveLeft` / `sweepRight` / `sweepLeft` / `megaSweepRight` / `megaSweepLeft` via a `BASE_CONNECTOR_DIRS` table; the cardinal-and-corner mix `arc45` from S to NE; the all-corner `diagonal` from SW to NE; the VibeCity-specific 4-way `intersection` with N / E / S / W; the multi-cell `hairpin` with two W-facing ports at footprint offsets `(-1, 0)` and `(1, 0)` matching VibeRacer's canonical 2x3 layout), `connectorsOf(piece)` returning just the directions in the same order, and `portsFaceEachOther(a, b)` enforcing the cardinal-vs-cardinal and corner-vs-corner pairing rule (a cardinal port never matches a corner port). The internal `rotatePort` helper rotates each port `(dr, dc)` clockwise via the canonical `(dc, -dr)` step and rotates the direction by `turns * 2` modulo 8 (each 90deg cardinal rotation ticks the 8-direction wheel by two stops). The `Object.is(value, -0)` collapse runs on the rotated offsets so two pieces that resolve to the same port produce equal port objects. `tests/lib/connectors.test.ts` ships 52 cases covering the Dir constants (canonical compass values, uniqueness, full 0..7 coverage), `DIR_OFFSETS` (per-direction step values, opposite-cancellation invariant), `opposite` (truth table, involution, formula equivalence), the `isCardinal` / `isCorner` partition, `connectorPortsOf` for each subgroup (cardinal-only block, arc45 cardinal-and-corner mix at every rotation, diagonal all-corner symmetry, intersection 4-way invariance under cardinal rotation, hairpin multi-cell ports with `-0` collapse on rotation), the fresh-array contract so a caller cannot mutate cached state, and the `portsFaceEachOther` truth table including cardinal-vs-corner cross rejection and self-non-match. The substrate is not yet imported by any runtime module; REQ-064 (segment-based path) is the first runtime consumer.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 912/912 pass (860 prior + 52 new connectors cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with route sizes unchanged (the new module is server-loadable but has no runtime caller in v1 yet; bundle impact lands when REQ-064 ships).
- Assumptions: The connector substrate ships ahead of the validator (the "this piece's east port faces that piece's west port" check across two adjacent placed pieces) because the validator pulls in neighbor lookups and a city-validity surface that VibeCity does not have yet; landing the validator in this slice would force REQ-064 (segment-based path) to ship as a sibling slice and the diff would balloon. The substrate is the smaller, reviewable slice; the validator lands with REQ-064. The `intersection` (REQ-019, VibeCity-specific 4-way) was added inline in `connectorPortsOf` rather than in the `BASE_CONNECTOR_DIRS` table because the table assumes a `[entry, exit]` pair and the 4-way has four ports; a special-case branch keeps the table's invariant clean and makes the intersection's symmetric port set explicit. The `hairpin` (REQ-060) port offsets `(-1, 0)` and `(1, 0)` assume the canonical 2x3 footprint that arrives with REQ-060 even though VibeCity's `defaultFootprintForPiece` currently falls through to single-cell for hairpin; the substrate names the port positions now so REQ-060 lands without reaching back to retrofit the direction module. The 90deg clockwise rotation step `(dr, dc) -> (dc, -dr)` matches VibeRacer's `rotatePorts` helper so the same connector geometry produces matching VibeRacer / VibeCity port shapes; this is the same step the editor's `rotateFootprint` already uses (snapGrid.ts) so the visual port after a rotate-tool press lines up with the visible piece glyph. The `Dir` type is a literal union `0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7` rather than an enum because TypeScript's `enum` would force a value re-export and a literal-narrowing cast at every call site; the union plus named constants gives the same readability without the export drift. The `BASE_CONNECTOR_DIRS` table is keyed by `Exclude<PieceType, 'hairpin' | 'intersection'>` so the type system enforces that every member of the cardinal-only block has a base entry and every special-cased piece is excluded from the table; adding a new cardinal piece type to `PieceTypeSchema` would surface as a TypeScript error in `BASE_CONNECTOR_DIRS` until the new entry lands. The `Pick<Piece, 'type' | 'rotation'>` parameter type on `connectorPortsOf` and `connectorsOf` accepts a piece-shaped object without forcing the caller to also have valid `row` / `col` integers; future call sites (palette glyphs, validator) frequently pass a partial piece. The `portsFaceEachOther` helper takes raw `Dir` values rather than `ConnectorPort` objects because the validator across two adjacent placed pieces does the absolute-cell match by adding the piece anchor and the port offset; the direction-pair check is a separate concern. Per slice discipline, REQ-064 (segment-based path), REQ-065 (multi-locator wheel contact), and the cross-piece validator stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the substrate.
- GDD coverage: REQ-063 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/06-city-schema.md` Status remains `partial` (REQ-064 still not started); a build log entry was added recording the connectors module, the test surface, the file list, and the deferred-validator scope decision.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Touch Mode Picker Settings Pane: REQ-042

- Branch: `feature/req-042-touch-mode-picker`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-042 by wiring a touch mode picker into the pause menu (REQ-039) below the existing camera tuning panel (REQ-040) so a player can pick a touch input layout (`dual-stick` or `single-stick`) and have the choice persist across visits via the substrate that landed in REQ-043. New pure module `src/app/[slug]/touchSettings.ts` ships `TOUCH_MODE_OPTIONS` (a readonly array of `{ value, label, description }` records: dual-stick first because it is the default and the more capable layout, single-stick second), `clampTouchMode(input)` that runs an unknown input through `TouchModeSchema.safeParse` and falls back to `DEFAULT_TOUCH_MODE` on any non-string or non-enum input, plus re-exports for `DEFAULT_TOUCH_MODE` and `TouchMode`. New client component `src/app/[slug]/TouchSettingsPanel.tsx` renders a controlled-component panel: the parent owns the live `TouchMode` state and reports each change via `onChange`; the panel renders a `role='radiogroup'` with a `role='radio'` button per option (so a screen reader announces the picker as a single set), each button mirrors `aria-checked` and `data-selected` so the live mode is observable from a test, and the Reset button calls `onReset` which the parent maps to `DEFAULT_TOUCH_MODE`; testids `drive-touch-settings`, `drive-touch-settings-reset`, `drive-touch-settings-options`, `drive-touch-settings-option-<value>`, `drive-touch-settings-option-<value>-label`, `drive-touch-settings-option-<value>-description` so a future e2e can drive the picker without inspecting the radio internals. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` adds a `touchMode` React state seeded from `DEFAULT_TOUCH_MODE` (the server render uses defaults; the existing first-mount `useEffect` that hydrates `cameraTuning` now also calls `setTouchMode(clampTouchMode(persisted.touchMode))` so a returning player sees their persisted choice on the first paint), `handleTouchModeChange` (clamps the patch via `clampTouchMode`, updates state, calls `saveControls({ touchMode })`) and `handleTouchModeReset` (mirrors the change handler but seeds with `DEFAULT_TOUCH_MODE`). The pause menu JSX renders `<TouchSettingsPanel />` immediately after `<CameraSettingsPanel />` so the two settings panels stack inside the dialog with consistent visual chrome (same panel padding, font, color palette, Reset button affordance). `data-touch-mode` rides on the scene root so a test can read the live mode without inspecting React state. The runtime touch input handler (REQ-035) stays deferred to its own slice; the picker only persists the choice in this slice and the runtime will read `loadControls().touchMode` once REQ-035 lands. `tests/app/touchSettings.test.ts` ships 10 cases covering the option-list invariants (an option exists for every `TouchModeSchema` enum value, no extra options, every option has a non-empty label and description, option values are unique, the default option is the first entry so the picker reads the default-first ordering, race-term vocabulary lockdown so REQ-037 does not regress through a label or description), `clampTouchMode` (non-string inputs collapse to the default, unknown string values collapse to the default, every valid mode passes through verbatim, idempotent on every valid mode), and the `DEFAULT_TOUCH_MODE` re-export shape. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` adds REQ-042 assertions on both the scaffold and empty-state specs: `data-touch-mode='dual-stick'` rides on the scene root and the `drive-touch-settings` testid resolves to zero elements (the panel only mounts inside the pause menu which itself only opens with a car mounted).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 860/860 pass (850 prior + 10 new touchSettings cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS unchanged at 141 kB (the new pure module and the panel component add only a small overhead absorbed by tree-shaking). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 20/20 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new REQ-042 assertions + 14 editor specs).
- Assumptions: The picker uses a button-as-radio pattern rather than native `<input type='radio'>` because the panel needs to render a label and description per option without splitting the click target across two elements; a button with `role='radio'` and `aria-checked` keeps the screen reader announcement correct while letting the entire option box be tappable. Dual-stick is the first option in `TOUCH_MODE_OPTIONS` (matching `DEFAULT_TOUCH_MODE`) so the picker reads default-first; a future rebalance that flips the default also updates the option order to keep the contract. The Reset button restores `DEFAULT_TOUCH_MODE` and persists it via `saveControls({ touchMode: DEFAULT_TOUCH_MODE })` rather than `clearControls()` so a future-persisted `camera` (REQ-040) or `keyBindings` (REQ-041) is not collateral damage; the touch-only reset is the slice-discipline-correct surface, mirroring the camera reset's contract. The panel sits below `<CameraSettingsPanel />` inside the pause menu rather than as a separate route or a fly-in because the pause menu already owns the click surface and a second nested overlay would compete with the menu's z-index; the two panels share the same panel padding / font / color palette so they read as a single settings drawer. The `clampTouchMode` helper accepts `unknown` (rather than `string`) because the panel onChange only fires with a `TouchMode` value but the same helper is used to defensively clamp the persisted payload on hydrate; an unknown shape (e.g. a forward-compatibility migration from a future schema) collapses to the default rather than throwing. The runtime touch input handler (REQ-035) is intentionally NOT wired in this slice; the picker only persists the choice and the runtime layer is a separate slice with its own touch event surface and gesture state machine. The `data-touch-mode` mirror exposes the live mode as a string attribute via React's auto-stringify so the e2e suite can read the persisted state without touching React state; the test asserts the literal string `'dual-stick'` so a future default change would surface as a test failure asserting the new default. Per slice discipline, REQ-041 (keyboard rebinding) stays deferred to its own slice; this slice ships only the touch mode picker surface.
- GDD coverage: REQ-042 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/10-settings.md` Status remains `partial` (REQ-041 still not started); a build log entry was added recording the touchSettings module, the panel component, the DriveSceneClient wiring, the test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Camera Tuning Settings Pane: REQ-040

- Branch: `feature/req-040-camera-tuning-pane`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-040 by wiring a camera tuning settings pane into the pause menu (REQ-039) so a player can adjust the chase rig (height, distance, lookAhead, followSpeed, fov) live without leaving the drive surface and have the tuning persist across visits via the substrate that landed in REQ-043. New pure module `src/app/[slug]/cameraSettings.ts` ships `CAMERA_SLIDER_BOUNDS` (per-field min / max / step / label: height 1..20 step 0.2, distance 4..30 step 0.2, lookAhead 0..16 step 0.2, followSpeed 0.02..1 step 0.01, fov 30..120 step 1), `snapToSliderStep(value, bound)` (clamps below min and above max, snaps in-range to the nearest step, non-finite collapses to min, decimals factor rounds out floating noise so the panel readout displays cleanly), `clampCameraTuning(input)` (every field through `snapToSliderStep`, returns a fresh object), `toCameraRigParams(tuning)` (bridges the persisted `CameraTuning` shape to the chase rig's `CameraRigParams` by passing through height / distance / lookAhead, mapping followSpeed to positionLerp, and filling targetHeight / targetLerp from the cameraRig defaults so the rig contract stays satisfied while the persistence shape stays minimal), and `CAMERA_SETTINGS_FIXED_RIG_FIELDS` exposing the cameraRig defaults for tests. New client component `src/app/[slug]/CameraSettingsPanel.tsx` renders a controlled-component panel with five sliders plus a Reset button: the parent owns the `CameraTuning` state and reports each change via `onChange`; the Reset button calls `onReset` which the parent maps to `DEFAULT_CAMERA_TUNING`; testids `drive-camera-settings`, `drive-camera-settings-reset`, `drive-camera-settings-<field>`, `drive-camera-settings-<field>-slider`, `drive-camera-settings-<field>-value` so future e2e specs can drive the sliders without inspecting the SVG / range-input internals. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` adds an `cameraTuning` React state seeded from `DEFAULT_CAMERA_TUNING` (the server render uses defaults; a `useEffect` hydrates from `loadControls()` on first client mount and feeds the result through `clampCameraTuning` so a stale persisted payload cannot poke an out-of-range value into the rig), a `cameraTuningRef` synced via a separate effect so the integration loop reads the live value each frame without re-attaching, a `perspectiveCameraRef` published to the camera object the integration effect creates so the panel's onChange branch can update the FOV imperatively without tearing down and rebuilding the scene, `handleCameraTuningChange` (clamps the patch, updates state, calls `saveControls({ camera })`, and pokes `camera.fov` plus `updateProjectionMatrix()` when the fov field changes), and `handleCameraTuningReset` (mirrors the change handler but seeds with `DEFAULT_CAMERA_TUNING`). The chase rig calls (`createCameraRig` on first mount, `createCameraRig` on respawn snap, `updateCameraRig` per frame) all read the live params via `toCameraRigParams(cameraTuningRef.current)` so a slider drag while paused updates the rig params on resume. The perspective camera's fov is initialized from `cameraTuningRef.current.fov` instead of the static `CAMERA_FOV` constant so a returning player whose tuning differs from the defaults sees the right framing on the first rendered frame; the `CAMERA_FOV` import was dropped (the constant in `driveScene.ts` is preserved for the unit-test surface). The pause menu JSX renders `<CameraSettingsPanel />` below the existing Press Esc to resume hint so the panel sits inside the dialog and inherits the menu's z-index; the pre-existing Resume / Edit buttons stay above it. `data-camera-height` / `data-camera-distance` / `data-camera-look-ahead` / `data-camera-follow-speed` / `data-camera-fov` ride on the scene root so a test can read the live tuning without inspecting React state. The integration effect's cleanup branch nulls `perspectiveCameraRef.current` so a stale slider change after unmount cannot poke the disposed projection matrix. `tests/app/cameraSettings.test.ts` ships 22 cases covering the bounds invariants (every CameraTuning field has a bound; every bound has min < max and a positive finite step at most max - min; every default value sits inside the corresponding bound; race-term vocabulary lockdown so REQ-037 does not regress through a slider label), `snapToSliderStep` (clamp below min and above max, in-range snap, non-finite returns min, on-step input preserved, sub-unit float-noise-free for the followSpeed bound, integer-step rounds for fov), `clampCameraTuning` (default round-trip, below-min and above-max blanket clamp, non-finite blanket collapse, fresh object contract), `toCameraRigParams` (tunable fields pass through, fixed fields from cameraRig defaults, `CAMERA_SETTINGS_FIXED_RIG_FIELDS` matches the bridge, default bridge equivalence with `CAMERA_RIG_POSITION_LERP`, fresh object contract), and the `DEFAULT_CAMERA_TUNING` re-export shape. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` adds REQ-040 assertions on both the scaffold and empty-state specs: the `data-camera-*` mirrors carry the persisted defaults (`6.4` / `14` / `6` / `0.12` / `60`) and the `drive-camera-settings` testid resolves to zero elements (the panel only mounts inside the pause menu which itself only opens with a car mounted).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `git diff --check` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 850/850 pass (828 prior + 22 new cameraSettings cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 141 kB (was 140 kB; the new module, the panel, and the wired-in tuning state add a small overhead). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 20/20 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new REQ-040 assertions + 14 editor specs).
- Assumptions: The slider step for height / distance / lookAhead is 0.2 world units rather than 0.5 because the v1 default values (6.4 / 14 / 6) must sit exactly on a slider step; with step 0.5 the height default 6.4 would clamp to 6.5 on a defaults-round-trip and the test would have to encode the snap. 0.2 keeps the step feel responsive (40 stops per unit) while letting the persisted defaults survive a round-trip through `clampCameraTuning`. The followSpeed step is 0.01 because the perceptual range between 0.05 (laggy) and 0.5 (snappy) covers most of the visually distinct presets; 100 stops in that range is enough resolution. The fov step is 1 degree because a sub-degree fov change is below the perceptual threshold for a 60 Hz refresh chase camera. The slider bounds (height 1..20, distance 4..30, lookAhead 0..16, followSpeed 0.02..1, fov 30..120) are conservative: a slider at min produces a usable view (camera close to the car, narrow fov, slow follow) and a slider at max produces an extreme but still-functional view; values outside these bounds are clamped on save so a future rebalance that loosens the bounds does not break a previously-persisted tuning. The panel is a controlled component (parent owns the state) rather than uncontrolled with internal state because the integration loop must read the live tuning through `cameraTuningRef` and a single source of truth keeps the slider readout, the persisted payload, the data attributes, and the rig parameters in sync without any debounce. The Reset button restores `DEFAULT_CAMERA_TUNING` and persists it via `saveControls({ camera: DEFAULT_CAMERA_TUNING })` rather than calling `clearControls()` because clearing would also wipe a future-persisted `keyBindings` (REQ-041) or `touchMode` (REQ-042); the camera-only reset is the slice-discipline-correct surface. The panel renders inside the pause menu (REQ-039) rather than as a separate fly-in or a dedicated route because the pause menu already owns the click surface, the player is already stopped, and a second nested overlay would compete with the menu's z-index. The panel sits below the existing Press Esc to resume hint so the existing dialog ordering (slug, Resume / Edit buttons, hint, panel) reads as a settings drawer. The fov change is the only slider that requires a live three.js poke (`camera.fov = ...; camera.updateProjectionMatrix()`) because height / distance / lookAhead / followSpeed all flow through the chase rig's `CameraRigParams` and the rig reads them each frame; the fov sits on the perspective camera object directly and a frame-time read of `cameraTuningRef.current.fov` would not change anything until the projection matrix is rebuilt. The orbit camera (used on the empty grid) inherits the live fov via the same code path so an empty-grid pause-menu fov change is also visible immediately on resume. The perspective camera's fov is initialized from `cameraTuningRef.current.fov` instead of the static `CAMERA_FOV = 50` constant so a returning player whose tuning differs sees the right framing on the first rendered frame; the unit-test surface still asserts the constant equals 50 because that constant is preserved (the value is just no longer the source of truth for the live camera). The integration effect's cleanup branch nulls `perspectiveCameraRef.current` so a stale slider change after the scene unmounts (e.g. the player navigates to the editor before the panel onChange fires) cannot poke the disposed projection matrix; the Mute toggle pattern from REQ-068 establishes the precedent. The data attribute mirrors expose the live tuning numbers as decimal strings via React's auto-stringify; the tests assert specific values (`'6.4'` / `'14'` / `'0.12'`) so a future change to the default tuning would surface as a test failure asserting the new value. Per slice discipline, REQ-041 (keyboard rebinding) and REQ-042 (touch mode picker) stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the camera-tuning surface. The panel's testid vocabulary follows the established `drive-<feature>` prefix so the existing testid hierarchy stays consistent.
- GDD coverage: REQ-040 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/10-settings.md` Status remains `partial` (REQ-041 / REQ-042 still not started); a build log entry was added recording the cameraSettings module, the panel component, the DriveSceneClient wiring, the slider bounds and step rationale, the unit and e2e test surface, and the file list.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Mega Sweep Palette and Type-Driven Footprint Default: REQ-058

- Branch: `feature/req-058-mega-sweep-palette`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-058 by extending the editor street palette with the `megaSweepRight` and `megaSweepLeft` long-radius turns and by porting the type-driven default footprint resolver so the four-cell mega sweep footprint flows through every existing reducer (placement overlap, atomic erase, drive scene ground quads, off-street penalty) without recording an explicit `footprint` field on the persisted piece. New exports in `src/app/[slug]/edit/snapGrid.ts`: `MEGA_SWEEP_RIGHT_FOOTPRINT` (the canonical 2x2 block anchored at the bottom-right cell, ported from VibeRacer's `MEGA_SWEEP_RIGHT_FOOTPRINT` in `trackFootprint.ts`), `MEGA_SWEEP_LEFT_FOOTPRINT` (mirror across the column axis, anchored at the bottom-left cell), and `defaultFootprintForPiece(piece)` that resolves a piece's canonical footprint from its type plus rotation (rotates the 2x2 block clockwise via `(dr, dc) -> (dc, -dr)` per 90deg step and collapses `-0` to `0` so two pieces that resolve to the same footprint produce equal cell objects). `pieceFootprintCells` now falls back to `defaultFootprintForPiece(piece)` when the piece has no explicit `footprint` field, so every existing helper (`occupiedPieceCells`, `placePiece` candidate-cell occupancy check, `erasePiece` footprint match, `editorPreview` overlap detection) inherits the multi-cell behavior automatically. `src/app/[slug]/driveScene.ts` mirrors the same constants and the same resolver locally (matches the established duplication pattern that keeps the drive scene module free of the editor dependency tree) and `pieceFootprintWorldCells` now reads the type-driven default so a placed mega sweep renders all four ground quads instead of leaving three visible holes through the placed piece.
