`data-preview-col` mirroring the hovered coords and the cell carrying `data-cell-previewed='true'`), places a piece and re-hovers to assert the same cell now reads place-invalid, toggles erase mode and re-hovers the occupied cell to assert erase-target, hovers an empty cell while still in erase mode to assert erase-empty, switches back to place mode and over to the building category to confirm an empty cell stays place-valid and the previously-placed piece cell reads place-invalid (proving the cross-layer rejection rule), and finally moves the mouse out of the grid to confirm the ghost disappears. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` Tools section updated to record the v1 hover preview half of REQ-024; build log entry added.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 551/551 pass (530 prior + 21 new editorPreview cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]/edit` route-specific JS at 4.22 kB (was 4.07 kB; the preview module and the ghost rect add a small overhead, no new dependency). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 18/18 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive + 12 editor specs including the new hover preview spec). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: The hover preview is the v1 half of REQ-024 (pan and zoom still deferred). Pan / zoom is a separate UX surface (camera controls over the grid); the ghost piece is the visual feedback for placement decisions. Both share the snap-grid render but neither depends on the other. The preview kinds map directly onto the place / erase reducer accept-or-reject decision so the ghost can never disagree with the post-click city; this keeps the "what you see is what you get" contract honest. Place mode rejects on either piece OR building occupancy across both palette categories because the placeBuilding reducer rejects on the same union; the ghost mirrors that rule even in street + place mode (an existing building blocks both a street piece and another building) so the visual feedback never diverges from the reducer. Erase mode is category-scoped: erasing in street mode targets only pieces, in building mode only buildings; the ghost reads erase-empty when the hovered cell holds content the active category cannot erase so the author understands the click would be a no-op without trying it. The ghost overlay renders with `pointerEvents='none'` so it never steals hover events from the underlying clickable cell; without this the mouse-leave fires on the cell and mouse-enter fires on the ghost on the same move, causing the ghost to flicker in and out. The leave handler clears the hover state only when the leave event matches the active hover so a fast pointer move that fires leave-then-enter out of order does not race-condition the state to null. The fill / stroke / opacity tables are pure data exports keyed on the union so a future settings pane that exposes accessibility colors (high-contrast mode) can swap the table without touching the helper or the SVG. The translucent ghost (fill opacity around 0.45 for active kinds, 0.25 for erase-empty) sits over the existing cell color so a hovered occupied cell still shows the underlying piece / building fill through the ghost; this keeps the cell identification clear even while the ghost reads out the next-click decision. Multi-cell footprint visualization for a placement ghost (where the ghost would span every cell the candidate piece would occupy, not just the anchor cell) is a future enhancement gated on the multi-cell footprint plumbing (REQ-059) landing in the editor; v1's palette ships single-cell pieces so a per-cell ghost covers every current case. The `data-preview-kind` / `data-preview-row` / `data-preview-col` attributes on the SVG root are the precise discriminator for tests that want to assert the live preview state without measuring the ghost's pixel position; the ghost element itself also carries the same attributes so a test that locates the ghost can assert both shapes match. Per slice discipline, the pan / zoom half of REQ-024 stays deferred to its own slice; this slice ships only the hover preview overlay and the helper module that drives it.
- GDD coverage: REQ-024 flipped `not_started` to `partial` (hover preview ghost piece ships; pan and zoom themselves still deferred). `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` Tools section updated and a build log entry added.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Off-Street Penalty: REQ-054

- Branch: `feature/req-054-off-street-penalty`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-054 by adding a cell-level binary off-street penalty that mirrors the building-cell collision (REQ-030, Q-005 default A) so a player who veers off the placed streets feels an immediate slowdown instead of cruising over the empty grid. `src/app/[slug]/offStreetPenalty.ts` ships a pure module with `OFF_STREET_PENALTY_MAX_SPEED = CELL_SIZE * 3`, `OFF_STREET_PENALTY_MAX_REVERSE_SPEED = CELL_SIZE * 1.5`, and `OFF_STREET_PENALTY_DRAG = CELL_SIZE * 8` tuning constants (forward cap is more permissive than the building cap of `CELL_SIZE * 2` so off-road feels lighter than a building hit; drag is lighter so the bleed is more forgiving); a private `cellKey(row, col)` helper matching the existing `buildingCollision.ts` and `edit/snapGrid.ts` key format; `streetCellSet(pieces)` that builds a `Set<string>` covering every cell of every piece footprint by calling `pieceFootprintCells` from `edit/snapGrid.ts` so multi-cell pieces (mega sweep, hairpin, future arc45 / diagonal once REQ-059 lands) expand correctly; `isOnStreetCell(x, z, set)` short-circuits on an empty set so a defensive-empty city reports every position as off-street; `applyOffStreetPenalty(state, onStreetCell, dt)` returns the input state unchanged when on-street (so the on-street path is allocation-free) or for a non-positive / non-finite dt, otherwise drags the speed magnitude toward zero by `OFF_STREET_PENALTY_DRAG * dt` (with sign-aware clamping that snaps a small residual to exactly zero) and clamps the speed to the off-street range. The `worldToCell` helper from `buildingCollision.ts` is reused (single source of truth for the world-to-cell conversion). `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` memoizes the street cell set via `useMemo` keyed on `city.pieces`, calls `applyOffStreetPenalty(vehicle, onStreet, dt)` immediately after `applyDriveStep` and BEFORE the building-cell penalty inside the per-frame integration loop (so the building cap stacks on top and wins when both apply), mirrors the live off-street flag onto the scene root as `data-off-street='true' | 'false'`, and adds the new street-cell set to the effect's dependency array so the cache invalidates correctly when pieces change. `tests/app/offStreetPenalty.test.ts` ships 27 cases covering the constants invariants (positive, finite, scale with `CELL_SIZE`, less than the on-street caps), `streetCellSet` (empty / single-cell / many / multi-cell footprint expansion / fresh-set), `isOnStreetCell` (empty-set short-circuit / hit / miss / off-center within bounds / next-cell over / multi-cell footprint coverage), and `applyOffStreetPenalty` (pass-through on-street, pass-through for non-positive / NaN dt, forward cap from `MAX_SPEED`, reverse cap from `MAX_REVERSE_SPEED`, forward drag below cap, reverse drag below cap, small-positive snap to zero, small-negative snap to zero, position immutability, full no-mutation, single-frame sustained-throttle cap, off-street cap more permissive than building cap). `e2e/drive.spec.ts` asserts `data-off-street='false'` on both the scaffold and empty-state specs (the playwright webServer runs without KV so `loadCity` returns the empty city; the per-frame check is gated on the car being mounted so the flag never flips on an empty grid; unit tests cover the populated case in full). `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` gained an Off-street penalty section and a build log entry; the Out-of-scope list dropped REQ-054.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 530/530 pass (503 prior + 27 new offStreetPenalty cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS unchanged at 137 kB (the new module is small and the per-frame call adds negligible overhead). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 17/17 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new `data-off-street` assertions + 11 editor specs). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: Cell-level binary off-street penalty mirrors the building-cell penalty (Q-005 default A model) because both are penalties for "the car center is on a cell where it should not be" and the simplest consistent rule lets a player understand the bleed without a separate vocabulary. The off-street cap (`CELL_SIZE * 3`, three-eighths of `MAX_SPEED = CELL_SIZE * 8`) is more permissive than the building cap (`CELL_SIZE * 2`) so a player who drifted off the road during a turn feels a clear penalty without losing as much speed as one who clipped a building; if both apply (the car center is on a building cell, which is also off-street) the building penalty stacks on top and wins because it caps tighter. Reverse cap is half the forward cap so a player who reversed off the road cannot rocket backward across the grid; matches the building-cell convention. `OFF_STREET_PENALTY_DRAG = CELL_SIZE * 8` is lighter than the building drag (`CELL_SIZE * 12`) so the bleed feels firm but more forgiving than a building hit; this is a tuning knob a future playtest can move. The "car cell" is derived from the vehicle's center position via `worldToCell` (nearest-cell rounding) rather than a per-wheel sample because v1 has no wheel contact module yet (REQ-032). Per-wheel sampling lands when REQ-032 ships and the four wheel-cell lookups collapse into the same cell-key Set this module already builds. The penalty is applied immediately after `applyDriveStep` and BEFORE the building-cell penalty so the integrator's normal coast / throttle / brake / steering math runs first, then the off-street cap clamps the result, then the building cap clamps tighter if both apply; this preserves the integrator's `applyDriveStep` test surface unchanged and gives the building penalty the final word when stacking. The lookup short-circuits on an empty cell set so a defensive-empty city pays nothing per frame; the early return is the cheapest version of the on-street allocation-free path; in practice the integration loop is gated on `city.pieces.length > 0` so the empty-set case is just defensive. The `data-off-street` attribute is the precise discriminator for tests that want to assert the penalty engages without sampling speed history; the e2e specs cover the empty-state default of `'false'` (the playwright webServer is unconfigured KV so the city is empty and no car is mounted). The shared `pieceFootprintCells` helper imported from `edit/snapGrid.ts` is a pure utility on `Piece` that both editor and drive consume; the cross-tree import is justified by single-source-of-truth on footprint expansion (a future slice that moves it to a shared `lib/` module is a separate refactor). Per slice discipline, REQ-032 (wheel contact port that refines the cell check to a per-wheel evaluation), REQ-040 onward (settings pane that lets the off-street caps and drag be tuned), and REQ-035 (touch input) all stay deferred to their own slices.
- GDD coverage: REQ-054 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Off-street penalty section added with a build log entry; the Out-of-scope list dropped REQ-054.
- Followups: none new. F-008 (seed a non-empty city in playwright so visible-movement assertions land) stays open and now has the off-street penalty ready to verify alongside the chase camera, the car movement, and the building-cell penalty once the seed mechanism lands.

## 2026-05-04, Drive HUD Speed Readout and Controls Hint: REQ-066

- Branch: `feature/req-066-drive-hud`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: gave the drive view its first non-pause overlay so a player can read live driving feedback and a first-time visitor can see which keys move the car. `src/app/[slug]/driveHud.ts` ships a pure module with `HUD_CONTROLS_HINT_LINES` (a five-line table covering throttle / steer left / brake / steer right / pause that mirrors the bindings in `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS` from `driveControls.ts`), `HUD_SPEED_LABEL = 'Speed'`, `HUD_SPEED_UNIT = 'wu/s'` (world units per second; the v1 number is unitless because the integrator is not calibrated to km/h or mph and the unit string is loud about that), `formatSpeed(speed)` that returns the rounded magnitude as a string and collapses non-finite inputs to `'0'`, `speedFraction(speed, maxSpeed?)` that clamps to `[0, 1]` with a defensive `0` for non-finite or non-positive `maxSpeed`, and `speedDirection(speed)` that returns `'idle' | 'forward' | 'reverse'` keyed by a tight symmetric threshold (`SPEED_DIRECTION_THRESHOLD = 1e-3`) so coast-to-zero does not flicker between forward and reverse. The HUD math reads the magnitude of the signed vehicle speed so reversing fills the bar the same way as accelerating forward, matching driver intuition. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` adds two refs (`hudSpeedValueRef`, `hudSpeedBarFillRef`) and an imperative `updateHud()` helper that writes the readout text and the bar `transform: scaleX(...)` each tick after `applyDriveStep` (so the React tree does not re-render every frame), mirrors `data-hud-speed` and `data-hud-direction` onto the scene root, and exposes `data-hud-visible` driven by `hasVehicle && !showPauseMenu` so the HUD hides while the pause menu owns the click surface. The JSX renders two new fixed-position overlays only when the vehicle is mounted and the pause menu is closed: a bottom-left speed readout (`drive-hud-speed` testid) showing the label, the unit, the live integer value, and a small horizontal speed bar with a left-anchored fill scaled to `speedFraction(vehicle.speed)`; a bottom-right controls hint (`drive-hud-controls` testid) listing the five binding rows from `HUD_CONTROLS_HINT_LINES`. Both overlays carry `pointerEvents: 'none'` so they do not steal clicks from a future canvas hit-test layer. `tests/app/driveHud.test.ts` ships 27 cases covering: `HUD_CONTROLS_HINT_LINES` length matches the action vocabulary (five rows: throttle / brake / steerLeft / steerRight / pause); every action label from `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS` appears in the hint text; W / A / S / D / Up / Down / Left / Right / Esc all appear so a screen reader announces both binding layouts; every line is non-empty and trimmed; `HUD_SPEED_LABEL` and `HUD_SPEED_UNIT` are non-empty and the unit is not falsely "mph" or "km/h"; `formatSpeed` rounds at rest, half-up, returns the magnitude for negative speed, and collapses NaN / Infinity to `'0'`; `speedFraction` is 0 at rest, 1 at MAX_SPEED, clamped at 1 above, 0.5 at half, magnitude-based for reverse, 0 for non-finite or non-positive `maxSpeed`, and respects a custom `maxSpeed` override (REQ-040 future settings); `speedDirection` reads idle at rest, forward / reverse outside a symmetric threshold band, idle inside the band, idle for non-finite, and the threshold is positive. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` adds five attribute / element assertions on both the scaffold and empty-state specs: `data-hud-visible="false"`, `data-hud-speed="0"`, `data-hud-direction="idle"` ride on the root regardless of the empty city, and the `drive-hud-speed` / `drive-hud-controls` testids resolve to zero elements (the listener gate that mounts the car also gates the HUD overlays so an author cannot see a speed readout for a car that is not on screen).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 503/503 pass (476 prior + 27 new driveHud cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 137 kB (was 136 kB; the HUD module and overlays add a small overhead, no new dependency). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 17/17 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new HUD assertions + 11 editor specs). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: A speedometer plus a controls hint is the v1 HUD surface; both are driver feedback, not race scoring, so they do not violate REQ-037 (anti-feature: NO laps, NO checkpoints, NO HUD lap timer). The HUD shows the magnitude of the signed speed so reversing fills the bar the same way as accelerating forward; this is the simplest consistent rule and the live `data-hud-direction` attribute disambiguates direction for tests and accessibility. Numbers display as integers (no decimals) so the readout flickers slowly enough to read at typical animation rates; the rounding cost is negligible. The HUD shows `wu/s` (world units per second) instead of pretending to show km/h or mph; the integrator is not calibrated to a real-world speed and the unit string is loud about that. The HUD is rendered through the React tree but the live value is written imperatively via refs so the integration loop does not trigger React re-renders (matches the existing `data-car-x` mirror pattern). Both overlays are hidden when `showPauseMenu` is true so the pause menu owns the click surface (REQ-039); on resume, the React tree remounts the HUD elements and the next tick writes the live values into them. The HUD is gated on `hasVehicle` so an empty city keeps the HUD dormant; the listener gate that mounts the car already gates the integration loop, and the JSX-level `hasVehicle` guard keeps the contract honest if a future slice unbinds that gate. `pointerEvents: 'none'` on both overlays means they never steal clicks from a future canvas hit-test layer (e.g. an in-scene UI). The controls hint lines come from `HUD_CONTROLS_HINT_LINES` rather than being computed from `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS` because the hint copy is a player-facing label string (e.g. "W / Up: throttle") and the binding table is keyed on `KeyboardEvent.code` (e.g. `KeyW`); a future settings pane (REQ-041) that lets a player rebind keys will need its own copy table that mirrors the live bindings. The unit tests assert the static hint mentions every default action so a future binding change without a copy update fails fast. The speed bar uses `transform: scaleX(...)` instead of width changes so the bar update is composited and does not trigger layout reflow. The bar's transform origin is the left edge so the fill grows from left to right, matching the readout above which reads from left as well. The HUD does not attempt to display the heading, the on-building flag, the camera mode, or the spawn anchor; those stay as data attributes on the scene root for tests. Per slice discipline, REQ-035 (touch input), REQ-040 onward (settings pane), REQ-054 (off-street penalty), and REQ-032 (wheel contact) all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the HUD overlay surface.
- GDD coverage: REQ-066 added to `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` as a new row (status `done`). `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Drive HUD section added with a build log entry; the Out-of-scope list dropped the speed readout note (REQ-037 still forbids the lap timer). REQ-037 stays `not_started` because this slice is the inverse of REQ-037 (the v1 HUD shows speed and controls only; lap timer / checkpoints / race HUD remain out of scope and the spec text in `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` reaffirms the boundary).
- Followups: none new. F-008 (seed a non-empty city in playwright so visible-movement assertions land) stays open and now has the HUD ready to verify alongside the chase camera and car movement once the seed mechanism lands.

## 2026-05-04, Building Cell Collision Penalty: REQ-030

- Branch: `feature/req-030-building-collision`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed REQ-030 by adding a cell-level binary collision penalty for buildings (Q-005 default A). When the car's center cell coincides with a building cell, an immediate speed cap plus extra drag pulls the car back to a slow crawl so a player who drives into a row of houses sees a clear penalty instead of breezing through. `src/app/[slug]/buildingCollision.ts` ships a pure module with `BUILDING_PENALTY_MAX_SPEED = CELL_SIZE * 2`, `BUILDING_PENALTY_MAX_REVERSE_SPEED = CELL_SIZE * 1`, and `BUILDING_PENALTY_DRAG = CELL_SIZE * 12` tuning constants; `worldToCell(x, z)` rounds the vehicle's world position to its integer cell coordinate matching the editor's cellToWorld convention so the cell key the lookup hits is exactly the cell the editor's place reducer wrote; `buildingCellSet(buildings)` builds a `Set<string>` keyed by `"row,col"` so the per-frame lookup is constant time; `isOnBuildingCell(x, z, set)` short-circuits on an empty set so a city with no buildings pays nothing per frame; `applyBuildingPenalty(state, onBuildingCell, dt)` returns the input state unchanged when off the building cell (or for a non-positive / non-finite dt) so the on-street path is allocation-free, and otherwise drags the speed magnitude toward zero by `BUILDING_PENALTY_DRAG * dt` (with sign-aware clamping that snaps a small residual to exactly zero) and clamps the speed to the penalty range so a held throttle on a building cell cannot push the car past the cap. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` memoizes the cell set via `useMemo` keyed on `city.buildings`, calls `applyBuildingPenalty(vehicle, onBuilding, dt)` immediately after `applyDriveStep` inside the per-frame integration loop, and mirrors the live flag onto the scene root as `data-on-building='true' | 'false'` so a future visible-movement test (F-008) can assert the penalty engages without sampling speed history. `tests/app/buildingCollision.test.ts` ships 28 cases covering the constants invariants (positive, finite, scale-with-CELL_SIZE, below the on-street caps), `worldToCell` (origin / cell-centers / negatives / nearest-rounding), `buildingCellSet` (empty / single / multiple / fresh-set), `isOnBuildingCell` (empty-set short-circuit / hit / miss / off-center within bounds / next-cell over), and `applyBuildingPenalty` (pass-through off-building, pass-through for non-positive / NaN dt, forward cap from MAX_SPEED, reverse cap from MAX_REVERSE_SPEED, forward drag below cap, reverse drag below cap, small-positive snap to zero, small-negative snap to zero, position immutability, full no-mutation, single-frame sustained-throttle cap). `e2e/drive.spec.ts` asserts `data-on-building='false'` on both the scaffold and empty-state specs (the playwright webServer runs without KV so `loadCity` returns the empty city; the unit tests cover the populated case in full). `docs/gdd/08-buildings.md` gained a build log entry for REQ-030; the Out-of-scope list still drops the hard-wall option since v1 ships Q-005 default A.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 476/476 pass (448 prior + 28 new buildingCollision cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS unchanged at 136 kB (the new module is small and the per-frame call adds negligible overhead). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 17/17 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new `data-on-building` assertions + 11 editor specs). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: Cell-level binary penalty (Q-005 default A) is the right v1 model. The hard-wall option (Q-005 option B) and the soft-bounce option (Q-005 option C) both require new collision code (line-segment collision against cell edges) that is not justified before playtest signal. The penalty cap (`CELL_SIZE * 2`, a quarter of `MAX_SPEED = CELL_SIZE * 8`) is slow enough that running into a building feels like a clear penalty without locking the car so hard that a brushing pass through a corner cell feels like a wall. Reverse cap is half the forward cap so a player who clipped a building cannot rocket out backward. The `BUILDING_PENALTY_DRAG = CELL_SIZE * 12` matches the on-street `BRAKE_DECELERATION` so the bleed feels firm; this is a tuning knob future playtest can move. The "car cell" is derived from the vehicle's center position via `worldToCell` (nearest-cell rounding) rather than a per-wheel sample because v1 has no wheel contact module yet (REQ-032). Per-wheel sampling lands when REQ-032 ships and the four wheel-cell lookups collapse into the same cell-key Set this module already builds. The penalty is applied immediately after `applyDriveStep` so the integrator's normal coast / throttle / brake / steering math runs first and the cap clamps the result; this keeps the integrator's `applyDriveStep` test surface unchanged. The lookup short-circuits on an empty cell set so a city with zero buildings pays nothing per frame; the early return is the cheapest version of the on-street allocation-free path. The `data-on-building` attribute is the precise discriminator for tests that want to assert the penalty engages without sampling speed history; the e2e specs cover the empty-state default of `'false'` (the playwright webServer is unconfigured KV so the city is empty and no car is mounted). REQ-054 (off-street penalty for cells with no street piece under the wheels) and REQ-032 (wheel contact port) stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the building-cell penalty so a player who drives into a building sees an immediate slowdown ahead of the broader off-street model.
- GDD coverage: REQ-030 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/08-buildings.md` Build log gained a REQ-030 entry; the Out-of-scope list still drops the hard-wall option since v1 ships Q-005 default A.
- Followups: none new. F-008 (seed a non-empty city in playwright so visible-movement assertions land) stays open and now has the building-cell penalty ready to verify alongside the chase camera and car movement once the seed mechanism lands.

## 2026-05-04, Drive Mode Pause Menu and Edit CTA: REQ-038, REQ-039

- Branch: `feature/req-039-pause-menu`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: closed the pause loop in the drive view so a player can step out of motion without leaving the page. `src/app/[slug]/pauseMenu.ts` ships a pure module with a two-state machine (`PauseState = 'running' | 'paused'`), the bound key code constant `PAUSE_KEY_CODE = 'Escape'`, the default starting state `DEFAULT_PAUSE_STATE = 'running'` (drive scene mounts in motion), and four pure transitions: `togglePauseState(state)` flips running to paused and back, `openPauseMenu(state)` forces the state to paused, `closePauseMenu(state)` forces the state to running, and `isPaused(state)` returns true only for the paused branch. VibeRacer's lap-timer compensation (which paused / resumed the lap timer around a paused interval) is dropped here because REQ-037 explicitly forbids the lap timer in VibeCity. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` now holds a `pauseState` React state and a `pauseStateRef` mirror so the per-frame integration loop can read the live value without a stale closure capture; a `useEffect` keeps the ref in sync on every state change. The car-mounted branch of the existing `useEffect` adds a `keydown` window listener for `Escape` (with the same text-target guard used for the WASD bindings so an Esc inside a future input dismisses the input as the browser default expects); the listener calls `event.preventDefault()` and dispatches `togglePauseState` so a single Esc press flips the menu open or closed. The integration tick checks `isPaused(pauseStateRef.current)` before calling `applyDriveStep`; when paused the loop renders one more frame against the frozen scene and resets `lastTimestamp = timestamp` so the first frame after resume does not back-integrate the elapsed pause duration into the vehicle delta (which would teleport the car forward by the pause length). The pressed-key set is intentionally NOT cleared on pause so resuming with throttle still down keeps accelerating, matching the driver intuition "I held W through the menu and the car kept going when I closed it." The pause menu cleanup matches the listener attach branch and removes the new `keydown` Esc handler on unmount. The component renders a pause overlay (`role='dialog'` / `aria-modal='true'` / `aria-label='Paused'` / `data-testid='drive-pause-menu'`) only when `hasVehicle && isPaused(pauseState)` so the empty grid (where the listener is dormant) cannot show the menu. The overlay sits at `zIndex: 10` above the slug label and the top-right Edit CTA so the menu owns the click surface while paused, lays the slug header over a translucent black backdrop, and offers two actions: a Resume button (`data-testid='drive-pause-resume'`, `autoFocus` so a screen reader / keyboard user lands on the primary action) that calls the same `closePauseMenu` transition, and an Edit CTA Link (`data-testid='drive-pause-edit-cta'`, `prefetch` like the existing top-right Edit) that navigates back to `/<slug>/edit` (REQ-038). A small "Press Esc to resume" footer reminds the player of the toggle. `data-pause-state='running' | 'paused'` mirror on the scene root is the precise discriminator for tests that want to assert the state without inspecting the React tree. `tests/app/pauseMenu.test.ts` ships 10 cases covering: `PAUSE_KEY_CODE === 'Escape'`, `DEFAULT_PAUSE_STATE === 'running'`, `togglePauseState` flips running to paused and back, two toggles round-trip from any starting state, `togglePauseState` does not mutate the input value, `openPauseMenu` and `closePauseMenu` force their respective targets regardless of prior state, `isPaused` returns true only for paused, and the running default matches `isPaused(DEFAULT_PAUSE_STATE) === false`. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` asserts `data-pause-state='running'` on both the scaffold and empty-state specs (the playwright webServer runs without KV so `loadCity` returns the empty city for both slugs and no car is mounted; the empty-state spec also presses Esc and asserts the menu does NOT render and the state stays running, proving the listener gate). `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Pause section is added with a build log entry; the Out-of-scope list drops REQ-038 / REQ-039.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 448/448 pass (438 prior + 10 new pauseMenu cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 136 kB (unchanged from the prior chase-camera slice; the pause module and overlay add a small overhead but stay inside the same chunk tier). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 17/17 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new `data-pause-state` assertions and Esc-on-empty check + 11 editor specs). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: Esc is the only key bound to the pause toggle in v1. Mirrors the platform convention across browsers; future settings (REQ-041) can rebind via a settings pane but should preserve Esc as the default. The pause menu lives only on the drive scene; the editor has its own keyboard shortcuts (Cmd+Z / Ctrl+Z for undo, R for rotate, E for erase) and an Esc inside an editor text input dismisses the input by browser default, so adding a global Esc listener to the editor would collide with that behavior. The pause listener is gated on the car being mounted (`if (car)` branch) so the empty grid cannot show the menu. The menu overlay is also gated in JSX on `hasVehicle && isPaused(pauseState)` so a future slice that unbinds the listener gate cannot accidentally render the menu against the empty-state prompt. While paused the integration loop still renders one frame per tick (so the overlay can read against a live canvas) but skips the `applyDriveStep` call and resets `lastTimestamp` so the first frame after resume does not back-integrate the elapsed pause duration into the vehicle delta. Pressed keys are NOT cleared on pause; resuming with throttle still down keeps the car accelerating, matching driver intuition. The menu offers two actions: Resume (closes the menu, returns to running) and Edit (navigates to `/<slug>/edit` via `next/link prefetch` so the route handoff is instant). The Edit CTA inside the pause menu duplicates the top-right Edit CTA but is the primary action while paused (the top-right is hidden behind the overlay's z-index 10 backdrop); duplicating the link is cheaper than hiding the top-right CTA conditionally and keeps the prefetch warm regardless of which menu the player ends up in. `autoFocus` on the Resume button means a keyboard / screen-reader user lands on the primary action; a player who pressed Esc to bring up the menu can press Enter to resume without grabbing the mouse. The overlay declares `role='dialog'` / `aria-modal='true'` so screen readers announce it as a modal dialog and confine focus inside it (the v1 surface ships only the two buttons so focus order is deterministic; if a settings pane lands later, the dialog will need a focus trap). The pause toggle does not save / restore camera or vehicle state because they are already preserved across the pause interval (the integrator simply does not run); resume picks up from the same vehicle position and heading. Per slice discipline, REQ-040 (camera tuning settings inside the pause menu), REQ-041 (keyboard rebinding), REQ-042 (touch mode), REQ-043 (settings persistence), REQ-035 (touch input), REQ-032 (wheel contact), and REQ-054 (off-street penalty) all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only the pause overlay and the Edit CTA.
- GDD coverage: REQ-039 flipped `not_started` to `done`. REQ-038 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` gained a Pause section with a build log entry; the Out-of-scope list dropped REQ-038 / REQ-039.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Chase Camera Rig: REQ-033

- Branch: `feature/req-033-chase-camera`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: replaced the static aerial / orbit camera with a chase rig that follows the placeholder car (REQ-047) once the keyboard input slice (REQ-034) and the kinematic integrator (REQ-031) move it. `src/app/[slug]/cameraRig.ts` ships a pure module: `CameraRigParams` (`height` / `distance` / `lookAhead` / `targetHeight` / `positionLerp` / `targetLerp`) and `CameraRigState` (`position` / `target`) types; tuning constants `CAMERA_RIG_HEIGHT = CELL_SIZE * 1.6`, `CAMERA_RIG_DISTANCE = CELL_SIZE * 3.5`, `CAMERA_RIG_LOOK_AHEAD = CELL_SIZE * 1.5`, `CAMERA_RIG_TARGET_HEIGHT = CELL_SIZE * 0.25`, `CAMERA_RIG_POSITION_LERP = 0.12`, `CAMERA_RIG_TARGET_LERP = 0.2` (chase-far preset matching VibeRacer's defaults at world scale); `DEFAULT_CAMERA_RIG` aggregates the constants; `desiredRigPose(carX, carZ, heading, params?)` returns the snap-to camera position behind the car (`car - forward * distance`) and the look target ahead of the car (`car + forward * lookAhead`) under the VibeCity heading convention `forward = (sin(h), -cos(h))` so a heading of 0 points the car along world `-z`; `createCameraRig(carX, carZ, heading, params?)` snaps the rig to the spawn pose so the first rendered frame already has the camera behind the car; `updateCameraRig(rig, carX, carZ, heading, params?)` mutates the rig in place toward the latest desired pose with a `clampLerp` helper that maps `NaN` / negative / above-1 lerp factors to safe values so a tuning bug cannot push the rig away from the desired pose. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` initializes a rig from the spawn pose when a car is mounted, calls `updateCameraRig` each tick before the renderer call, copies `rig.position` into the camera and calls `camera.lookAt(rig.target)` via a single shared `THREE.Vector3` so the per-frame loop allocates nothing, exposes `data-camera-mode='chase' | 'orbit'` on the scene root for tests, and exposes `data-camera-x` / `data-camera-z` mirroring the rig position for a future visible-movement test. The static aerial / orbit aim still applies on empty cities so the empty-state prompt reads against a neutral backdrop centered on the grid origin. `tests/app/cameraRig.test.ts` ships 27 cases covering: tuning constants invariants (positive finite, lerp factors in `(0, 1]`, target below camera height above ground, distance / look-ahead scale with `CELL_SIZE`); `DEFAULT_CAMERA_RIG` mirror; `desiredRigPose` forward-direction conventions across the four cardinal headings (0 -> camera south of car, `PI/2` -> west, `PI` -> north, `-PI/2` -> east) plus a 45deg case; the camera-to-target-vector pointing forward through the car at the resting pose; translation with car position; height vs target-height separation; custom-params override leaves the `DEFAULT_CAMERA_RIG` literal untouched; `createCameraRig` snaps and returns a fresh object distinct from the desired pose; two successive create calls return independent state objects; `updateCameraRig` mutates the rig in place rather than allocating; lerp moves position and target toward the desired pose by the configured factor; lerp 1 snaps in one step; lerp 0 leaves the rig unchanged; clamping for negative / above-1 / NaN factors; repeated lerp 1 stays snapped to the latest car pose; repeated default lerp converges over 200 iterations; independent position / target lerp factors; y axis preservation across lerps. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` asserts `data-camera-mode='orbit'` on both the scaffold and empty-state specs since the playwright webServer runs without KV so `loadCity` returns the empty city for both slugs (the chase-mode assertion will land alongside the visible-movement work tracked under F-008). `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Camera section flipped from a v1 placeholder paragraph to a full chase-rig spec; build log entry added.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 438/438 pass (411 prior + 27 new cameraRig cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS at 136 kB (was 135 kB; the rig math and the per-frame `lookAt` add a small overhead, no new dependency). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 17/17 (smoke + 3 home + 2 drive specs with the new `data-camera-mode` assertions + 11 editor specs). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: VibeRacer's chase rig math (`initCameraRig` / `updateCameraRig` in `src/game/sceneBuilder.ts`) does not port verbatim because VibeRacer's heading convention uses `forward = (cos(h), -sin(h))` while VibeCity's `applyDriveStep` integrator uses `forward = (sin(h), -cos(h))`. Porting the math by hand (and unit-testing the four cardinal-heading cases plus a 45deg case) catches the convention swap up front instead of waiting for a visual regression. The chase-far preset is the only camera surface in v1; settings (REQ-040) replace the hard-coded constants with a tunable pane in a later slice. The rig is initialized from the spawn pose so the first rendered frame already has the camera behind the car instead of starting at the orbit aim and lerping in (which would produce a one-second swooping intro on every page load). Position and target use independent lerp factors (`positionLerp = 0.12`, `targetLerp = 0.2`) so the look-ahead point pans through turns slightly faster than the camera body settles; matches the VibeRacer arcade convention. The lerp-clamp helper maps `NaN` / negative / above-1 factors to safe values (0 / 0 / 1) so a future settings pane that exposes the lerps as user-editable cannot push the rig off the desired pose with a malformed input. The rig only engages when a car is mounted; an empty city stays in the orbit aim so the empty-state prompt reads against the same neutral framing the v1 scaffold shipped. The chase camera and the orbit aim are the two camera modes for v1; future modes (cockpit, hood, bumper per VibeRacer's `cameraForward` parameter) wait for the settings pane slice. The per-frame `camera.lookAt(rig.target)` call uses a single shared `THREE.Vector3` initialized outside the tick so the integration loop does not allocate per frame. The `data-camera-mode` attribute is the precise discriminator for tests that want to assert chase / orbit engagement without inspecting the WebGL state; `data-camera-x` / `data-camera-z` mirror the rig position so the future visible-movement test (F-008) can assert that pressing a key moves both the car and the camera in lockstep. Per slice discipline, REQ-040 (camera tuning settings), REQ-038 / REQ-039 (pause menu and Edit-from-pause), REQ-032 (wheel contact), REQ-054 (off-street penalty), and REQ-035 (touch input) all stay deferred to their own slices.
- GDD coverage: REQ-033 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Camera section flipped from a v1 placeholder paragraph to a full chase-rig spec; build log entry added at the top.
- Followups: none new. F-008 (seed a non-empty city in playwright so visible-movement assertions land) stays open and now has the chase camera ready to verify alongside the car movement once the seed mechanism lands.

## 2026-05-04, Keyboard Controls and Kinematic Vehicle Integration: REQ-034, REQ-031 (partial)

- Branch: `feature/req-034-keyboard-controls`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: wired up the first keyboard input slice and a planar arcade vehicle integrator so the placeholder car (REQ-047) actually moves when keys are pressed. `src/app/[slug]/driveControls.ts` ships a pure module with `DriveAction` (`'throttle' | 'brake' | 'steerLeft' | 'steerRight'`), `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS` (a `KeyboardEvent.code` table mapping `KeyW` and `ArrowUp` to throttle, `KeyS` and `ArrowDown` to brake, `KeyA` and `ArrowLeft` to steer left, `KeyD` and `ArrowRight` to steer right so the binding is layout-stable across QWERTY / AZERTY / Dvorak), tuning constants (`MAX_SPEED = CELL_SIZE * 8`, `MAX_REVERSE_SPEED = CELL_SIZE * 4`, `ACCELERATION = CELL_SIZE * 6`, `BRAKE_DECELERATION = CELL_SIZE * 12`, `COAST_DRAG = CELL_SIZE * 3`, `STEER_RATE_AT_REST = PI * 1.6`, `STEER_RATE_AT_MAX_SPEED = PI * 0.8`, `MAX_DELTA_SECONDS = 1/15`), and pure helpers `createVehicleState`, `emptyInput`, `inputFromPressedKeys(pressed, bindings?)`, `steerRateForSpeed(speed)`, and `applyDriveStep(state, input, dt)` that integrate one frame of the vehicle. Throttle adds forward acceleration, brake decelerates a forward-moving car and accelerates in reverse from rest, steering rate scales linearly from `STEER_RATE_AT_REST` (parked car can pivot in place) to `STEER_RATE_AT_MAX_SPEED` (high-speed turns less twitchy), reverse flips the steering sign so the car steers from the rear axle, and `dt` clamps at `MAX_DELTA_SECONDS` so a long pause (tab in background, devtools breakpoint) cannot teleport the car across the map on resume. `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` keeps the existing `useEffect` and adds: a `rootRef` for imperative DOM updates, `keydown` / `keyup` / `blur` window listeners scoped to the car-mounted branch (`city.pieces.length > 0`), a `requestAnimationFrame` integration loop that calls `applyDriveStep` with the live pressed-key set and the actual frame delta, mirrors the resulting `vehicle.x` / `vehicle.z` / `vehicle.heading` / `vehicle.speed` into the placeholder car's `THREE.Group` `position` / `rotation` and into `data-car-x` / `data-car-z` / `data-car-heading` / `data-car-speed` attributes via imperative `setAttribute` (so React does not re-render every frame), `data-keys-pressed` mirrors the live key set as a sorted space-separated string, `data-controls-active` mirrors `hasVehicle` so a test can assert the listeners are dormant on the empty grid, `event.preventDefault` is called on bound key codes inside `keydown` so arrow keys do not scroll the page while the player is steering, an editor-style text-target guard short-circuits before `preventDefault` so a future text input on the drive surface is not blocked, and the `blur` listener clears the pressed-key set so a tab switch does not leave the throttle stuck on. `tests/app/driveControls.test.ts` ships 40 cases covering `DEFAULT_KEY_BINDINGS` invariants, `inputFromPressedKeys` (empty / single key / either-binding / combined / unbound-key-ignore / custom-table-without-touching-default), `createVehicleState` (state shape and fresh-object), `steerRateForSpeed` (rest / max / below-negative-max / linear-interpolation / negative-speed-by-magnitude / sharper-at-low-speed), and `applyDriveStep` coast / drag / dt-clamp / dt-zero-or-negative / dt-NaN / fresh-object / throttle-accelerate / throttle-cap / world-axes-by-heading / brake-decelerate-forward / brake-clamp-at-zero / brake-reverse-from-rest / reverse-cap / throttle-and-brake-cancel / steer-left / steer-right / both-cancel / reverse-flip-sign / parked-pivot. `e2e/drive.spec.ts` asserts `data-controls-active="false"` on both the scaffold and empty-state specs (the playwright webServer runs without KV so `loadCity` returns the empty city). `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Input section flipped from "v1 ships no input handling" to a full spec describing the bindings, the integration loop, the data-attribute mirror, and the gated listeners; build log entry added.

JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: Listeners attach to `window` rather than the canvas so the canvas does not need focus to drive; a click anywhere in the page does not steal driving control. The pressed-key set is keyed on `KeyboardEvent.code` rather than `KeyboardEvent.key` so the binding is layout-stable across QWERTY / AZERTY / Dvorak; a future locale-aware rebinding pane (REQ-041) can swap the table without touching the integrator. Listeners and the integration loop both gate on the car being mounted (`city.pieces.length > 0`); an empty city keeps both dormant so an author cannot accidentally accelerate a car that is not on screen, and the listener-removal path in the cleanup matches the listener-attach path so unmount stays symmetric. Reverse caps at half forward speed (`MAX_REVERSE_SPEED = MAX_SPEED / 2`) so the car cannot rocket backward through buildings; matches arcade convention. Steering rate scales linearly with speed magnitude (sharper at rest, less twitchy at high speed) so the car steers from the rear axle in reverse; a parked car still pivots so a builder can re-aim before driving, which is the intuitive behavior even though it is not strictly physical. `dt` clamps at `MAX_DELTA_SECONDS = 1 / 15` so a long pause (tab switch, devtools breakpoint) cannot teleport the car across the map on the first frame after the pause resolves; the cap is generous enough that a low-end laptop's animation loop still lands inside it on a normal frame. The car's world-space coordinates are mirrored into `data-car-*` attributes via imperative `setAttribute` so the per-frame integration does not trigger React re-renders (which would re-create the three.js scene every frame); the `rootRef` is the discriminator for the test surface. `event.preventDefault` is called on `keydown` for bound key codes so arrow keys do not scroll the page while steering; the editor-style text-target guard short-circuits before `preventDefault` so a future text input on the drive surface stays usable. `blur` clears the pressed-key set so a tab switch does not leave the throttle stuck on; this mirrors VibeRacer's input-clear-on-blur convention. Per slice discipline, the chase camera (REQ-033), wheel contact (REQ-032), off-street penalty (REQ-054), pause menu (REQ-039), settings pane (REQ-040 onward), and touch input (REQ-035) all stay deferred to their own slices; this slice ships only keyboard input and the kinematic integrator so the build / drive loop has a movable car.
- GDD coverage: REQ-034 flipped `not_started` to `done`. REQ-031 flipped `not_started` to `partial` (the kinematic integrator ships; the upstream physics surface concerns wait for their own slices). `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Input section flipped from a "v1 ships no input handling" placeholder to a full spec; build log entry added at the top.
- Followups: F-008 (seed a non-empty city in playwright so visible-movement assertions land) added. The unit tests cover the integration math in full; the open gap is a true black-box assertion that pressing a key moves the visible car, which requires either a playwright KV fixture or a test-only seed endpoint.

## 2026-05-04, Placeholder Player Vehicle at Spawn Anchor: REQ-047

- Branch: `feature/req-047-placeholder-car`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: replaced the chevron-shaped spawn marker (REQ-036) with a primitive-composed placeholder player vehicle so the build / drive loop has a visible car ahead of physics (REQ-031), input (REQ-034 / REQ-035), and the chase camera (REQ-033). `src/app/[slug]/driveScene.ts` gained a CAR constant block: `CAR_BODY_COLOR = 0xc0392b` / `CAR_CABIN_COLOR = 0x8e2a1f` / `CAR_WHEEL_COLOR = 0x1c1c1c` (saturated red body, slightly darker cabin so the windscreen line reads, near-black wheels so the contact patch reads), `CAR_LENGTH = CELL_SIZE * 0.7` / `CAR_WIDTH = CELL_SIZE * 0.36` / `CAR_BODY_HEIGHT = CELL_SIZE * 0.18` body footprint (length > width so the forward axis is unambiguous from the orbit camera), `CAR_CABIN_LENGTH = CELL_SIZE * 0.34` / `CAR_CABIN_WIDTH = CELL_SIZE * 0.32` / `CAR_CABIN_HEIGHT = CELL_SIZE * 0.14` / `CAR_CABIN_OFFSET = CELL_SIZE * 0.06` cabin geometry (cabin fits inside the body, offset toward the rear so the windscreen line reads forward), `CAR_WHEEL_RADIUS = CELL_SIZE * 0.08` / `CAR_WHEEL_THICKNESS = CELL_SIZE * 0.05` / `CAR_WHEEL_INSET = CELL_SIZE * 0.02` / `CAR_AXLE_OFFSET = CELL_SIZE * 0.22` wheel placement, `CAR_GROUND_LIFT = CELL_SIZE * 0.02` so the wheels touch the ground without z-fighting. Added three pure helpers: `carWheelOffsets()` returns a fresh 4-element array of `{ x, z, side, axle }` so a caller cannot mutate a shared singleton (the four-wheel mirror symmetry is encoded in the math, not in a frozen literal), `carBodyY()` and `carCabinY()` derive the body and cabin vertical centers from the same constants (so a future tuning slice flips one constant and both helpers track). `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` builds a `THREE.Group` named `placeholder-car` at the spawn anchor when `city.pieces.length > 0` containing the body box (`BoxGeometry(CAR_WIDTH, CAR_BODY_HEIGHT, CAR_LENGTH)` in `CAR_BODY_COLOR`), the cabin box (`BoxGeometry(CAR_CABIN_WIDTH, CAR_CABIN_HEIGHT, CAR_CABIN_LENGTH)` in `CAR_CABIN_COLOR` offset toward the rear by `CAR_CABIN_OFFSET`), and four wheel cylinders (`CylinderGeometry(CAR_WHEEL_RADIUS, CAR_WHEEL_RADIUS, CAR_WHEEL_THICKNESS, 16)` rotated 90deg around z so the round face is the contact patch and the axis runs left / right of the body). The chevron spawn marker is removed; the car composition replaces it at the same spawn anchor cell with the same first-piece-rotation orientation. `data-vehicle="true" | "false"` flag on the scene root tells Playwright whether the car is mounted (it is omitted on an empty grid because the empty-state prompt owns the visual focus). The renderer cleanup pass (`scene.traverse`) already disposes every Mesh's geometry and material, so the additional car meshes are cleaned up on unmount without a code change. `tests/app/driveScene.test.ts` gained 13 new cases: a CAR constants block (color hex bounds, distinct body / cabin colors, every dimension positive and bounded by `CELL_SIZE`, length > width invariant, cabin fits inside body, wheels fit inside body width once inset is applied, axles inside body length, cabin offset keeps cabin inside body footprint), a `carWheelOffsets` block (four wheels split into front / rear and left / right, left and right mirror across the body x axis, front and rear mirror across the body z axis, fresh-array contract, every wheel sits inside the body footprint accounting for cylinder thickness on the x axis), a `carBodyY / carCabinY` block (body vertical placement equals ground lift plus wheel radius plus half body height, cabin vertical placement equals body top plus half cabin height, cabin y > body y so the silhouette reads as a car). `e2e/drive.spec.ts` asserts `data-vehicle="false"` on both the scaffold and empty-state specs since the playwright webServer runs without KV so `loadCity` returns the empty city for both slugs. `docs/gdd/11-scene.md` Vehicle section flipped from "Out of scope for v1" to a full spec; build log entry added. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Spawn anchor section updated to reflect the chevron-to-car migration.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported the expected pass count (356 prior + 13 new car cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS unchanged in tier (the car composition adds a few constants and four meshes but no new dependency). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed all specs (smoke + 3 home + drive scaffold + drive empty + editor specs). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: A primitive-composed car (body box + cabin box + four wheel cylinders) beats a single placeholder mesh because the four-wheel silhouette reads as a car at a glance from the orbit camera while a single box reads as a chevron or a brick. The primitives stay; the GLTF import path is deliberately not introduced in v1 to avoid pulling a loader into the bundle before the loop is fun. Per AGENTS.md Rule 3, no new core dependency is added; raw three.js primitives cover the silhouette. The car is a static mesh in v1; it does not move, accept input, or update across frames. The next slices attach the keyboard input (REQ-034), physics integrator (REQ-031), wheel contact (REQ-032), and chase camera (REQ-033) to the same `THREE.Group`. The group node is named `placeholder-car` so a future slice can `scene.getObjectByName('placeholder-car')` and attach a transform-driven update without re-walking the children. Saturated red is picked for the body so the car reads against the asphalt-grey pieces and the cream ground plane; the cabin is a slightly darker shade so the windscreen line is legible; wheels are near-black so the contact patch reads. The cabin sits offset toward the rear by `CAR_CABIN_OFFSET` so the windscreen line reads forward; without the offset the cabin would center on the body and the orbit camera could not tell which end is the nose. Wheel placement is encoded in math (`carWheelOffsets()` returns the four corners of a `(CAR_WIDTH - 2 * CAR_WHEEL_INSET) x (2 * CAR_AXLE_OFFSET)` rectangle) so a tuning slice flips one constant and the four-wheel positions stay symmetric. Cylinder geometry is rotated 90deg around z so the round face is the contact patch and the axis runs left / right of the body, matching the orientation a future steering rig will inherit. Vertical placement uses `carBodyY()` and `carCabinY()` rather than literal numbers so the body / cabin / wheels move in lockstep when a tuning slice adjusts the ground lift or the wheel radius. The spawn anchor data attributes (`data-spawn-row` / `data-spawn-col`) are preserved; the new `data-vehicle="true" | "false"` flag is the precise discriminator for tests that want to assert the car is mounted without inspecting the WebGL scene graph. The chevron marker constants (`SPAWN_MARKER_COLOR` / `SPAWN_MARKER_LENGTH` / `SPAWN_MARKER_WIDTH` / `SPAWN_MARKER_HEIGHT`) stay exported so a future slice that swaps the car for a higher-fidelity model can still reference the same anchor footprint while the placeholder car is in flight; the constants are no longer wired into the scene mount but the unit tests still cover their invariants (REQ-036 spawn anchor coverage). Per slice discipline, REQ-031 (physics), REQ-032 (wheel contact), REQ-033 (chase camera), REQ-034 / REQ-035 (input), REQ-038 / REQ-039 (pause / Edit-from-pause), and REQ-054 (off-street penalty) all stay deferred to their own slices.
- GDD coverage: REQ-047 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/11-scene.md` Vehicle section gained a v1 placeholder-car spec; build log entry added at the top. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Spawn anchor section updated to reflect the chevron-to-car migration.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Home Page List and Create Form: REQ-011, REQ-050

- Branch: `feature/req-011-050-home-page`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: turned the home page at `/` from the placeholder pitch into the canonical entry point for the build / drive loop. `src/lib/recentSlugs.ts` ships a pure helper `recentSlugs(limit?)` that reads the `city:index` sorted set via `kv.zrange(... 0, capped - 1, { rev: true })`, validates each member through `SlugSchema` (skipping any malformed member with a `console.warn` so a manual KV edit cannot leak a broken link into the home page UI), defaults to `DEFAULT_RECENT_SLUGS_LIMIT = 12` (enough to fill a default desktop viewport without paying for a large KV read), caps at `MAX_RECENT_SLUGS_LIMIT = 100` so an unbounded request cannot DoS KV, and returns an empty list when KV is not configured (`hasKvConfigured() === false`) so the local-dev experience without env vars is a clean empty state instead of a thrown error. `src/app/HomeCreateForm.tsx` is a use-client form that holds the raw input in local state, runs it through `normalizeSlug` and `SlugSchema.safeParse` via `useMemo`, renders a live preview via `aria-live='polite'` showing the destination URL ("Will open /downtown/edit") or the validation hint, gates the submit button on validity, and navigates via `useRouter().push('/<normalized>/edit')` on submit. `src/app/page.tsx` is now a server component (`export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` so the recently-updated list reflects the latest writes on every request) that calls `await recentSlugs()` and renders the heading, the `HomeCreateForm`, and a "Recently updated" section that is either an empty-state paragraph (`data-testid='home-recent-empty'`) or a grid list of `<Link href={`/${slug}`} prefetch>` entries each carrying `data-slug`. Each list entry links to `/<slug>` (the drive view) rather than `/<slug>/edit` because the home page is the entry point for visitors who want to look at cities other people built; a builder who owns the city already has the Edit CTA in the drive scene's top-right corner. Created `docs/gdd/12-home-page.md` as the canonical spec for REQ-011 + REQ-050 with a build log entry. Added `tests/lib/recentSlugs.test.ts` (10 cases: KV unconfigured, empty index, newest-first ordering via `{ rev: true }`, explicit limit smaller than count, limit greater than count, zero / negative limit short-circuit, MAX cap via `vi.spyOn(fake, 'zrange')` confirming the stop arg, invalid-member skip with warn, namespace key invariant). Added `e2e/home.spec.ts` (3 specs: home page surface end-to-end with heading + form + empty list section; Create input flow exercising normalize on mixed-case + disallowed characters, all-disallowed collapse to empty disabling submit, leading-dash strip rescue; submit click navigates to `/<normalized>/edit`).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported the expected pass count (346 prior + 10 new recentSlugs cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/` now a server-rendered dynamic route (was a static placeholder; `force-dynamic` keeps the recently-updated list fresh on every request). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed all specs (smoke + 3 new home specs + drive specs + editor specs). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: 12 entries is the right v1 default for the recently-updated list. Enough to fill a default desktop viewport without paying for a large KV read; a paginated route can opt into a larger limit via the `limit` parameter on `recentSlugs(limit)`. `MAX_RECENT_SLUGS_LIMIT = 100` is a defensive cap so a future caller cannot accidentally pull the full sorted set across the wire; the home page never goes near this in v1, but the cap exists so a paginated route cannot silently DoS KV. The list links to `/<slug>` (drive view) rather than `/<slug>/edit` because the home page is the visitor entry point; builders who own a city use the in-scene Edit CTA. The Create form normalizes raw input via `normalizeSlug` (lowercase, drop disallowed characters, strip leading dashes, clamp to 128 chars) and validates the normalized result against `SlugSchema` to gate the submit button. Live preview shows the destination URL so a visitor who types `Downtown!` sees `downtown` and understands what URL the Create button will navigate to; an all-dash input (which `normalizeSlug` collapses to empty) shows a helper message and disables submit. `aria-invalid` flips on the input only when the normalized result fails the schema and the visitor has typed at least one character, so an empty input does not falsely report an error on first focus. `aria-describedby` points at the preview paragraph so a screen reader announces the destination URL or hint. `export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` on the home page is the simplest consistent rule for keeping the list fresh; a future slice can revisit if request volume grows. The form lives at the top of the page above the list so a returning visitor reaches the Create input without scrolling past every existing city; the empty-state copy under "Recently updated" reads "No cities yet. Pick a slug above to start building." which points back to the form. Invalid members in the sorted set are skipped (not erroring the whole call) because a malformed member is most likely a manual KV edit or an old format, not a corruption that should crash the home page; the warn is enough to surface it in deploy logs. The `recentSlugs` helper does not deduplicate or filter empty cities (a slug whose latest hash points at an empty city) because every PUT to a slug writes to `city:index`, and an author who erased their last piece still owns a slug that should appear in the list. Per slice discipline, the list does not show piece counts, owner ids, or visual previews; those live in their own slices when playtest signals they matter.
- GDD coverage: REQ-011 flipped `not_started` to `done`. REQ-050 flipped `not_started` to `done`. Created `docs/gdd/12-home-page.md` as the canonical spec; build log entry added.
- Followups: none new.

## 2026-05-04, Drive Spawn Anchor and Placeholder Marker: REQ-036

- Branch: `feature/req-036-spawn-anchor`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: gave the drive scene a deterministic spawn anchor and a visible placeholder marker so a builder can see where the future vehicle (REQ-047) will appear when the physics slice (REQ-031) lands. `src/app/[slug]/driveScene.ts` gained `spawnAnchor(pieces)` (pure helper that returns `(piece.row, piece.col)` of the first placed piece, or `(0, 0)` when no pieces exist) plus four placeholder-marker constants (`SPAWN_MARKER_COLOR = 0xd94f3a`, `SPAWN_MARKER_LENGTH = CELL_SIZE * 0.6`, `SPAWN_MARKER_WIDTH = CELL_SIZE * 0.32`, `SPAWN_MARKER_HEIGHT = CELL_SIZE * 0.22`). `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` memoizes the spawn anchor across re-renders, renders a small chevron-shaped `BoxGeometry` marker mesh at the spawn anchor world position when `city.pieces.length > 0` (orientation flows from the first piece's rotation so the marker points the way the future vehicle would face), and exposes `data-spawn-row` / `data-spawn-col` on the scene root for tests and future overlays. The marker is omitted on an empty grid because the empty-state prompt owns the visual focus there. Added 9 new vitest cases to `tests/app/driveScene.test.ts` covering `spawnAnchor` (empty city -> origin, first-piece anchor, second-piece tiebreak by array order not by numerical proximity, negative coordinates, rotation stability across the four 90deg increments, fresh-object return on each call) and the `SPAWN_MARKER_*` constants (hex bounds, positive-and-bounded-by-CELL_SIZE dimensions, length-greater-than-width forward axis). Extended `e2e/drive.spec.ts` so both the drive-scaffold-spec and the empty-state spec assert `data-spawn-row="0"` / `data-spawn-col="0"` on the root (the playwright webServer runs without KV so `loadCity` returns the empty city for both slugs and the anchor lands on the origin). Updated `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` with a Spawn anchor section and a build log entry; the Out-of-scope list dropped REQ-036.
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 346/346 pass (337 prior + 9 new driveScene cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` route-specific JS unchanged in tier (the marker mesh adds a few constants but no new dependency). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 14/14 (the same scaffold + empty-state specs now also assert the spawn data attributes). `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: The spawn anchor follows array order in `city.pieces`, not numerical proximity to the origin, because the editor's `placePiece` reducer appends to that array (REQ-020) so the array order is the placement order. "First placed" matches a builder's mental model of "the first road I dropped is where the car shows up." Erasing the first piece shifts the anchor to the next piece in the array; this is the simplest consistent rule and a builder can re-place the original piece to restore the anchor. Buildings do not affect the spawn anchor because v1 cannot drive through buildings (REQ-030 off-street penalty); spawning on a building cell would put the car off-street from frame zero. The marker is a chevron-shaped box (length > width) rather than a sphere or square so the orientation reads at a glance from the orbit camera, even though v1 has no visible "car nose" yet; this lets the future REQ-047 vehicle slice swap a real car model into the same spot without rewiring the orientation contract. The marker color (`#d94f3a`, a saturated orange-red) is picked to read distinctly against the asphalt-gray pieces and the cream ground plane so a first-time author cannot miss the spawn point. Marker height is intentionally lower than the shortest building (`small-house = CELL_SIZE * 0.6`) so a marker on a piece adjacent to a small house does not visually compete with the building silhouette. Per slice discipline, REQ-047 (VibeRacer car model) stays `not_started` because the marker is a chevron placeholder, not the VibeRacer model; the next slice that ports the actual car can replace the marker mesh without changing the spawn anchor contract. The physics (REQ-031), wheel contact (REQ-032), chase camera (REQ-033), input (REQ-034 / REQ-035), pause / Edit-from-pause (REQ-038 / REQ-039), and off-street penalty (REQ-054) all stay deferred to their own slices.
- GDD coverage: REQ-036 flipped `not_started` to `done`. `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` Build log gained a REQ-036 entry; the Spawn anchor section was added and the Out-of-scope list dropped REQ-036.
- Followups: none new.

Added `src/app/[slug]/driveScene.ts` (pure helper module: `CELL_SIZE = 4` matches VibeRacer world units; `SKY_COLOR` (`0xbfd9e8`) and `GROUND_COLOR` (`0xf2ecd9`) defaults; `AMBIENT_LIGHT_INTENSITY = 0.6` and `DIRECTIONAL_LIGHT_INTENSITY = 0.9` plus `DIRECTIONAL_LIGHT_POSITION = (60, 100, 40)` for the noon-style lighting rig; `CAMERA_FOV = 50` / `CAMERA_NEAR = 0.1` / `CAMERA_FAR = 1000` / `CAMERA_HEIGHT = CELL_SIZE * 24` / `CAMERA_DISTANCE = CELL_SIZE * 24` for the aerial / orbit camera; `PIECE_GROUND_LIFT = 0.02` to avoid z-fighting with the ground plane; `BUILDING_HEIGHTS` and `BUILDING_COLORS` per-type maps for the four placeholder building primitives so silhouettes vary (small-house shortest, factory tallest); `PIECE_COLORS` partial map plus `DEFAULT_PIECE_COLOR = 0x4a4a4a` fallback so any future piece type still renders without a code change here; `pieceColorFor` / `buildingColorFor` / `buildingHeightFor` helpers; `cellToWorld(row, col)` that maps grid cells to world `(x, z)` matching the editor convention (row -> z south, col -> x east); `cityWorldBounds(pieces, buildings)` returns null for an empty city so the empty-state branch is a single null check; `rotationToRadians`). Added `src/app/[slug]/DriveSceneClient.tsx` (use-client three.js mount: builds the renderer / scene / camera, attaches the ambient + directional lights, lays down a `PlaneGeometry` ground plane sized `CELL_SIZE * 64`, renders street pieces as flat `PlaneGeometry` quads at the cell center lifted by `PIECE_GROUND_LIFT` and rotated around world Y by the persisted rotation, renders buildings as `BoxGeometry` extrusions sized `CELL_SIZE * 0.85` square with per-type heights and colors, fits the camera to the bounds center on every city change, exposes the slug label in the top-left overlay and the Edit CTA in the top-right overlay, renders the empty-state prompt centered on the canvas when the city has zero pieces and zero buildings with an Open editor link back to `/<slug>/edit`, and cleans up renderer / scene materials on unmount via a Mesh traverse + dispose pass so navigating away does not leak GPU memory across slugs). Updated `src/app/[slug]/page.tsx` to validate the slug, load the saved city via `loadCity` (REQ-015 read path), and mount `DriveSceneClient` (REQ-006 fully wires the drive view route end to end). Added `tests/app/driveScene.test.ts` (29 cases: constants invariants for `CELL_SIZE`, the camera fov / near / far / height / distance defaults, ambient + directional intensities, sky / ground color hex bounds, ground lift; `pieceColorFor` totality across `PieceTypeSchema.options` plus the asphalt fallback; `buildingColorFor` / `buildingHeightFor` totality plus distinct-color guard plus the silhouette ordering invariant; `cellToWorld` symmetry across the four cardinal directions; `rotationToRadians` for every 90deg increment; `cityWorldBounds` empty-city null pivot, single-piece centered-on-origin case, two-piece inflate, multi-cell footprint expansion, building-only city, mixed pieces + buildings). Added `e2e/drive.spec.ts` (two specs: drive route mounts the canvas with the slug label in the top-left and the Edit CTA pointing at `/<slug>/edit` in the top-right; drive route shows the empty-state prompt for a fresh slug with the right copy and the Open editor link routes back to the editor). Created `docs/gdd/09-drive-mode.md` (canonical spec for REQ-031 through REQ-039 plus REQ-053; build log entry for the v1 scaffold) and `docs/gdd/11-scene.md` (canonical spec for REQ-044 through REQ-047; build log entry for the v1 scaffold). Added `three` and `@types/three` dependencies to `package.json` (raw three, no R3F per AGENTS.md Rule 3; the loop instructions explicitly authorize three for the drive scene).
- Verification: `npm run check:dashes` exited 0. `npx tsc --noEmit` exited 0. `npm test` reported 337/337 pass (308 prior + 29 driveScene cases). `npm run build` produced a green production build with `/[slug]` reporting 134 kB route-specific JS (up from 4.07 kB to cover the three.js library plus the scene mount; expected because three is ~120 kB minified+gzipped). `npx playwright test --project chromium` passed 14/14 (12 prior + 2 new drive specs) in roughly 10s. `git diff --check` clean. JSON syntax of `docs/GDD_COVERAGE.json` validated.
- Assumptions: The drive scene is a static aerial / orbit view in v1, not a chase camera over a movable car. The chase camera (REQ-033), physics (REQ-031), wheel contact (REQ-032), keyboard / touch input (REQ-034 / REQ-035), spawn anchor (REQ-036), pause menu and Edit-from-pause (REQ-038 / REQ-039), and off-street penalty (REQ-054) all wait for their own slices because the segment-based path (REQ-064) and multi-cell footprint plumbing (REQ-059) have not landed yet; shipping the static scaffold first means the build / drive round trip can be experienced ahead of a drivable car, and the next slice that ports physics has a real scene to attach to. `CELL_SIZE = 4` matches VibeRacer's world units so a future port of physics inherits the same world-space unit. Lighting is the noon preset only; the time-of-day / weather settings (REQ-040 / city mood) wait for the settings pane slice. The renderer renders a single static frame on mount (no `requestAnimationFrame` loop) because v1 has nothing animating; the loop ships with the car. Resize handling is debounced via `requestAnimationFrame` so the renderer / camera stay in sync as the page reflows during the editor / drive transition without thrashing. The slug label and Edit CTA are DOM overlays positioned with `position: absolute` over the canvas, not three.js text meshes, because DOM is screen-reader friendly and does not require a font texture in v1. The empty-state prompt is also a DOM overlay declaring `role="status"` / `aria-live="polite"` so screen readers announce the "Place a road first" copy; the underlying three.js scene still renders the ground plane and lighting rig so the empty city does not appear broken. The Edit CTA uses `next/link` with `prefetch` enabled so Q-002 default C (smooth client-side transition between drive and edit) gets the prefetch wiring without an extra slice; the smooth transition itself (REQ-055) lands later. Per slice discipline, REQ-031 (physics) and the rest of the drive runtime stay deferred; this slice ships only the scene scaffold so a builder can see their city.

## 2026-05-04, Editor Arc45 and Diagonal Palette Entries: REQ-061, REQ-062 (F-007)

- Branch: `feature/req-061-062-corner-palette`
- PR: #N (when known)
- Changed: extended the editor street palette with the two corner-connector pieces from REQ-061 and REQ-062 per F-007. `src/app/[slug]/edit/editorState.ts` `STREET_PALETTE` now lists `straight`, `left90`, `right90`, `scurve`, `scurveLeft`, `sweepRight`, `sweepLeft`, `intersection`, `arc45`, `diagonal` in that order; the new entries sit after the REQ-019 intersection so the corner-connector block stays grouped at the trailing edge of the palette and `Straight` remains the first entry (default selection unchanged). Both pieces are single-cell with the implicit `(row, col)` footprint, so the existing `placePiece` / `erasePiece` reducer paths cover them without 8-direction connector or sampled-centerline plumbing; the existing rotate tool (REQ-021) cycles their rotation through the standard 0 / 90 / 180 / 270 cycle. `EditorClient` and the click-to-place handler need no code changes; the palette renders the new buttons by iterating `STREET_PALETTE` and the click handler dispatches `placePiece(city, type, ...)` exactly as before. `tests/app/editorState.test.ts` updated the `STREET_PALETTE` enumeration test to expect all ten entries in order, added an ordering assertion that pins `arc45` after `intersection` and `diagonal` at `STREET_PALETTE.length - 1`, dropped `arc45` and `diagonal` from the deferred-piece exclusion guard (replaced with a guard that still excludes `megaSweepRight`, `megaSweepLeft`, `hairpin`), and added a `placePiece with REQ-061 arc45 and REQ-062 diagonal` block (6 cases) covering single-cell placement for each type, rotation pass-through, overlap rejection on a piece-occupied cell, round-trip with `erasePiece`, and CitySchema validity. `e2e/editor.spec.ts` gained a `/corner-palette-spec/edit` spec that intercepts autosave PUTs, asserts the Arc 45 and Diagonal palette buttons are visible with the right text, that neither is the default selection, picks Arc 45, places at the origin and asserts `data-cell-occupied-kind="piece"`, switches to Diagonal, places at `(0, 1)`, and confirms a duplicate click on an occupied cell stays a no-op. `docs/gdd/07-editor.md` Build log gained a REQ-061 / REQ-062 entry; the Piece palette spec text now records the corner-connector entries as the trailing two single-cell pieces. F-007 marked resolved in `docs/FOLLOWUPS.md`. REQ-061 and REQ-062 stay `partial` because the runtime concerns (sampled centerlines per F-003, wheel contact per F-004, pace notes per F-005, difficulty scoring per F-006, 8-direction connector validation per REQ-063) wait for the drive scene scaffold (REQ-031).
